# AGENTS.md

Project guidance for coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, …) working in this repository. The Claude Code-specific entry point `CLAUDE.md` forwards here.

## Heuristic Learning Loop

This project uses Heuristic Learning: the G2P rules are the learnable policy; Claude is the learning agent. The loop runs across sessions using Claude's memory system for continuity.

### Running the loop

1. **Diagnose** — `yarn test:eval --cluster`: find top rule failure patterns. `yarn diagnose:postlex` measures each post-lexical rule's removal through the actual pipeline; add `--all --include-long` for the whole dictionary. It verifies instrumented replay, records recursive stem effects, and writes every changed prediction with win/loss scores to `/tmp/phonemize-postlex-diagnosis/results.json`. Use these as diagnosis, then narrow or repair the rule and run the normal rebuild/adoption gates.
2. **Trace** — `yarn trace <word> [word...]`: see which rule fired for specific words, pre- and post-dictionary
3. **Fix** — edit `PHONEME_RULES`/`SUFFIX_RULES` (in `src/en/syllabify.ts`), the post-lexical tables (`src/en/postlex.ts`), or `tryMorphologicalAnalysis` (in `src/en/g2p.ts`)
4. **Validate** — `yarn test` (zero regressions), `yarn test:eval` (rules-only lenient accuracy does not decrease), `yarn test:parity` (runtime strict parity ≥ baseline). Measure a candidate rule as a win/loss list over the whole dict before adopting it (rules-only dump before/after; `yarn test:parity --dump` for the runtime path)
5. **Commit** — accumulate validated changes until English-subset strict accuracy improves by at least **1 absolute percentage point** over the previous committed baseline (user commit minimum, 2026-10-03); smaller gains remain uncommitted. All gates must also pass. Refresh the English-subset, runtime parity and stress-aware baselines in that qualifying commit; retain the historical full-dict target baseline.

Check compression triggers (see Rule Compression section) before committing any fix.

### Reading cluster output

`--cluster` groups mismatch words by the rule that fired at the first IPA divergence point. Clusters dominated by foreign proper nouns (long words, unusual consonant clusters) signal a coverage gap that is out of scope for English rules — skip those and focus on clusters with recognisable common English words.

### Eval baselines

`scripts/eval-baseline.json` (rules-only, `yarn test:eval`), `scripts/eval-common-baseline.json` (rules-only, English subset, `yarn test:eval --subset common`) and `scripts/parity-baseline.json` (shipped pipeline, `yarn test:parity`) store the last committed scores; each run shows its delta automatically. Update a baseline only after a confirmed improvement is committed.

`yarn test:eval` measures the rule path alone (`disableDict: true`). `yarn test:parity` measures what users get — exceptions table + morphology + fallbacks + rules — over every dict word, and exits non-zero when strict parity drops. Both of the v2.0.x bug reports (#27 `wind`/`solutions`, #28 `Seann`) were composition failures that the rules-only score cannot see, so a change to the table miner, a lookup fallback or a morphology handler is judged by parity, not by `test:eval`.

The rules-only baseline (86.998% lenient, dated 2026-06-13) was reached with the mined stress/vowel gram tables that `build-pipeline.ts` no longer runs (they scored dictionary match but hurt real pronunciation quality). The rule path alone has scored 71–74% lenient since; the baseline is kept as the target to recover by rule improvements, so its delta is negative until then — the gate on a change is "no decrease", not "≥ baseline".

English-subset scoring (set 2026-09-27, the user's decision). Of the ~24.2k remaining rules-only lenient failures on the full dict, a measurement found ~71% are proper names/surnames/brands (foreign or English), ~2% dict noise, and only ~27% English common/learned words — the full-dict score was mostly measuring rule coverage of names the rules were never meant to reach. A partition of the same rules-only run (`disableDict: true`, testable-word filters unchanged) by frequency-list presence and by the `isForeign` regex both `evaluate-strict.ts` and the new `--subset common` share: top-5000 n=3,675, strict 65.01% / lenient 84.33%; full frequency list (all ranked words present in the dict, foreign words *not yet* excluded) n=7,123, strict 62.75% / lenient 83.15%; the FOREIGN-flagged population alone, n=8,058, strict 44.22% / lenient 67.37% — well below the non-foreign rest, confirming names are the bulk of the gap. Of the lenient failures specifically: stress-position-only 8,144, foreign 1,806, everything else 14,115; 4,647 of the 8,144 stress-position failures (57%) would pass lenient if only the stressed syllable were right (segments otherwise masked-equal) — stress placement, not segmental coverage, is most of that bucket. Together these gave a realistic rules-only ceiling of roughly 83% lenient / 66% strict on the full dict, but roughly 88.5% lenient / 74% strict restricted to the top-5000 words people actually type.

The user's decision: the long-term rules-only goal (previously ≥ 60% strict / ≥ 90% lenient on the full dict, set 2026-09-26) is now scored on the **English subset** — dict words that are both in `scripts/.common-accuracy-cache/frequency.txt` (real usage, not dictionary long-tail) and not `isForeign` — via `yarn test:eval --subset common` (also `yarn test:eval:common`). The full-dict `yarn test:eval` rows above become a **no-regression gate**: a rule change must not lower them, but the improvement target lives on the subset rows. The user's stated long-term target is **≥ 80% strict** on this subset (lenient is tracked, with the earlier ≥ 90% long-term figure carried over but no separate checkpoint). Note this is a harder bar than the raw ceiling measurement above suggests once foreign words are actually removed from the numerator as well as reported separately: `--subset common`'s own first run measured 7,011 testable non-foreign frequency-list words at 63.02% strict / 83.38% lenient (top-5000 slice: 3,629 words, 65.14% strict / 84.43% lenient) — already close to the ~66%/~74% strict ceiling estimated above, which means reaching 80% strict will need more than closing the rules-vs-names gap; it needs the irregular common words themselves fixed (the many single-word findings throughout this file — property/process/reference/yesterday/percent and the rest of the unresolved prefix-stress list are exactly that population).

The heteronym row is the one metric that sees context. Parity and top-5000 score a word in isolation, so a wrong reading of *read*, *bow* or *minute* in a sentence is invisible to them. Reference: Google's WikipediaHomographData (Gorman et al. 2018, Apache-2.0, pinned commit + content hash in `scripts/homograph-sources.json`), 162 homographs × ~100 Wikipedia sentences, three-annotator reading labels. Each output is mapped to the nearest labelled reading, so transcription conventions never decide the score. Primary stress outweighs reduced-vowel quality there, and a tie between readings counts as wrong (12 on eval after unstressed ə~u was made a half-cost pair: the source writes the noun *document* as /ˈdɑːˌkjuːmənt/, which tied the correct /ˈdɑkjəmənt/ with the verb). Diagnose on `--split train` and gate on eval, so rules are never tuned on the gated split. The first number sits 20 points **below** always picking the most frequent reading: `homographs.json` splits readings by V / !V only, and the word-level failures are noun/adjective readings taking the verb form (affiliate, moderate, fragment, conjugate, document), senses that POS cannot separate (bass, bow, tear), and proper nouns (Polish, Nestlé, Bologna). Beating the floor needs sense, not just POS. A 2026-09-26 side experiment (since removed) found that TypeSafe's Jev can't judge pronunciation at all: IPA, ARPABET, respelling and per-dimension stress/vowel/consonant questions all scored near chance. Asked to pick a sense gloss, though, it got 28/28 hand-written heteronym sentences. It is an external API, so it can serve only as a labelling source, not a runtime option.

First heteronym pass (2026-09-26, 64.33 → 80.43 eval, 63.35 → 80.31 train). Most of the gain came from the POS tagger, whose V/!V call on in-table homographs was 64%, **below always answering !V (68%)**. Rules measured below a coin flip were removed: next word looks like a noun → V (27%), next word is "to" → V (26%), and a bare verb suffix -s/-ate/-ize/-er/-en → V (25–28%). After a form of be/have the V reading was right 12% of the time, so that context now tags !V (a participle or predicate adjective follows). "to" + word is now V (infinitive, 88%) instead of a preposition context, and "not" + word is V (84%). `EnglishG2P.tagWord` also dropped an empty `prev`, so a sentence-initial word read its NEXT word as its previous one. Separately, ten upstream `homographs.en` entries had the noun reading under V or a wrong transcription (analyses, articulate, diagnoses, exploit, fragment, frequent, misuse, mouth, precipitate, predicate); they are overridden in `src-data/en/homographs-custom.txt`. Measure tagger changes on the train split only.

Second heteronym pass (2026-09-26, eval 80.43 → 84.33, train 80.31 → 83.70). Words missing from the table now come from misaki's POS-keyed US lexicon (hexgrad/misaki, Apache-2.0), via `scripts/import-misaki-homographs.ts` → `src-data/en/homographs-misaki.txt`. It is a whole-lexicon import, not the benchmark's word list, and build-dict merges it UNDER upstream and custom, so it only fills gaps. misaki's readings are not trusted alone. A split is kept only when pinned CMUdict attests one reading and the other is attested too, or differs only by a regular noun/verb alternation: a stress shift (where the de-stressed vowel may reduce) or word-final -ate /ət/~/eɪt/. That took 582 raw splits down to 263 and dropped misaki errors that otherwise shipped: houses with /s/, augmented as /ˈɔɡməntɪd/, a doubled l in illegitimate. A NOUN split that differs only by voicing is also dropped, because English voices the verb (use, house) and a voiced "noun" is another lexeme (closer, "one who closes"). That filter must also require the segments to differ. A first version compared the readings after devoicing only, which also matched pure stress shifts and silently dropped 61 of them (transform, overthrow, overflow, restart).

`{DEFAULT, NOUN}` splits are stored as N / !N, and the tagger now emits N on real noun evidence: a preceding article/possessive, a preposition, or a following be/have/modal (subject position, 79%). Without evidence a word keeps misaki's DEFAULT. Mapping them onto V/!V had given "consists" its rare noun reading /ˈkɑnsɪsts/ in running text, because the tagger's no-evidence default is !V. N is still "not V", so upstream V/!V entries behave as before. Emitting N also woke upstream's lone N entry (closer); it is neutralised in `homographs-custom.txt`. Demonstratives are not noun evidence (these consist of …), and a pre-verbal adverb (also, often, never …) is V (76%); an -ly adverb is not (55%).

`arpabetToIpa` (utils) put the stress mark after the previous vowel, which gave table entries like ɪˈmpækt and kəˈnsɪsts. It now uses the maximal licit onset (ɪmˈpækt, kənˈsɪsts), and that also changes custom.dict entries and ARPAbet passed to `addPronunciation`. The benchmark's non-target words (~250k tokens) are the check for words outside the 162: diff them old vs new and read every segmental change in context before adopting an import.

Third heteronym pass (2026-09-26, eval 84.33 → 85.39, train 83.70 → 84.92). A relative pronoun (that/which/who) + word is V (77%). Three upstream entries also had their readings on the wrong side. learned and blessed carried the adjective /-ɪd/ under V, so "he learned" took it; they are now N pairs, with the adjective only on noun evidence. dogged gets the verb under V, like crooked/jagged/ragged upstream. wound had /wund/ under V; /waʊnd/ is only ever the verb (past of wind), so it is now V. lead is now an N pair with /lid/ as the no-evidence default (lead vocals, lead to), and the metal stays after a determiner (the lead pipe). Measured on the non-target prose diff: each of these was a net win, and the misses left are "that + noun" (that impact injuries, that subject) and the metal without a determiner (chemical and lead isotopic).

Fourth heteronym pass (2026-09-26, eval 86.63 → 87.06, train 86.13 → 86.53). Two more kinds of noun evidence. A possessive before the word is N (82%: Hammond's postulate). A word ending in an attributive-adjective suffix before it is N (political intrigue, parallel postulate), limited to -ical/-ial/-ous/-ful/-less/-ible/-able/-ular/-ic/-al. -ent/-ant/-ive/-ary were measured and dropped: they end too many nouns (government, agent, detective, library), and as subjects those turned the following verb into a noun ("the government uses" → /jusəz/). The wide set scored +0.06 more on train but cost more regressions in the prose diff. Also measured and rejected: a capitalized name + an -s word as a V cue. It lifted V/!V on the table words but lowered the full pipeline, because "Bronze Age axes" is a noun. The known misses left: possessive + lead takes the metal (Herring's lead grew), and -al/-ic nouns as subjects (the canal consists of).

Open: a determiner before an adjective–noun pair is read as noun evidence, so "an invalid argument" gets the noun /ˈɪnvəlɪd/ (invalid 20% on eval). Separating ADJ from N needs the next word, and "next word is a noun" was measured at 27% as a V cue, so it has to be re-measured as an ADJ cue, not reused. Also open: senses POS cannot split (bass, bow, tear as nouns), and proper nouns. Measured 2026-09-26: 438 of the 1952 remaining train errors are the target capitalized mid-sentence, and 331 of those are five names (bologna, Polish, Ravel, Nestlé, Celtic). Adding those five by hand would only teach to the test. A capitalonym mechanism (a Titlecase word mid-sentence takes its proper-noun reading) needs a source of name readings. misaki has only 34 Titlecase entries that differ from lowercase, and most are notation noise, so the source does not exist yet. The tokenizer now cuts POS context at . ! ?, which the mechanism would need to tell sentence-initial capitals apart.

Parallel worktree agents, one rule family each, merged sequentially, produced both passes: the first measured strict +1547/−308 on the rule path, the second (unstressed reduction, back vowels, tense i/e) a further +709/−179 with +68/−1 on the top-5000 list. A rule change is only visible to parity after `yarn build-dict` re-mines the table, so parity is judged after the rebuild, never in a worktree.

The fourth pass also took primary stress: `com-` over a lax root keeps its stress, the long-prefix loop yields to the penult fallback at 4+ syllables, a final vowel before /ŋ/ is exempt from reduction (English has no /əŋ/), -ance/-ence at four slots splits on the second syllable's coda, and an undoubled `-er` stem with final stress falls through to the rules (reference, conference). It also added `FINAL_GRAM_STRESS`, a seven-entry word-final gram table consulted before `isSyllableHeavy` — which scores 10587/21827, barely over always-penult. That table is the mechanism this project removed once before for scoring dictionary match while hurting pronunciation, so it carries a third adoption test the old miners lacked: a gram needs ≥20 dict words, ≥75% modal agreement, **and ≥3 agreeing top-5000 words**. The guard held — top-5000 default accuracy rose with it. Without that third test, 114 four-letter grams qualify and they are all surname endings (`nger`, `rman`, `gton`, `wicz`, `oski`, `moto`).

The fourth pass audited `src/en/phonotactics.ts` rule by rule — that file rewrites the output of BOTH paths, and nothing in it had ever been measured. Four rules were narrowed (the -ed allomorph was building impossible /Cɹd/ codas; silent-h deletion ate the licit borrowed /hl hr hm hn/ onsets; unstressed /ɪɹ/ coalescence ate the /aɪ eɪ ɔɪ/ offglide; syllabic-l epenthesis fired inside an /ɹl/ coda and before /lj lw/), and `addInitialSecondary` moved to `applyPostStress` so it no longer marks lexical output. A structural fact came out of it worth keeping: `data/en/exceptions.json` stores raw dict IPA, so any phonotactics rule applied to a table hit can only be a no-op or a corruption — 4467 of 53,759 entries were leaving the pass different from the entry they were read from, now 2526.

The third pass (alternating secondary stress, `s`-voicing depth, unstressed rhotics) added strict +964/−102 on the rule path and found a shipped bug: `deRhoticBeforeStress` in phonotactics rewrote /ɝ/ to /ə/+/ɹ/ before a stressed vowel on BOTH paths, against a lexicon that spells that position /ɝ/ 1659:101. Removing it moved runtime parity +1.38 on its own. The lesson generalises: a phonotactics rule applies to dictionary output too, so it needs the same win/loss evidence as a rule-path rule, and none of that file's rules were measured that way.

A rules-only prediction is NOT independent of the mined table: with `disableDict: true` the morphology handlers still look their stems up in it, so a test that pins a full IPA string for a derived word can move when `build-dict` re-mines. Pin the segment the rule owns instead.

Diagnosis that fed the second pass: group the rules-only mismatches in `scripts/.common-accuracy-cache/rules-report.json` by their single-edit signature (normalise ɫ→l first, and drop the 36 closed-class function words, which are lexical by design). That ranks the remaining classes by how many common words each would fix.

The rules-only baseline is deliberately NOT lowered to today's number: the gap is the ground the rules must recover without the removed gram tables.

Rules of the goal:

- Every user-reported word gets a regression test in `__tests__/issue-<n>.test.ts` and a fix for its *class* (e.g. #28 → doubled-final-consonant name variants), never a per-word entry. If no class fix passes the gates, the word goes to `src-data/en/custom.dict` and the class is recorded here as open.
- Each loop session moves at least one metric up without moving any other down; both baselines are updated in the same commit as the improvement.
- Rules-only accuracy is a means, not the end: a rule fix that raises `test:eval` but lowers parity is rejected.
- Foreign-origin words (set 2026-09-26): many are naturalised enough that English rules cannot fully reach them. Write a rule when a pure rule works without hurting the metrics (orthographic classes such as -ino/-ano penult stress are rules, like -ford/-stein/-bury in SUFFIX_RULES). Otherwise put the word in a dictionary file (`src-data/en/custom.dict`). **Never a whole-word → pronunciation mapping table in code.**
- Strict scoring neutralises /ʌ/ vs /ə/ (2026-09-26, same code 49.11 → 52.94). The metric is "exact after stress removal", and in American references STRUT is the stressed form of schwa: CMUdict has one AH phone for both (AH1/AH0 in `cmudict.phones`), and Merriam-Webster writes both \ə\ ("\ˈə\ in stressed syllables as in humdrum, abut (IPA [ʌ])", Guide to Pronunciation). ipa-dict writes ə under stress, so /ʌ/ output was a stress mismatch scored as a vowel error. Output is unchanged, and `normalizeStrut` stays. `test:parity`'s strict still separates them; the same argument applies there, but that gate's baseline history is kept as-is until it is changed deliberately. evaluate-strict's headline got the same neutralisation (same code 50.71 → 54.61): it keeps the stress mark, so a stressed ə and ʌ are one phoneme there too. Without it, every STRUT-spelt-o rule (love, month) counted its losses but none of its wins.
- `scripts/evaluate-strict.ts`'s vowelOnly/structural bucket boundary was missing ɑ (2026-09-27, same code, headline unaffected). Two separate vowel definitions live in that file: `PHON_VOWELS` (used by `enUsPhonemic`, the headline metric) already included ɑ, but `VOWELS` (used by `primaryStressIdx`, which decides `samePrimary`) and the `predNoVow`/`expNoVow` consonant-skeleton masks did not, so any word with ɑ before or at the stressed syllable could get the wrong `primaryStressIdx`, or get its ɑ left un-masked and compared literally against the mask's `·` placeholder at the expected word's vowel position. Both were missing the same symbol and both feed the same bucket decision, so they're fixed together. The headline (`phonemicExact`) is untouched by construction — `enUsPhonemic` never used the buggy set — confirmed by running before/after on identical code: 50684/90369 (56.09%) both times, exact/stressOnly also unchanged (42063/4727). What moved is which bucket a non-exact, non-stress-only word lands in: vowelOnly 12242 → 15877 (+3635), structural 31337 → 27702 (−3635) — words that differ from the dict only in vowel quality, with the primary stress in the same place, were being misclassified as structural mismatches whenever an ɑ sat at or before the stress. `scripts/eval-strict-baseline.json` updated to the corrected bucket counts.
- Solve with rules, not data (set 2026-09-26). The misaki homograph import (~300 entries, `src-data/en/homographs-misaki.txt`) is the accepted order of magnitude for a vouched data supplement. Do not add datasets, trained models or name lexicons to raise a score. That rules out WikiPron capitalonyms (CC BY-SA as well) and a trained POS tagger. Where a rule can replace imported entries, prefer the rule. Measured once, rejected: an -ate rule (noun evidence + 3+ syllables + dict /-ˌeɪt/ → /-ət/) cannot replace the 39 -ate data entries. Which reading an -ate word takes WITHOUT context is lexical (laminate, conjugate, postulate, affiliate default to the noun; isolate, animate, associate to the verb), so the rule recovered 0.23 of the 1.73 points the entries carry, and it adds nothing with them present.

Open classes: heteronym ADJ-vs-N after a determiner (invalid) and sense-only heteronyms (bass, bow) — see the heteronym passes above.

Compression (2026-09-10, first pass): it ran snapshot-gated (empty diff over 1.3M predictions) and took the three modules from 2930 to 2685 lines. Still 85 over the ceiling; what remains is comment carrying the dict ratio behind each rule, which the procedure says to keep. The opt-in `predictPrincipled` path is the one block that may be vestigial — retiring it is a behaviour decision, not a compression.

Compression (2026-09-27, second pass, snapshot-identical over 1,297,085 predictions): the SUFFIX_RULES stress flag and its assignStress loop were dead (its one true entry matched only the whole words ic/ics, which are one syllable and return earlier), so SUFFIX_RULES is now [pattern, ipa]; ^ou + ^ow, ^ai + ^ay, ^ei + ^ey and the two ʃən suffix entries were merged; the -able/-al doubled-stem lookup, the sounded-final-vowel test and two suffix-join compositions became shared helpers. Code lines 2495 → 2488.

Compression (2026-09-28, third pass, snapshot-identical over 1,296,494 predictions and trace-identical over all dict words): g2p.ts — matchPos/sAllomorph/edPast/wellKnown simplified (two dead disjuncts removed from sAllomorph), a shared ruleSyllables helper for trace() and renderRuleForm(); syllabify.ts assignStress — redundant length guards and Math.max(0, len-2) removed after the length ≤ 1 early return, endsWith chains merged into regexes, -ate and -ator branches merged (identical bodies), orphan `;` removed, isLikelyCompound and coalesceOnset simplified. Code lines 2645 → 2589; the interleaved assignStress return-penult branches were left alone because non-penult branches sit between them.

Compression (2026-09-28, fourth pass, snapshot-identical over 1,296,494 predictions and trace-identical over the 100,818-word disableDict trace dump of every alphabetic dict word — verified twice: once in the source worktree, once again from scratch after a session interruption, by re-applying the worktree's `src/` diff onto a clean checkout of the eviction commit, rebuilding `data/`, and re-diffing both dumps against freshly-taken before-dumps). `g2p.ts`'s -s-plural handler folds its rule-derived-stem branch and its -ate/-ial rule-exact-stem branch into one shared `if` (both just rule-predict a stem and require it to stay consonant-final; a comment records the shared reasoning instead of restating it twice). `postlex.ts` — six `POST_LEX_RULES` entries OR-merged into an existing sibling that already shared their exact `(re, sub)` pair (`mechan` + `anchor` → tʃ→k; the Germanic ei→aɪ family's four entries folded into two; the compound-internal thought/think/thank θ rule folded into the thet/theis/thesis/... entry; the g→ɡ Germanic-surname family; `achet` + `alet`; `cchi` + `chet(ti|ta|to|te)` + psych-/tech- ch→k) — a global-replace `re` makes OR-ing the `when` predicates exactly equivalent to keeping the rules separate. The -ger/-gers/-gen postlex family became one entry via a capture group (`dʒ(ɝz?|ən)$` → `ɡ$1`), reproducing each original branch's own substitution (ɡɝ / ɡɝz / ɡən) exactly, guards intact. `syllabify.ts` — ten `PHONEME_RULES` alternation merges (`^e[ae]` + `^e(?=o(?!r))`; `^bt$` + `^ght`; `^ow` + `^eau[x]?` + `^oa`; `^a[iy]` + `^e[iy]`; `^ear(?=[nlcr]|th)` + `^eur`; `^tsch` + `^cz` + `^t?ch`; `^s(?:ch|z)` + `^sh`; `^oo` + `^ue` + `^ui`; `^iew` + `^eu`; `^ould$` + `^ood`), four dead `SUFFIX_RULES` entries deleted (`^ers$`, `^age$`, `^ist$`, `^ics$` — each unreachable: the whole-word forms they covered already resolve through an earlier, more specific rule or the suffix's own singular entry), the -le tensing rule's i and a/o branches folded into one shared guard (`isStressed && isNextLastSyllable && nextSyllable === "le" && !doubledCoda`) with two inner tests instead of duplicating the guard twice, two `skip.add` sites consolidated (the four conditions that block the tense `^o$` default; the th-voicing family including `THETA_CLUSTER_CODA`, which used to run its own separate check after the loop instead of joining the first), and two bare-vowel tests (`aFire`'s "a" branches, `eFire`'s "e" branches) factored out of their disjuncts by the distributive law (`(p&&a)||(p&&b) === p&&(a||b)`). One placement is load-bearing, flagged in a code comment: the `^ow`/`^eau[x]?`/`^oa` merge must sit ahead of the merged `^e[ae]` rule, because `[ae]` also matches "eau"'s leading "ea" — a first attempt placed the merge after `^e[ae]` and silently broke beau (oʊ → i, caught only by the snapshot diff, not by any existing test). Left unmerged: entries a `skip.add` call or another literal string check elsewhere in the file depends on by their exact source text (`^al$`, `^g(?=[eiy])`, `^y$`, `^o$`, `^ous$`, `^th(?=[aeiou])`, `^the$`, `^ar`, `^[eiu]r`, plus `^rh`'s compound r|h boundary guard and `^scien$`'s SUFFIX_RULES-shadow comment), and the single-letter vowel fallbacks, whose bare pattern is their own identity. Code lines 2655 → 2600 (g2p.ts 824, syllabify.ts 1294, postlex.ts 482; block-comment-aware count per the counting note above), landing exactly on the 2600 ceiling.

Compression (2026-09-28, fifth pass, snapshot-identical over 1,296,352 predictions and trace-identical over the 100,806-word disableDict trace dump — `{path, syllables, steps}`, not just the final IPA — of every alphabetic dict word). `assignStress`'s same-return branches were OR-merged in order-preserving groups, never reordered across a branch with a different return value: the `tion/sion/cial/tial` and `[^aeiouy]ity$` checks (both length-2); `[^aeiouy]ial$` and `[ae]ntal$` (both length-2, both gated `>= 3`); `LATIN_HIATUS_ENDING` and the word-final `que` check (both length-2); the a/o/i-ending penult rule, the Greek -graphy/-nomy/-… family and the -ium/-osis/-itis/-ysis/-iasis family (all three length-2, sharing no state, now one `if`); the `man/son/ton`+`ville` group (`>= 3`) and the `ary`+`crat` group (`=== 3`) (both return 0, grouped by their own length guard rather than flattened into one, since the guards differ); inside the 2-syllable branch, the assimilated-`ad-` prefix test now also covers the bare `firstSyl === "a"` case it sat next to; and the four 2-syllable return-1 tests (`RHOTIC_VOWEL_RE` + ad/ac/in/mis/out, `FRENCH_FINAL_ENDING`, `oon`, `een`) merged into one OR ahead of the branch's `return 0` default. In the `>= 3` branch, `isLikelyCompound` and the word-final `-ture/-ure` root-stress check merged into one `if` returning 0 (`syllables[3] === "ture" || syllables[3] === "ure"` collapsed to `/^t?ure$/`). -ance/-ence's nested `if`s became one ternary (`syllables.length === 3 || (=== 4 && open second slot) ? 0 : 1`), same values, no branch reordering. The `-ion` rule's `!/(?:tion|sion)$/` exclusion was deleted as dead code, not merged: this function's very first statement already returns unconditionally for every `tion`/`sion` word, so no such word can ever reach the `-ion` check to need excluding — a comment records the logical argument, and the pass's own byte-identical snapshot/trace diff is the empirical confirmation, though this one clause wasn't isolated separately since the proof doesn't need it. `g2p.ts`: `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE`'s two negated `gn$`/`(?:ook|ood)$` tests merged into one alternation; the -s-plural handler's rule-derived-stem branch and silent-e-stem branch were restructured from a nested ternary (two separate call sites for the same `this.predictInternal(stem, undefined, true)`) into one OR'd condition feeding a single ternary, so the call appears once instead of twice; the -al suffix loop's two `continue` guards (`b.endsWith("i")`, `/[ae]nt$/.test(b)`) merged into one, both still requiring `>= 2` vowel groups; `tryCompoundSplit`'s two exclusion guards (the `log/graph/nom/soph/mat` + `ic$` regex, and the bare `b === "pose"` check) merged into one `if (A || B) continue`. `postlex.ts` and `syllableToIPA` were left alone — already compact, per the third pass's own "What not to compress" note. Code lines 2623 → 2599 (g2p.ts 830, syllabify.ts 1287, postlex.ts 482 unchanged; block-comment-aware count).

Compression (2026-09-28, sixth pass, snapshot-identical over 1,296,352 predictions and trace-identical over the 100,806-word disableDict trace dump of every alphabetic dict word). `g2p.ts` — the -ed/-ing silent-e gate and the -s-plural silent-e gate each independently re-derived "is this rule-predicted stem usable as a consonant-final base" (the vowel check plus the coda-/ɝ/ exemption with its 4-syllable cutoff for configure/measure/gesture-class stems); factored into one shared `isUsableConsonantStem(ipa, base)` helper called from both sites, comment moved onto the helper instead of repeated at each call site. The -ply prefix-stress guard iterated `KEEP_UNMERGED_AFTER_PREFIX` with `Array.from(...).some(...)` on every -ly word; replaced with a direct `Set.has()` lookup against the stripped `-ply` prefix, same semantics. The dying/lying/tying/vying -ie restoration branch carried a `base.length === 2` guard alongside its `/^[bcdfghjklmnpqrstvwxz]y$/` regex; the regex alone already anchors the base to exactly one consonant + "y" (length 2), so the length check was dead and was removed. `syllabify.ts` — `hiatusATense` (-ium/-io/-ius hiatus tensing) and `encyATense` (-ency/-ancy tensing) shared the identical `isStressed && !!nextSyllable` guard and fed the same `aFire` disjunct with no other interaction, so they were merged into one `aTailTense` alternation; the comments explaining each branch's own measured win/loss evidence were kept, concatenated rather than restated. `aOrLax` (the l/m/n-before-`or` laxing exclusion) was a same-purpose sibling of the adjacent r-onset exclusion already inline in `aFire`'s condition — both block the same `twoSylTense` branch — so it was folded into that condition as `!/^r|[lmn]or$/.test(nextSyllable!)` instead of a separately named negated variable. The `^cre$`/`^tre$`/`^bre$` `skip.add` triplet was measured for a regex-alternation merge like the fourth pass's other `skip.add` consolidations, and rejected: those three lines call `skip.add` on three separate literal keys into a lookup set, not a single regex tested once, so alternation would need a loop or three calls regardless — there is no `PHONEME_RULES`/`SUFFIX_RULES` table entry backing them where a `$1`-substituted alternation could apply, unlike the true regex merges elsewhere in this file. Left as three calls, only reformatted onto one line. Code lines 2641 → 2635 (g2p.ts 840, syllabify.ts 1313, postlex.ts 482 unchanged; block-comment-aware count).

Found, not fixed (2026-09-10): `syllabify` splits `e|xist`, so two-syllable ex- words get initial stress; 3+-syllable penult stress is a coin flip on syllable heaviness, and no feature tried (heaviness, onset cluster, coda, openness) got a two-syllable `a-` prefix above 64%, so it needs suffix class or POS; no post-primary secondary-stress rule exists; `sch`+vowel → /sk/ and `og$` → /ɔɡ/ both lose on the name-heavy dict (school/scheme/blog are lexical); `-iver` has no orthographic discriminator between driver and river, so the v-exclusion in `iFire` stays; open `wa` (quality, water) has no majority in the dict.

Next, measured and waiting (2026-09-10): `assignStress`'s prefix rules put the primary on the wrong syllable for property/process/proper/expert/reference/conference/advertise (they return index 1, the dict wants 0) and for yesterday/percent/internet — no vowel rule can reach those. The same is true of most of the remaining `a` errors: of 1475 æ→ə positions, 788 sit where the rules stress syllable 0 and the dict reduces it (abet, abort, abash), and 1519 of the 1949 ə→æ positions are dict-stressed. Of that, the `oi` gap (avoid) and the magic-e rime (alone, amaze) are now fixed — the magic-e case never reached the two-syllable branch at all, because the syllabifier gives the silent e its own syllable, so the rule had to move to the 3+ branch. The r-controlled half is CLOSED as disproved: a closed r-rime after `a-` is 11 final : 11 initial and a word-final `-Vr` is 8 : 23, so there is no rule there.

A stressed open `a` behaves like `o` and `y` under trisyllabic laxing, not the opposite: at three syllables it is tense only 11% of the time against 31% at two, and the frames that win at two invert at three. radio, agency, canadian, favorite, native and capable are lexical minorities that no general rule reaches.

Do NOT extend the last-syllable prefix guard to an `n` coda for format/impact/content: measured on the genuinely-unstressed subset, final `a` with an `n` coda is 1809 ə : 190 æ and final `e` with `nt` is 324 ə : 40 ɛ. Those three are stress errors, not guard omissions.

Line-count trigger (2026-09-10): it now counts code lines, not total lines. Measured at 2789 total the split was 2002 code / 706 comment / 165 blank, i.e. the ceiling was being tripped by the measured-ratio comment this loop requires on every rule, not by code growth. Counting code keeps the trigger honest in both directions: it still fires on real accumulation, and it stops rewarding the deletion of the evidence behind a rule.

A sixth, smaller pass (2026-09-22) closed a real dead-code bug: `syllabify()` always attaches word-initial `x` to the FIRST syllable (`exist` → `["e","xist"]`, never `["ex","ist"]`), so the `"ex"` entry already sitting in `PREFIXES_2SYL` could only ever fire for ex+consonant words (export, expect — 25:17 final), never ex+vowel, where the prefix is hidden inside `syllables[0]`. `assignStress` now aliases `firstSyl` to `"ex"` when it is `"e"` immediately followed by `x`, without touching `syllabify()` itself (worked around it, the same way the pre-existing `a-` prefix special-case does). True 2-syllable ex+vowel words: 11:5 final in the dict — matching a stale, never-actually-verified comment that had been sitting next to the dead entry. Same pass fixed two related bugs found while chasing it: /ɡz/ (not a licit onset) was landing the stress mark on the wrong side of the cluster in 112/112 dict entries containing it, and `exh-` before a reduced vowel (exhibit, exhaust) wasn't voicing to /ɡz/ the way `ex` + a full vowel already did. `exit` is the one measured regression — no orthographic signal separates it from exist/exert, and it is the minority side of the split.

Rules-only pass (2026-09-26). An unstressed vowel before a word-final silent-e -ce, and its -ces/-ced/-cing inflections, now reduces to /ə/ (palace, notice, practice, service, furnace): 26 strict wins : 6 losses, and 4 top-5000 wins. After a two-syllable compounding prefix (inter/over/under/counter/super) the -Vce is a free root and keeps its vowel (interface, interlace); `syllableToIPA` gained a `head` argument for that test. -se was measured and left out, because -ise/-ose/-use are the stressed or secondary suffixes (advertise, franchise, diffuse). Two knock-ons had to be fixed before the gates held, and both are worth remembering. First, once a base form becomes rule-exact it leaves the exception table, so its inflections (services, notices) fall through to the rules, and the rule has to cover them too. Second, trisyllabic laxing counted the inflection -es/-ed/-ing toward depth, so notices laxed to /ˈnɑtəsəz/; the depth is now counted on the base. Rejected: reducing unstressed final -ain (captain, mountain, certain) scored 14 : 22 strict. The losses are domain/maintain/abstain, whose stress the rules already put on the wrong syllable, and reduction turns that stress error into a two-edit miss.

The -ation morphology handler built abdication from abdicate + /ʃən/ and kept the stem's primary (ˈæbdəˌkeɪʃən). -ation, like -ization, takes the primary on its own /eɪ/ and demotes the stem's, so it now does (ˌæbdəˈkeɪʃən, the onset from utils' licit-onset set). 316 dict words have penult stress and were read otherwise; evaluate-strict 49.05 → 49.24. Note that `test:eval`, `rule-diff` and parity all compare stress-stripped, so a pure stress fix scores 0 : 0 there: judge stress changes on `evaluate-strict` (and the homograph benchmark), not on the segment metrics.

-ity and -ial take the primary on the syllable right before them (activity, abnormality, accessibility; editorial, adversarial). The syllabifier keeps consonant + ity/ial as one final slot, so `assignStress` returns length - 2. -ial needs 3+ slots, because at two the <i> is itself stressed (denial, trial). -iety is a different frame. -ity: 59 strict wins : 11, lenient +84. The losses are an open pretonic first syllable reduced too far (docility, hostility, humidity keep a full vowel in the dict), which is its own frame, not a stress error. -ial words also had to skip the generic -al morphology (adversari + -al kept the stem's stress), except over a one-syllable stem (trial): 20 : 0. Pattern worth reusing: when a suffix shifts stress, check whether a morphology handler builds the word first and keeps the stem's stress. -ation had the same bug.

The same slot rule covers -ental/-antal (accidental, environmental) and the Greek -graphy/-nomy/-sophy/-scopy/-pathy/-gamy/-cracy endings (photography, philosophy, democracy): 12 : 1 and 12 : 1 strict. -ential needed its -cial/-tial morphology handler to skip a 3+-syllable -en/-an stem (confiden+tial kept the stem's stress). The 2-syllable stems essential/potential still come out initial-stressed from that handler, but routing them to the rules mangles essential, so they stay open. Found, not fixed: fundamental goes through compound decomposition as fund+amen+tal, and geo-/bio- are one syllable slot, so -graphy stresses the wrong vowel inside it (geography, biography).

-ate puts the primary two syllables before its own /eɪt/ (abdicate, accelerate, anticipate, and the adjectives accurate, delicate). The syllabifier writes it as C+a · te, so that is slot length - 4, or length - 3 when the C+a slot is a hiatus holding two syllables (appre·cia·te, gra·dua·te); fewer than four slots is left alone (debate, rotate). It scored 131 strict wins : 12 and fixed demonstrate/generate/indicate/operate/separate. The losses are prefix + two-syllable -ate (relocate, dislocate, underrate, overestimate), which want the base's stress.

It also exposed a shipped rule-path bug. The primary mark's position was computed on the syllable strings BEFORE applyPostLexical and then inserted into the string AFTER it. Any post-lexical edit ahead of the mark (degeminating ac·com, bə|ɹeɪ → bɝeɪ) shifted it into the stressed vowel (əkɑmədeˈɪʃən). The mark is now anchored on the untouched tail of the word. An onset consonant the pass absorbed into a vowel is dropped from the anchor, but one it merely rewrote is not (chord, gehrig). An ɪɹ/əɹ pair stays ahead of the mark so it can still coalesce to ɝ, which is the convention applyPostStress's onset pass keeps. Stress-position exact matches: 219 wins : 0. Rules-only strict segments: 340 : 0, mostly Mc- names whose later passes key on the mark. Known cost: collaboration now comes from the whole-word path (collaborate is rule-exact and left the table), which has no secondary on co·LLA, because secondaryStressIndices skips two open slots in a row. Its rhotic test now pins only the /ɝˈeɪ/ segment.

The word-initial closed-syllable frame that keeps a full vowel before the stress (initialClosedA/E) now covers <o> too (bom·bastic, cog·nition, dog·matic), minus the com-/con-/cor-/col-/ob- prefixes that reduce (community, objective): 87 strict wins : 14. Measured and rejected: dropping secondaryStressIndices' two-open-slots exclusion gives collaboration/examination their co·LLA beat but costs -ification/-ization and the Greek -o- linker, 41 : 59 lenient.

First foreign-name rule class (2026-09-26): Italian endings -ino/-ano/-ini/-oni/-elli/-etti/-ello/-etto/-ucci/-acci. Stress goes on the penult at 3+ slots (`ITALIAN_ENDING` in syllabify). That stressed vowel takes its Italian value (a /ɑ/, i /i/, e /ɛ/), and so do two-syllable -ino/-ano names, but not an English two-syllable -ini (mini). The other unstressed e/o/u stay full (lo·ZA·no /loʊ/) after the English reduction has run; <a> and <i> are anglicised in the dict and do reduce. Stress without the vowel quality was net negative on lenient (84 : 169), because the English rules give a stressed a /æ/. Stress plus stressed vowel scored 164 : 43 strict; the full-vowel pass added 178 : 36 (e/o/u; adding i or a won more strict but lost more). lozano/molano's vowel test expected initial stress, against ipa-dict and CMUdict (both penult); with the user's approval it now expects ɫoʊˈzɑnoʊ / moʊˈɫɑnoʊ. Open in this class: the -iano hiatus (juliano, gagliano) puts the mark on the i of the "ia" slot.

-ia stresses the syllable before it (india, malaria, cafeteria). An open a/e/o there is tense (al·BA·nia /eɪ/, ME·dia /i/, mon·GO·lia /oʊ/), as Latin lengthening before a hiatus predicts: 86 strict : 19, lenient +83. <u> is left out because furia/luria keep /ʊ/, and a/o alone scored lower. -ator stresses like its -ate verb (generator, administrator) and keeps /eɪ/ on the <a> even under the secondary that alternating stress gives it. Stress alone turned that <a> into /æ/ (0 : 5); with the vowel it is 35 : 0.

Germanic compound surname elements -berger/-inger/-erman(n)/-heimer/-meyer/-meier/-hofer/-felder keep initial stress at 3+ slots (aldinger, bamberger, oppenheimer), as English words sharing them do: 73 strict : 5, headline +0.31. -ington was measured in the set and taken out on the user's call. It is an English place-name element, not a Germanic one, and American usage varies there (ellington heard as ɛˈɫɪŋtən, although ipa-dict and CMUdict both write ˈɛɫɪŋtən). The remaining losses are first-element laxing (fry·berger, cry·derman get /ɪ/).

A word of 3+ slots ending consonant + a/o/i (banana, dakota, montana, colorado, anatoli) takes penult stress, and its stressed penult reads continental: open e/o/u → /ɛ oʊ u/, closed e/u → /ɛ u/, a and i left to the English rules (an open a → /ɑ/ variant gained lenient but lost top-5000). The grams that do not have a penult majority are excluded: -ica 18% (africa, america, jessica), -oro 48%, -aco 50%, -ula 56% (formula), -omo 65%, -ico 65% (mexico), -ema 67% (cinema), -iga 67%. Rules-only strict 432 : 135, lenient 905 : 319, top-5000 5 : 3 (alabama, alaska, colorado, dakota, montana vs barbara, buffalo, canada), headline 54.91 → 54.95. Accepted rules-path losses are lexical minorities of majority grams: agatha (-tha 76%, its test now pins only the voiceless th) and etcetera (-era 88%). etcetera surfaced at runtime because `etc` expands to it before lookup (the `etc` table entry is never reached) and its ipa-dict entry carries two primaries, which the miner drops by policy. It is now in custom.dict with one primary. About 1400 dict entries are dropped that way, so any stress rule can expose one at runtime: diff the runtime top-5000 report, since the rules-only common count cannot see it.

The agent noun of a Greek -y noun (-log/-graph/-nom/-soph + -ist/-er) is now read from the -y form in morphology, swapping its final /i/ (biology → biologist, photography → photographer, economy → economist): 29 strict : 1. The loss is misnomer, whose "misnomy" is not a word. German -auer is /aʊɝ/ (bauer, neubauer): 43 : 0, one PHONEME_RULES entry.

-ola joined the Italian endings (dicola, spinola, buccola): 28 strict : 8, lenient +58. The losses are Greek/older loans with antepenult stress (gondola, parabola, pergola). At two syllables the Italian vowel is withheld from -ini and -ola (mini, nola) but kept for the rest (gino, gucci). -ara was measured and dropped: barbara, ankara and clara are English/anglicised (0 : 1 on the top-5000). -era is not tried, because camera and opera are antepenult.

The -ia rule now covers the whole Latin hiatus family through one `LATIN_HIATUS_ENDING` shared by assignStress and the tensing rule: -ia/-ian (with plural -s), -ious and -eous (canadian, various, spontaneous). The old -ia pattern matched only a bare final a, so these fell to the heaviness fallback. When the suffix syllable has an r onset, the lengthened vowel is the r-coloured SQUARE/NEAR one (bar·BAR·ian /ɛ/, al·GER·ian /ɪ/). Result: 119 strict : 19, top-5000 8 : 0. The losses are e+r names that the dict writes with tense i (valeria, tiberia against siberia), plus -cious/-tious words (judicious) whose newly unstressed first vowel reduces further than the dict does.

Word-final -ine took the magic-e /aɪ/ whenever the next slot was bare "ne", stressed or not. When the rules leave it unstressed and the gram before -ine is a measured not-/aɪn/ one, the syllable now falls through to /ɪ/. The grams: `FRENCH_INE_GRAM`, cine/chine/sine/zine/rmine/rtine/stine, and a vowel + rine/tine. The measured not-aɪn : aɪn ratios are in the comment; -line/-mine/-dine/-ntine/-vine/-pine are majority /aɪn/ or split and are left alone. The result is 4 strict : 2 and lenient 25 : 1. The first version used a regex lookbehind, which Hermes (React Native) may not support; it was rewritten as an equivalent plain pattern, since the regex is only used with .test().

-ive landed stress one syllable late. The syllabifier gives the silent e its own slot (ac·ti·ve), and assignStress' gram/heaviness fallback counted that slot, the problem secondaryStressIndices already corrects with SILENT_E_SLOT. The fallback now merges a final consonant+i · ve pair before counting (not -cei-ve, whose digraph already stresses right). -sive/-cive/-xive keep the penult even when it is light (elusive, pervasive). A compound on the free word "motive" keeps its own stress (automotive). Result: 21 strict : 1, top-5000 1 : 0 (negative). Left open: first-syllable -ative words (cumulative, speculative) lose the 41 : 18 majority to antepenult, and syllabification pulls the s of -gest- into the onset (digestive). (Re-measured 2026-09-27 over the -ative/-itive population by syllable count specifically — a narrower scope than this "41 : 18" count, which can't be reproduced from that measurement alone. At 5 syllables the dict is 19 initial : 41 not, confirming this row's direction. At 4 syllables the dict is the other way, 24 initial : 5 not — but the rules already predict that majority correctly without a new rule, so forcing initial stress there only helps the residual the rules currently miss, and that trade is 1 win : 4 losses. Both counts agree there's no case for a rule here, one because antepenult wins the population and one because the rules already have the population that matters.)

The syllabifier keeps a two-vowel hiatus in one slot (geo·graphy, bio·graphy, prio·rity), so a slot-level stress put the mark on the first vowel. When the stressed slot is a genuine hiatus (`isHiatusSlot`), the mark now moves to its second nucleus (`hiatusMarkOffset`, on the post-lexical string). Excluded: vowel digraphs and doubled letters; eo before r; ae/oe, which a later pass elides; g/qu + vowel (gu·ar·dian); and a combining form with an onset before /oʊ/, which keeps its own stress (biome). Stress-position exact 65 : 0 and strict segments 44 : 0. The one lenient loss is inferiority: the corrected mark lets unstressed ɪɹ coalesce to ɝ before it, where the dict keeps ɪɹ. Rejected: making a hiatus penult "heavy" so assignStress picks it (curiosity, juliano), −10 net on the headline.

Indefinite pronouns and adverbs are read as determiner (any/every/some/no) + free noun (one/body/thing/where/how/way/time/place), each part from the lexicon or rules, with a secondary on the second. That is a closed grammatical class, not a word list: 8 strict : 1, top-5000 7 : 0 (anyone, anything, someone, something, somewhere, nobody, anywhere). Measured and rejected: unstressed -ard → /ɝd/ (22 : 20 lenient; -art and -hard keep /ɑɹ/ in German names).

STRUT spelt ou: before -ble/-ple (26 : 1 in the dict), -ntr (10 : 1) and -ng not -nge (19 : 0; lounge is /aʊ/). This is a PHONEME_RULES entry placed ahead of ^oup/^ou, plus a slot-level check for dou·ble/cou·ple, where -ble is the next slot. ear before th is /ɝ/ (earth, dearth: 19 : 2). Together: 17 strict : 1, top-5000 6 : 0.

STRUT spelt o: after l/b before a final -ve slot (lo·ve, a·bo·ve: 18 : 5), before -vern (go·vern: 26 : 3) and before -nth (month: 9 : 1): 9 strict : 3 (bove, clove, sloves). cov- (40 : 28) and s/m + ome/one (87 : 50) are too split for a rule.

The -ation handler now falls back to the rules for the -ate verb when it has two syllables (≤ 3 slots): locate is rule-exact, so it left the exception table, and location fell through to the whole-word path and reduced its /oʊ/. Result: 16 strict : 0, top-5000 2 : 0 (location, duration). Allowing the fallback for every -ate verb was net negative earlier (generation, operation), so it stays limited.

The -er handler's lookups had no defence against lexicon collisions. A doubled base + e hit an unrelated pronounced-e loan (bette, latte, butte, passé → better, latter, butter, passer), and a short base + e hit a headword whose e is sounded (ente → enter, mete → meter). The magic-e lookup now skips a doubled base, as `inflect()` does for -ed/-ing, and rejects a vowel-final hit unless the base ends in y/w/r (flyer, hower, acquirer). With no magic-e stem it tries the singled base (better ← bet, controller ← control) and then a bare base of 5+ letters (grosser ← gross), but never one ending in th, since -ther is one morpheme (brother, not broth + -er). Result: 192 strict : 50, lenient 104 : 20, top-5000 6 : 1. The common loss is recorder, whose base record defaults to its noun reading; forcing the verb reading was net negative (converter, presenter). The other losses are mostly German/French surnames whose bare base collides with a short word.

-al and -able now read a doubled consonant as orthographic (refer → referral, control → controllable, commit → committal), as `inflect()` does for -ed/-ing. -al also recovers a silent-e stem from the table (approve → approval, arrive → arrival, survive → survival). That is a table lookup only: rule-predicting base + e always yields something (classic + e, addition + e), and that measured 104 : 230. A vowel-final hit is a loan whose e is sounded (principe, cantone), so it is rejected. Result: 22 strict : 3, lenient 17 : 3, top-5000 4 : 0.

Name-forming -man/-son/-ton as the last slot of a 3+-slot word keeps the root's initial stress (addleman, abelson, appleton: 94%, 94% and 91% initial in the dict, still a majority where the name starts with a prefix-like ab-/ad-/con-/de-). -ington is left out on the same grounds as in GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING. A stressed open o before an s+stop/sh/ch/th/x onset stays lax before a final -Cn slot (boston, coxon). Result: 63 strict : 21, lenient 81 : 26, top-5000 1 : 0 (boston). The losses are prefix + word compounds (inhuman, dehumanize, northampton) and -elton names with medial stress. Runtime strict parity fell by one word, subhuman. It became a table hit and `normalizeStrut` writes its secondary ə as ʌ, which is the ʌ/ə convention gap described below, not a segment error.

The -ive silent-e fold in assignStress' 3+-slot fallback now covers any trailing consonant + e slot at 4+ slots (favorite, heritage, medicine, episode, average, absolute), because the fallback counted the silent e as a syllable. Two exclusions: a hiatus antepenult (affiliate, associate: that slot already holds two syllables) and a digraph nucleus (disbelieve, misconceive, which self-stress through isSyllableHeavy). Result: 128 strict : 35, lenient 230 : 73, top-5000 1 : 0 (absolute), headline +0.18. Most losses are un-/mis- + -ed/-ing words: the -ed/-ing handler probes a fabricated base + e (uncollecte) through the rule path, and the fold moves that non-word's stress too. Measured and rejected: a light-open-penult cut on the re-/de-/pre-/con- prefix loop (resident, president, register) is 37% initial, so there is no rule there. Three compounds whose ipa-dict entries carry two primaries (videotape, videotaped, travelodge) fell to the rule path and lost parity; they are in custom.dict with one primary, as etcetera is.

A vowel + re rime (adore, aspire, allure, aware) now counts as a tense root after a- and after an undoubled ad-/ac-/in-/mis-/out- prefix (admire, inspire, misfire), so the root keeps the stress. ab- (0 : 1) and con- (1 : 2, conjure) were measured and left out. A doubled consonant + one at three slots is an Italian surname with penult stress and a sounded final e (cannone, bottone: 22 : 4). After a single consonant, -one has no discriminator (capone, malone vs ketone, ozone, hormone). Result: 14 strict : 2, lenient 23 : 1, top-5000 1 : 0 (aware). Also measured: -ette already gets its stress right (its failures are segmental), and -ene is 44 final : 63 initial. Found, not fixed: compound-parts treats in + quire as a compound, so inquire never reaches the rules.

The y-stem (-ied/-ies/-iness/-iest) and -ates handlers looked their stem up in the table only, so once a base became rule-exact and left the table (dictionary, communicate), every inflection fell to whole-word resyllabification (dictionaries → dɪkˈtɪoʊnɝiz). They now read a rule-derived stem too: 126 strict : 4, top-5000 5 : 0 (bodies, copies, injuries, married, strategies). A vowelless remainder is no stem (priest is not pr + -iest), and -ier stays lexicon-only (32 : 60 against French/German surnames). Measured and rejected: dropping ng from inflect()'s closed-stem list fixes -nge verbs (changed, ranged: 24 : 3 on the dict) but turns bringing/singing/longing into /ndʒɪŋ/, which no gate sees because those forms are not dict keys. Open: -is/-us are vowel-quality and stress work in syllabify, not morphology (-haus names, -ysis).

Polish surname spellings joined -owski as rules: -wicz/-wich and -czak stress three slots from the end (markiewicz, adamczak: 45/49 and 6/6), -wicz reads its vowel + w as /əv/, cz in -czak is /tʃ/ (the syllabifier split it k|z), and -ewski/-awski/-szewski devoice w as -owski does (-niewski is split in the dict and left out). The secondary-stress onset backtrack in postlex also now treats tʃ/dʒ as one onset (spiritual, habitual). Result: 42 strict : 1 (rawski), lenient 68 : 2, top-5000 0 : 0; evaluate-strict's FOREIGN filter excludes these words, so the headline does not move. Measured and rejected: -ski/-sky stress (already 82.5% right), -nger (-inger splits ŋ 253 : ndʒ 73 with letter-identical pairs such as beringer/deringer), -ough, and rz (three treatments). Found, not fixed: the primary-mark anchor in g2p splits a same-length kz → tʃ swap (karczewski → kɑɹtˈʃɛfski).

A word-initial unstressed open u after one consonant keeps its full /u/ (humidity, nutrition, tutorial, futility): English does not reduce open /u/ as it does a/e/o/i. Excluded: a next syllable starting with r (dur-/tur- names take /ɝ/) and sub-, a reducing prefix. Unstressed word-final -sia/-cia after a single consonant is one palatalised syllable, /ʒə/ and /ʃə/ (asia, malaysia, amnesia, acacia); doubled -ccia/-ssia is left alone. Result: 151 strict : 44, lenient 154 : 43, top-5000 4 : 0, headline +0.11. Losses: Polish -owski names and French names that reduce the open u (dumaine, brunelle), and Spanish/Italian -cia/-sia names that keep the hiatus (garcia, valencia). tutorial became rule-exact and left the table, which broke tutorials at runtime, so the -ates plural branch now covers -ials too. Measured and rejected: open initial o (docility, nobility) is 511 : 448 against colonial/bologna/botanic, and -tia (dementia keeps /ʃiə/) is 66%. -ki is 88% Polish -ski and the rest Japanese names.

-ular over a bound Latin root (molecular, particular, avuncular, vehicular) stresses the root's last syllable: 30/30 in the dict, where rendering the root as a free word gave initial stress. The handler renders the stem with a forced final stress through `renderRuleForm`, the rule pipeline factored out of Priority 7, and restores the /ɡ/ of an -ng stem before the suffix (angular, singular). -ysis/-osis: the a before -lysis is lax (analysis, paralysis; the aCy tensing rule had fired inside the merged "naly" slot), the -y- before -sis reduces (paralysis, dialysis) and final -is reduces at 3–4 vowel groups (osmosis, diagnosis: -osis 17 : 2). A word-initial scien- is /saɪən/ (science, scientific: 8/8), and a syllable spelled haus is /haʊs/ (backhaus, neuhaus: 14 : 0). Result: 38 strict : 1 (testicular), lenient 37 : 4, top-5000 5 : 0 (analysis, diagnosis, molecular, science, scientific). Measured and left: a blanket final -is ɪ → ə (409 ɪ : 86 ə outside the Greek suffixes), -esis (11 ɪ : 8 ə), -ar full vs reduced (296 ɝ : 275 ɑɹ), dis + s-cluster stress (23 : 27). Open: holthaus/grothaus lose the h to a th/ph onset before the haus rule sees it.

Two-syllable final stress for French -eau/-eaux/-oux/-aire (chateau, giroux, voltaire: 141 : 11; -ureau is initial in both its dict words, bureau and lamoureaux), -oon (balloon, cartoon: 37 : 2) and bare -een (45 : 9, not -deen or -teen). At 3+ slots a final que slot takes the penult (antique, critique, boutique: 33 : 7), not -esque, whose stress is the root's. Result: 54 strict : 14, lenient 26 : 10, top-5000 0 : 0, headline +0.06. -elle was measured and dropped: most two-slot -elle words are monosyllables (belle). Rejected on the two-syllable population: every bound verb prefix (per- 22%, en- 39%, con- 29%, ad- 27%, in- 31%: POS, not spelling, decides these), -ain 58%, -eer 59%, bare -ee 23%. Found, not fixed: `IPA_VOWELS` in `src/en/stress.ts` (the predictPrincipled path) is missing ɑ.

-singer surnames (bassinger, kissinger, dysinger) are carved back out of the -inger n+dʒ → ŋ collapse: 48 : 18 soft within that family, the opposite of -inger overall, so the postlex rule now excludes any -Xsinger word (keeping the bare word "singer" itself on the collapsed reading). A stressed open a/e/o before the Latin hiatus endings (-ia/-ian/-ious/-eous) is now also blocked from tensing when the onset before the hiatus is an s+stop or ch cluster (caspian, sebastian, lancastrian, appalachia keep the lax vowel), while sh and x are left out of that exclusion so kardashian and ataxia stay tense. Result: strict 38 : 14, lenient 47 : 15, top-5000 0 : 0. The losses are 14 -Xsinger surnames that are hard-g (blessinger, messinger, …), which have no orthographic split from the soft ones. Also measured and rejected in this family: a wholesale -nger soft flip (311 hard : 110 soft), unstressed -ain (already rejected above), and the -on/-en/-in remainders (clusters of ≤4 foreign names each). A single-consonant -Can a-tensing (caban, pagan, satan: 44 : 2) was measured too and left out — its one loss, alan, is a top-5000 word with no common win to offset it. Open: -enger (passenger, clevenger) still misses GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING's initial stress.

German -baum ("tree") joins the compound-surname elements: `PHONEME_RULES` reads a whole -baum syllable as /baʊm/ (rosenbaum, birnbaum, tannenbaum, apfelbaum), guarded to skip the bare word "baum" itself, which keeps the elsewhere-regular English reading (dict /bɔm/, as in the author L. Frank Baum); GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING gains -baum at 3+ syllables (28/28 in the dict), alongside its existing -berger/-inger/-ermann/-heimer/-meyer/-meier/-hofer/-felder set. Name-forming -ville (aldenville, andersonville, bartlesville) gets its own whole-word check in `assignStress`, initial-stressed at 3+ syllables: 149/163 (91.4%), since the syllabifier splits it "vil·le" as two slots rather than the one slot the other reduced-suffix checks key on; the minority is short French-origin roots that keep their own stress (Seville, Deville, Douville, Courville) and Mc-/Mac- surnames. `syllableToIPA` also tenses a stressed single-consonant a/o coda before a bare -le syllable (cradle, ladle, bodle, bohle), the same closed-syllable frame as the existing rules, but leaves the doubled-coda case lax by the syllable-closing default (paddle, bottle, coddle) and excludes a w coda, where -owle is the /aʊ/ digraph (crowle, fowle). Combined: strict 46 : 3, lenient 67 : 8, top-5000 0 : 0. The three losses: axle keeps its dict /æ/ against the new tensing, because its coda is the cluster /ks/, which the single-character exclusion `[^aeiouwylr]` doesn't catch — it needs the same treatment as the doubled-coda case, but for a cluster rather than a digraph, and is left open. ackermanville and andersonville take the new initial stress on the -ville element itself rather than the dict's stress on the -man-/-son- link, which then keeps a full vowel that the new initial-stress rule doesn't predict. Rejected: -oodle tensing, because the -ed/-ing handlers' shared `inflect()` in g2p.ts fabricates a base+"e" probe (`silentE`/`ruleBaseE`) to recover a silent-e stem, and that probe is a real string fed through the normal rule pipeline — it inherits ANY closed-syllable vowel rule added to `syllableToIPA`, so a bare -oodle → tense rule leaked through the fabricated "goodle"/"woodle" bases into goodling/woodling (lenient 0 : 2). That coupling is structural, not specific to this rule, and is worth checking for any future vowel-quality rule that could apply inside a silent-e ending. Also rejected: rule-predicted -able silent-e stems (43 : 61, the same shape as the -al precedent above), and -ical, which has three entangled bugs (Greek ch → /k/, -ical's own reduction depth, and no -ological handler) — left open.

Follow-up on that -ical family (2026-09-27): of its three entangled bugs, one was already fixed and one is confirmed unfixable by rule. ps-/pn-/pt- initial silent p (psalm, pseudo, pneumatic, ptarmigan) and geo-/bio- hiatus stress (geography, biography, bibliography) were already correct; -ologist/-ology agent nouns (psychologist, ecologist, biologist) already come through the existing `greekAgent` handler. Greek ch → /k/ was the real gap: word-initial chem-/chor-/charact-/charis- (chemical, chemistry, chorus, choral, chord, character, charisma) now read /k/ via a new `GREEK_CH_ROOT` root check, plus a `tryCompoundSplit` guard stopping the mined "-logic" compound tail from eating -ological's own stress (psychological, ecological, astrological reach it because psycho-/eco-/astro- are ALSO verified compound heads elsewhere). The submitted root pattern scoped chem- with five branches, two of them `$`-anchored to match only the literal strings "chem" and "chemi" — a whole-word entry wearing a regex, not a root, against the -graphy/-nomy precedent above of generalising one suffix across many words. Trimmed to the three productive suffixes chem- actually takes (-ic, -ist, -o), the combined change (GREEK_CH_ROOT plus the tryCompoundSplit guard) measures strict 7 : 2, lenient 13 : 2 (unrestricted chem- was measured too — 9 : 6 strict, 16 : 8 lenient, technically still net-positive but takes real losses on real words: chemfix, chemie, chemins, chemerinsky — rejected). Attributed: `tryCompoundSplit`'s guard alone is the -ological family, strict 3 : 1 (immulogic); `GREEK_CH_ROOT` alone is chor-/charact-/charis-/chem-, strict 4 : 1 (chorney) — chem- and charis- clear the consonant in chemical/chemistry/chemo/charisma but each word carries a separate, unrelated vowel-or-stress gap the rule doesn't touch, so those four land as lenient wins only, not strict. The two strict losses are inherent to the family, not the trim: chorney (surname, no orthographic split from chorus/choral) and immulogic (a brand name whose dict entry keeps head-stress, traded for the -ological family). Headline 55.83 → 55.84, rules top-5000 69.22 → 69.26 (+2: chemical/chemistry, invisible to rule-diff's win/loss because ipa-dict and CMUdict disagree on chemical's second vowel — ə vs ɪ — and the rules already emit the CMUdict-matching ɪ). Rejected: a blanket ɪ→ə swap for the syllable right before -ical's own -cal (chemical vs critical) — no orthographic feature (onset, cluster, syllable count, distance from stress) separates the split; 26 : 100 over the dict, re-confirming this file's existing "-ical would fix 0 and break 94" finding above. Open: chem- reached through compound decomposition (biochemical, agrochemical, electrochemical, petrochemical, photochemical) still reads /tʃ/ in the rules-only path — `GREEK_CH_ROOT` is gated to `syllableIndex === 0` and would need a position-relative match plus its own false-positive check (e.g. against "anchor") to extend; autobiographical loses bio-'s tense /aɪ/ nested inside a longer prefix; bibliographical still misses primary stress in the -ographical family; chimerical has no orthographic split from its own tʃ root "chimera".

The fabricated silent-e probe the -oodle rejection above flagged as a structural risk turned out to be a shipped bug, not just a hazard (2026-09-27): `inflect()`'s -ed/-ing silent-e fallback built base + "e" unconditionally and ran it through the full rule pipeline to test for a dropped-e stem, so a base whose ending has no silent-e spelling in English got the same magic-e treatment as one that does. asked/masked/formed aren't dict keys, so no gate ever saw "aske"/"maske"/"forme" get tensed and shipped as /ˈeɪskt/ at runtime. A `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE(base)` gate now restricts the probe to endings a real silent e can plausibly follow: a single vowel + single consonant (hop→hoped), a base ending in c/g/s/v/l or st/th (announce, abridge, cause, involve, amble, paste), or an exactly-bisyllabic base — the classic CONtract/conTRACT noun/verb stress class, where the fake syllable happens to land the syllable-count heuristic on the verb reading. A doubled final consonant is excluded either way: that's gemination (kidnapp, sandbagg), not a dropped e, and keeping it out was worth more on evaluate-strict (which sees stress placement) than the plain rule-diff count suggested. Result: strict 31 : 15, lenient 18 : 6, top-5000 0 : 0, headline 55.84 → 55.87. The losses are that same doubled-base trade-off (kidnapped, sandbagged, backslapping, overlapped…) plus a handful of unrelated gaps the gate doesn't touch (gehring, a surname; offspring, a monomorphemic noun the -ing strip shouldn't have split; unprotected's prefix-stress error). Open: the -es plural handler carries the identical fabricated base+"e" probe (gases → /eɪ/), and MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE can't gate it out: "gas" + "e" is a single-vowel + single-consonant base, the exact shape the gate has to allow through for real silent-e words (case, base). No orthographic feature distinguishes "gas"/"cas" (which double before a vowel suffix: gasses, no such rule exists for cases) from words where that shape's silent e is genuine — it is lexical, not structural.

Two more surname/place-name endings and an -ary stress rule (2026-09-27). `GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING` gains -enger (passenger, messenger, ballenger, clevenger) at 3+ syllables, guarded against avenger: its "a-" is the genuine unstressed prefix of avenge, not the Germanic element, tested the same way the weak-a-prefix rules elsewhere in `assignStress` key off a bare "a" first syllable (`syllables[0] === "a"`) rather than a word check. Measured: widening that exclusion to the whole GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING set (any a-initial-syllable name, not just -enger) is net negative — achenbaum, adelsberger, aderman, akerman, amerman, avinger and azinger all keep the dict's correct initial stress and would be broken to fix only avenger, confirming the -enger scoping is load-bearing, not incidental. A 3-syllable Latinate -ary word now takes initial stress by default (secretary, legendary, necessary, voluntary: 57 initial : 7 elsewhere over the dict), excluding the -iary/-uary hiatus (auxiliary, judiciary), whose extra vowel is already excluded by the existing consonant-before-"ary" requirement — a redundant `!/(?:iary|uary)$/` re-check in the same condition was dead code (confirmed by a byte-identical rule-diff dump before/after removing it) and has been deleted. `syllableToIPA` also generalises its -ory/-ary reduction (memory, factory, accessory, directory: /ɔɹ|ɑɹ/ + /i/ → /ɝ/) from a fixed `syllableIndex === 1` (2 slots only) to adjacency-to-the-primary via a new `prevStressed` parameter threaded through both call sites in g2p.ts. **Correction (this note originally said this generalisation moves zero -ary words; it does not — that was checked against the wrong comparison and was wrong in both the code comment and here, now fixed in both places).** Isolated from the rest of the family (this branch alone, disabled vs enabled, filtered to words that also differ from the true pre-change baseline): 23 words are net-new over what the old 2-slot check already reached — 3+-syllable -ory (accessory, advisory, aleatory, compulsory, degregory, desultory, excretory, inventory, peremptory, predatory, promissory, repertory, unsavory) and -ary words whose primary lands elsewhere in the stem, not on the word-initial syllable the separate 3-syllable -ary rule above covers (alimentary, anniversary, elementary, rudimentary, sedimentary, semilegendary, testamentary, unnecessary) — raw /ar/ is still /ɑɹ/ there and only reduces once adjacent to wherever -ental/-mentary's own stress actually lands. -ery genuinely never reaches this branch, confirmed over the whole dict: its vowel is /ɛɹ/ from the plain `^er(?=[aeiouwy])` rule, reduced to /ɝ/ by the general unstressed-vowel merge regardless of stress adjacency, so it never carries the raw /ɔɹ|ɑɹ/ this test looks for — -ory 42:17, -ary 39:22 and -ery 155:6 are all real dict-wide evidence for the -Vry reduction default, but only -ory and -ary's evidence is this branch's own to claim. Independent of slot count, the same generalisation also fixes a real bug: the old 2-slot check ran on the -iary/-uary hiatus slot too and wrongly reduced it, since the maximal-onset syllabifier folds the hiatus's extra vowel into the same slot as "ary". A new `isHiatusSlot` exclusion stops that, fixing actuary, aviary, estuary, january, mortuary, sanctuary, statuary and topiary, which the dict keeps at a full /ɛɹi/. Combined family result: strict 19 : 3, lenient 23 : 4, top-5000 2 : 0 (necessary, secondary), headline 55.87 → 55.90. The 3 losses (dispensary, fragmentary, unitary) are the -ary initial-stress rule's minority population. Rejected in this family: -ative/-itive forcing initial stress on the residual the rules currently get wrong is a losing trade at both 4 and 5 syllables (the dict's own raw population is majority-initial at 4 syllables, 24 : 5, and majority-NOT at 5, 19 : 41 — see the reconciliation note on that line above — but the rules already predict the 4-syllable majority correctly, so forcing initial there wins only 1 case against 4 new losses); -ure/-ture at 4 syllables has a 16 : 6 initial-stress majority in the dict but fails in practice (strict 1 : 2) because postlex's `addFullVowelSecondaries` independently promotes the open penult regardless of primary-stress position (temperature → ˈtɛmpɪˌɹætʃɝ) — open, needs a coupled change to that pass, not just to `assignStress`.

A stressed open o before a syllable-initial x now stays lax regardless of what follows it (boxer, boxes, coxen, doxie, epoxy, hypoxia, obnoxious, biloxi), generalising the x member of the s+stop/sh/ch/th/x onset set noted above (previously gated to a final -Cn slot only, boston/coxon) to any position: strict 18 : 0, lenient 21 : 0, top-5000 1 : 0 (boxes). Measured and rejected in the same pass: a stressed open o before any s+stop alone, with no x restriction, is not a rule (foster vs poster: strict 65 : 29 but top-5000 1 : 2, no orthographic discriminator between the two), and final s/z voicing is a noun/verb alternation decided by POS, not spelling, so it isn't one either. A companion measurement of word-initial th voicing found the shipped "monosyllabic first syllable → /ð/" heuristic backwards: over word-initial th + a/i/o/u, 11 dict words are voiced against 67 voiceless, and the voiced 11 are exactly the closed class of demonstratives/conjunctions (that, than, thou, though, this, those, …) — already dict words, so runtime pronunciation is unaffected regardless. A list-free fix (θ always, dropping the voiced exception) scored strict 64 : 9 but top-5000 2 : 5 and breaks the pinned `this` test; the closed-class word list that would fix it cleanly is a whole-word → pronunciation mapping, which this project's rules forbid in code. Neither form was adopted, so the heuristic ships unchanged — open.

A word-final -ture/-ure syllable at exactly 4 syllables now keeps the primary on the root's first syllable (literature, temperature, architecture, agriculture: 16 initial : 6 elsewhere over the dict), closing the -ure/-ture item left open above, but the mechanism is more layered than that note assumed. `addFullVowelSecondaries` (postlex.ts, FULL_NUCLEI-based) is indeed what places the secondary mark in the finished string, confirming that note — but `secondaryStressIndices` (syllabify.ts) also matters on its own account: a new `tureMedial` exemption there leaves an open single-onset syllable right before the tail unmarked, matching the dict's own unmarked medial (literature, temperature, caricature: 5 : 0), while a complex-onset or closed syllable there (architecture, bonaventure, superstructure) still gets marked, protecting its vowel inside `syllableToIPA`'s own reduction pass (10 : 0 measured). That 10:0 holds only up to `syllableToIPA`'s internal string, not through to the shipped one: `secondaryStressIndices` and `addFullVowelSecondaries` are two independent mechanisms that don't consult each other — the former only suppresses reduction inside `syllableToIPA`, the latter alone decides whether a ˌ mark actually lands in the finished string, by scanning vowel identity against FULL_NUCLEI. FULL_NUCLEI deliberately excludes /ʌ/ (its own comment explains why: marking it also marks a genuinely unstressed /ʌ/ elsewhere and blocks `normalizeStrut`'s /ʌ/→/ə/ demotion there — 0 wins : 5 losses in the patch's own dict-wide count; the brief that commissioned this merge had measured it at 0:4). So a closed <u> syllable right before -ture/-ure (agriculture, acupuncture, horticulture) is marked internally but never gets a ˌ in the output: the unmarked /ʌ/ then reads as unstressed and `normalizeStrut` demotes it to /ə/ — and because `data/en/dict.json` (ipa-dict) itself writes STRUT as /ə/ even under stress, that demoted output is now stress-stripped-exact against the dict entry, so `build-dict` evicts the word from `exceptions.json` and ships the demoted rule form. This is the STRUT-convention trap described below, triggered here by table eviction rather than by removing `normalizeStrut` outright, and it cost `test:common-accuracy` a real point against CMUdict (agriculture ˈæɡɹɪkəɫtʃɝ, CMUdict's stressed /ʌ/) before a guard went in: the new `assignStress` rule now excludes a syllable matching the same closed-<u> frame `PHONEME_RULES` uses for `^u → ʌ` (`/^[^aeiouy]*u[^aeiouy]+$/` on the syllable before the tail), leaving agriculture/acupuncture/horticulture on the old, mis-stressed-but-vowel-safe fallback until FULL_NUCLEI can carry /ʌ/ through this one position without the collateral loss elsewhere — the "record open" item is therefore not "primary right, secondary missing" but "primary and vowel both still wrong, by design, for this trio." Checked directly: the guard does not reach superstructure despite its closed <u> penult ("struc") matching the same frame — it resolves via `tryCompoundSplit`'s mined super-/-structure heads before `assignStress`'s -ture branch is ever reached, and its rules-only dump is byte-identical with and without the guard, so the patch's own "architecture, bonaventure, superstructure" list is one word wider than what this guard's exclusion or inclusion can actually affect. Separately, `nextIsMagicE` drops its `isStressed ||` branch, so a Ce neighbour only counts as silent-e when it is the word's LAST syllable, not merely the stressed one (literature's medial "ra" no longer misreads as magic-e); this is what fixes the two top-5000 words (average, category) but costs a disclosed, systematic set of silent-e compound heads whose magic-e vowel no longer tenses because it isn't word-final either (bakeman, fireman, iceberg, nineteen, timezone, widespread and the rest of that shape) — 135 lenient wins against 80 losses, accepted as bundled. Combined family result, measured on main: strict 72 : 7, lenient 135 : 80, top-5000 2 : 0 (average, category), headline 55.92 → 56.03 (crosses the ≥ 56% target). The guard changes none of this — confirmed by diffing its rules-only dump against the unguarded patch from an identical build lineage, the only lines that differ are agriculture/acupuncture/horticulture themselves, invisible to strict/lenient scoring either way (stress-stripped). 3 of the 7 strict losses — concatenation, incineration, obliteration — are `nextIsMagicE`'s own cost, not the guard's: their -ate stems (concatenate, incinerate, obliterate) become rule-exact under the fix and leave `exceptions.json`, so the -ation suffix handler composes from the freshly rule-derived stem instead of the table entry, and that composition drops the stem's stress mark. This is only visible once `build-dict` has re-mined between the before- and after-dump; a dump taken immediately after `git apply` (before any rebuild) instead shows 72 : 4, which is what a rules-only comparison against a stale table reports and is not what ships — the exceptions.json substitution is confirmed by isolating `nextIsMagicE` on a clean pre-merge-1 checkout and diffing `exceptions.json` before and after a rebuild. The doubled-coda /ɛ/ test's example word moved from temperature to centennial, a substitution that keeps the same asserted property (a word-initial unstressed closed <e> survives a doubled coda) — temperature no longer exercises it now that its first syllable is correctly stressed.
