The -ation handler's rules-fallback cap (2026-09-27) closes the exact gap the previous note flagged. It was gated to ≤3 syllables (locate → location) as a proxy for "is this really an -ate verb", but assignStress's own -ate stress rule already places the primary correctly at any length, so once `nextIsMagicE` made concatenate/incinerate/obliterate rule-exact and evicted them from `exceptions.json`, their -ation nouns fell past the cap to the whole-word path and lost the stem's stress — the "concatenation/incineration/obliteration" losses above. Lifting the cap to any syllable count fixes that directly, guarded against -ification nouns (acidification, justification, qualification), which also end "ation" but derive from -ify, not -ate ("acidificate" isn't a word) — the rule pipeline never refuses a fabricated form, it just mis-syllabifies it, and that garbage would otherwise outrank the correct whole-word reading. -ization gets the matching fix: its stem+ize lookup switched from `lex` (table-only) to `stemPron`, so a rule-exact -ize verb that has itself left the table (legalize, organize) is still read correctly instead of falling to the bare-stem branch and losing the /aɪz/ syllable's own vowel. Both depend on a third fix in `suffixPrimary`: a bare /ɹ/ right after an unstressed /ɪ/ or /ə/ was being pulled into the new syllable's onset, stranding the stress mark between the vowel and the rhotic and permanently blocking the later unstressed-ɪɹ→ɝ phonotactic pass from ever rejoining them; leaving the pair adjacent (generate → generation now composes as dʒɛnɝˈeɪʃən, matching the dict) is the same convention `applyPostStress`'s own onset-maximization already keeps on the whole-word path. Combined: strict 84 : 8, lenient 39 : 4, top-5000 9 : 0 (lenient 4 : 1), headline 56.03 → 56.09, rules top-5000 69.56 → 69.74. The 8 losses: four bare stems that need a final silent -e this fallback doesn't try (compilation, derivation, deprivation, undervaluation, from compile/derive/deprive/value), a genuine noun/verb ambiguity in the -ate reading itself (intimate), a rare word whose isolated rule rendering picks a different doubled-r syllable split than the whole word does (aberration), and two more -ify-shaped nouns the suffix-final "if" check doesn't catch because their own stems aren't -ify words (jubilation — jubilate is real but isn't what it derives from; multiplication has no orthographic tie to "multiplicate"). Rejected: the same rules-fallback pattern for -ance/-ence bare stems (86 : 123 — abund-, consci- aren't words in isolation, so the fabricated form is wrong more often than right), and running the -ate fallback's output through `applyPhonotactics` before returning it (breaks happily). Also re-verified in the same pass and left unchanged: the -es plural handler's bare-stem-vs-base+e choice, compared over the 39 ambiguous dict words — base+e is right 18 : 3, so the existing default stays; no orthographic shape separates gases from corpses.

A rejected, unmerged 3-syllable stress-gram mining pass (2026-09-27): over the 10,772 three-syllable words that reach `assignStress`'s heaviness fallback, the only ending gram clearing support ≥20 / modal agreement ≥75% was -ter at exactly 3 slots (strict 22 : 6), but all 22 wins are Germanic surnames (-walter, -stetter, -meister) and its only touched top-5000 word, newsletter, is a loss (lenient 0 : 1) — failing the ≥3-agreeing-top-5000-words test in spirit even though the raw counts pass it. -age/-ice/-ge (4 : 26, 7 : 17, 4 : 31) and -ble (0 : 9) fail outright because the orthographic 3-slot count includes a silent-e slot that isn't a real syllable (justice, notice are 2-syllable words the syllabifier counts as 3). Word-final -er, the largest population, is 91 : 109 — no majority. -cle is 0 : 0 on segments but costs 3 on the headline. No word-initial gram reaches 3 agreeing top-5000 words either. Conclusion: 3-syllable stress has no qualifying gram left to mine; what remains (property/process/proper/expert/reference/conference/advertise and the rest of this file's open prefix-stress list) needs morphology or POS, not another gram table.

`tryDecomposition`'s compound-split DP now rejects a 3-or-more-word split when any chunk is under 4 letters, falling through instead of returning it (2026-09-27). `this.dictionary` (the exceptions table) is riddled with 3-4-letter surname/word fragments (tal, ary, eba, ugh, oft) the DP has no morphology to weigh against a real decomposition, so it was joining fund+amen+tal over fund+ament+al and giving fundamental the wrong stress. A 2-word split needs no such check — both halves already cover most of the word, so a coincidental short match is unlikely — and a blanket minimum on every chunk is net negative (a candidate try scored strict 1 : 3, since the DP just finds another short-chunk split for the same words). Scoped to 3+-word splits only, it is neutral on the measured dict (strict 0 : 0) while fixing the flagged words: lenient 4 : 1, top-5000 +1 on both `yarn test:common-accuracy` (93.34 → 93.36) and its `--rules` mode (69.74 → 69.76), fixing fundamental/fundamentally/meadowland/postretirement/thirtysomething. The same investigation also measured `mine-compound-parts.ts`'s mined tails and found two more real offenders, "ham" and "sell": both clear the miner's win-rate threshold on a handful of genuine compounds (beckham/cunningham/tottenham; outsell/oversell/undersell) but are mostly the bound English surname/place-name suffix in the dict, and excluding them by name scores strict 32 : 15. That fix was not taken — a hand-named exclusion set is exactly the whole-word list this project's rules forbid in code — and a general net-vs-rule-path adoption test swept over every mined tail failed at every threshold tried, because "land" scores net −59 by the same measure yet is still needed for homeland/farmland/mainland: the dict is surname-heavy enough that a real compound tail and a false one can carry the same negative number, so no blanket cutoff stands in for the two hand-verified cases. That leaves the mined-tails exclusion open for a separate, general test (a dict-wide-net-negative-AND-zero-top-5000-dependents gate, mirroring `FINAL_GRAM_STRESS`'s third adoption test, is the untried candidate). "quire" was checked and kept out of any such exclusion — esquire's correct reading depends on the es+quire split — while inquire's own stress (a separate, unrelated prefix-stress question) stays open. Two decomposition paths were confirmed unaffected by any of this: medial `chem-` (biochemical) resolves through `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`, never reaching the compound DP, and `autobiography` resolves through the plain rule path (its `-graphy`/`-y$` endings), not compound decomposition either.

The "untried candidate" from the paragraph above was tried (2026-09-27) and rejected, and it corrects two of that paragraph's own numbers — **the "land scores net −59" and "untried candidate" claims above are superseded by this paragraph.** A `mine-compound-parts.ts` adoption gate (keep a mined part only if it's net-positive vs the non-compound rule path, or has a top-5000 dependent; support floor 2) was built and measured across a full `build-dict`: rules-only strict 49 : 45, lenient 22 : 21, top-5000 2 : 0, parity flat — but the evaluate-strict headline fell 6 words, and the gate makes `build-dict` run two extra child-process sweeps (once to mine parts, once more to measure each part's net effect with and without it). Not adopted. Its findings correct the earlier hand-measured numbers: "land" is net **+12** (50 : 38) under this gate's argmax-attributed on/off measurement, not the −59 claimed above — the earlier figure was measured a different way and was wrong. "ham" is a coin flip (16 : 20: bickham/bonham reduce correctly with it in, beckham/framingham don't). "sell" is the one genuinely bad tail (3 : 12), matching the original note. No part that failed the net-effect test had any top-5000 dependent, so the top-5000 rescue clause in the gate never fired — it was a no-op on this dict.

R-colouring pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a4e2af80aeb5670c6): a bare ar/er rime before a syllable starting with r no longer fuses into ɑɹ/ɝ (ar·range, er·ror, car·ry — the r is the next syllable's onset), scoped to the next-to-last syllable, not i/u, not after inter/over/under/counter/super; SUFFIX_RULES `^er$` is skipped when the next syllable starts with r; a wrong postlex rule (`ɪɹɝ$` → `ɝɝ`) is removed (all 11 dict words spelling that shape keep ɪɹɝ: mirror, nearer); `^jour` → dʒɝ and `^our` before c/s/t → ɔɹ (court, course, source, journal). Rule-diff: strict 258 : 58, lenient 92 : 30, top-5000 9 : 0. Making "error"-class words rule-exact evicts them from the exceptions table, and that broke their plurals: `errors`/`actors`/`authors`/`doctors`/`investors`/`vendors`/`motors`/`sponsors`/`factors`/`dollars` were reusing the table pronunciation via `wellKnown(stem)` in the -s-plural handler, and once the stem left the table that lookup started returning nothing, falling through to whole-word rule syllabification of the INFLECTED form — where `syllableToIPA`'s final-unstressed-or/ar→ɝ reduction only ever inspects the literal last phoneme, so the plural's own trailing "s" masks the "or"/"ar" nucleus it's meant to catch (`errors` → ˈɛɹɔɹz instead of ˈɛɹɝz). Fixed in `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s -s handler (`src/en/g2p.ts`): when `wellKnown(stem)` fails for an r-final stem, force the rule path on the stem alone (`predictInternal(stem, undefined, true)`) rather than letting the whole inflected word fall through — the stem's own rule prediction is already correct (that's the entire point of this pass), and `sAllomorph` already voices correctly off its r-colored ɝ ending. A broader fix attempted first — teaching `syllableToIPA`'s reduction to look one phoneme past a trailing coda consonant — was rejected: it also fires on any final syllable with a genuine root-final consonant after an unstressed ɔɹ/ɑɹ (concord, cohort, aardvark, denmark, and the already-carefully-scoped -ory/-ary reduction a few lines below it), corrupting far more than it fixed (strict swung to 339 : 310) and was reverted in favor of the g2p.ts fix, which touches only the one call site that was actually broken. Net effect measured on `yarn test:common-accuracy`: 93.36% → 93.56%, zero regressions, 10 words newly correct (the plurals above, most of which were already wrong before this pass for the unrelated ɔɹ-vs-ɝ vowel bug this rule also happens to fix once the stem is rule-derived).

/j/ glide pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a7e59a760eeeede8d). Mid-word unstressed open u after a non-coronal onset (p b k ɡ m f v) keeps the glide even off-stress (accuracy kjɝ, ambulance bjə, amputate pjə), against the coronal population (t d s z n l r) which reduces to a bare ə; a next syllable starting with s is excluded (it's how -Cus bases like campus/circus resyllabify under -es/-on, not this frame). Separately, a mid-word unstressed u+a/o hiatus keeps a full glide+u regardless of stress depth (continuous, actuary, gradual), with the coronal stops palatalizing (t → tʃ, d → dʒ); gu+a is excluded (jaguar/vanguard's silent-u digraph). The -ual morphology handler in `src/en/g2p.ts` now gives an n/l stem a literal j (annual, manual) and a final-d stem dʒ (gradual, residual). Its comment cited "a proper-noun exception set: padua/anzaldua/basaldua" as if the code carved those words out; it does not — grepped both touched files and found only a prose mention, no word-list mechanism — so nothing needed removing, and the comment (here and at the matching u+a/o hiatus branch in `syllabify.ts`) was reworded to say plainly that these are a known miss, not an exception. Rule-diff: strict 80 : 5, lenient 54 : 16, top-5000 9 : 0. `yarn test:common-accuracy` held flat at 93.56% with zero regressions (this pass doesn't touch the r-coloring family the previous commit fixed).

th voicing pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a7e9e1fcc5c6b91cb). Word-initial th before a monosyllabic/magic-e consonant-cluster coda (2+ letters, digraphs like ck/tch/mb/mp/nk/ng/ft/rd/rm/rn/rp/rt counting as one) is voiceless θ regardless of the general open-syllable default (theft, third, thumb, thank, thong), gated by a new `THETA_CLUSTER_CODA` constant in `syllabify.ts`. The postlex `[lɹ]ð → θ` devoicing rule is now guarded off for the closed set of real -ther/-thern/-thest/-thing/-the continuations (farther, further, northern, northey, worthey) and for word-final -orthy (worthy and its compounds), where the r-exclusion the rule is built around is the whole point. This landed as a real merge conflict against the r-coloring pass (both add an independent `if` block to the same skip-computation site in `syllableToIPA`, right after the existing -the-cluster check); resolved by keeping both blocks back to back — the r-onset-skip block first (unchanged from commit dfac9ef), then the THETA_CLUSTER_CODA block. Rule-diff: strict 30 : 4, lenient 2 : 0, top-5000 2 : 1. The one top-5000 loss, `thermal` (rules-only θɝməɫ → ðɝməɫ), is a table hit at runtime (`this.dictionary["thermal"]` still holds the correct pronunciation), so it costs nothing on `yarn test:common-accuracy`, which instead rose (93.58%, zero regressions, +1 word: `northerner`).

s/z voicing pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a7c08a1d14cbdae37). Final -se voicing no longer fires on the German/Baltic surname spellings -eise/-oese (freise/heise/weise, froese/genoese/kroese — the `[ieo]$` half of the voicing test was matching a digraph's LAST letter too); -tose (the sugar/chemistry suffix: comatose, fructose, galactose, lactose, maltose) is now voiceless, unlike its sibling -pose/-rose/-nose/-mose classes which stay majority-voiced; the unguarded `^ns$` → `nz` rule now skips before f/d (transfer, transform, transducer — trans- keeps its coda voiceless there, matching its own -g/-r family already handled); the si-onset voicing frame extends to a -ti/-te tail (composite, opposite, positive, dispositive, positivism). Clean merge, no conflicts. Rule-diff: strict 17 : 0, lenient 11 : 0, top-5000 2 : 0 — zero losses on every axis. `yarn test:common-accuracy` held flat at 93.58% with zero regressions.

-nge inflections pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a73d3e79a0f6606f8). `inflect()`'s closed-stem list (`ll|ss|ch|sh|ck|ng|lk`) keeps a bare rule-derived stem for a base ending in one of those clusters, because adding a fictitious silent e usually changes the vowel wrongly (call+ed, reach+ing) — but "ng" is the one cluster where that default is sometimes wrong: change/range/hinge really did drop a silent e, while bring/sing/hang never had one, and the two shapes are orthographically identical (chang vs bring). The one exact signal is the table's own -es plural: -nge is always spelled -nges (changes, ranges), never -ngs, so a stem is restored only when the table independently attests its -es form (`this.wellKnown(base + "es", ...)` in `src/en/g2p.ts`) — corroboration by an existing table entry, not a word list. Clean merge, no conflicts; the worktree's own new test block in `__tests__/en-inflection-regressions.test.ts` (38 cases, both `disableDict` states) was taken as-is. Rule-diff: strict 6 : 0, lenient 6 : 0, top-5000 2 : 0 — zero losses. `yarn test:common-accuracy` held flat at 93.58%, zero regressions. Open (per the worktree's own note): hinged/cringing/binged have no -es dict entry to corroborate against, and banging collides with the surname exception bange, both left unresolved.

Stress pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-aa8f0b16c10383ecc). The Greek antepenult-ending regex (`graph|nom|soph|scop|path|gam|crac`) gains `tom|em|etr`, covering -tomy/-emy/-etry at 3+ syllables (anatomy, academy, archenemy, spectrometry): strict 4 : 0, lenient 6 : 1, top-5000 1 : 0. A 3-syllable Greek -crat word now takes initial stress (democrat, autocrat, bureaucrat, eurocrat, kleptocrat, plutocrat, technocrat: 7/7 in the dict), checked ahead of the prefix loop whose "de" entry would otherwise stress the root; 4+-syllable aristocrat is untouched by the length guard and was already correct. Clean merge, no conflicts. Rule-diff: strict 5 : 0, lenient 7 : 1, top-5000 1 : 0. `yarn test:common-accuracy` held flat at 93.58%, zero regressions. Measured and rejected/left open by this pass: 2-syllable prefix stress needs part-of-speech, not a general rule (con 38%, de 36%, re 40%, pro 23% agreement with dict stress, all below a usable threshold); -ent/-ant has no safe suffix-based stress override (49 : 129 against the rules' existing default); a pro- laxing carve-out scored net negative on the common-word subset (1 : 2); -ol (protocol-class words) is blocked from a clean win by two unrelated segment errors elsewhere in the pipeline, not by the stress rule itself.

ASCII-g fix (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-ac84c641d3ecd267e). Three `PHONEME_RULES` entries in `src/en/syllabify.ts` (`^giv`, `^gif`, `^gir`) were spelling their hard-/ɡ/ onset with the ASCII letter "g" (U+0067) instead of the IPA script letter "ɡ" (U+0261) every other rule, `data/en/dict.json` and the rest of the codebase use — invisible on screen, but a different Unicode codepoint, so gift/girl/give under `disableDict` silently shipped text that couldn't be string-compared against the dictionary's own /ɡ/. Fixed by respelling all three entries with U+0261. `src/en/phonotactics.ts`'s `SYLLABIC_L_LEFT`/`SYLLABIC_L_RIGHT` sets, which had carried the same dead ASCII "g" alongside the real ɡ (redundant even before this fix, since nothing else ever emitted it), had it dropped now that nothing produces it. A new regression test, `__tests__/en-ascii-g.test.ts` (35 cases: rules-only, dict-enabled, `toIPA()` en-US/en-GB, out-of-vocabulary nonce words, and ARPABET conversion), guards against the codepoint reappearing. Clean merge, no conflicts; the worktree's untracked test file was copied over directly, since `git diff` doesn't see untracked files. Rule-diff: strict 29 : 1, lenient 3 : 0, top-5000 2 : 0. The one strict loss (girdler, rules-only ˈɡɝdəɫɝ → ˈɡɝdɫɝ, an unrelated syllabic-l epenthesis wobble) is a table hit at runtime and costs nothing shipped. `yarn test:common-accuracy` held flat at 93.58%, zero regressions.

Careful-vs-casual dictionary variant pick (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a1f4f9a5bff340f54, `scripts/build-dict.ts` only). ipa-dict/en_US.txt lists more than one pronunciation for 8,419 entries, and the old code always took `variants[0]`, which is frequently the CASUAL-SPEECH form (next ˈnɛks, not ˈnɛkst; accounted əˈkaʊnəd, not əˈkaʊntɪd) — and since `data/en/dict.json` is both the runtime lexicon and the reference every eval script scores against, shipping the casual form was also grading against it. Of the 8,419, 7,909 have exactly two unique variants (357 have 3, 153 have 4, left untouched — no clean two-way diff generalises to them); of those, 3,852 differ only by stress or a same-length vowel swap (a lexical axis, never a register one, so also left on `variants[0]`). For the remaining 4,057, a Levenshtein edit script between the stress-stripped long and short form is classified as a casual deletion only when every inserted/deleted segment is one of four documented processes — word-final t/d cluster simplification, t/d lost from /nt(d)/, a reduced /ə/ lost before a sonorant, or a /j w/ glide lost, optionally paired with an adjacent vowel reducing to /ə/ as a direct consequence. That classifies 1,241 of the 4,057 as a recognised pair; 451 differ from the old pick. Checked against pinned CMUdict as a tie-breaker (not consulted at pick time, only to confirm the rule): 0 of the 451 are worse, 8 are strictly better (debussy, cusip, uclaf — proper nouns where CMUdict has only the fuller reading), the rest tie. Heteronyms (the union of `homographs.en` + `homographs-custom` + `homographs-misaki`) are excluded entirely, since a homograph's two variants can encode a sense split, not a register one. 330 of the 451 flips actually change a `dict.json` value after `custom.dict` and the long-word trim are applied on top. This changes the REFERENCE, so the usual rule-diff win/loss test doesn't apply; judged instead on `yarn test:common-accuracy` (93.58% → **93.74%**, zero regressions, 8 words newly correct: international, environmental, interactive, atlantic, intellectual, interaction, implemented, implementing) and `yarn test:homographs` (87.06%, unchanged). `scripts/eval-baseline.json` (the frozen 2026-06-13 target) is left untouched, per its own stated convention of never moving except by a genuine rule recovery — this is a reference change, not one. Open: the 3–4-variant entries are untouched by this pass: reduced-vowel and glide are the softer of the four categories (a real minimal pair can share their shape without being a register split) and would need a narrower rule if extended there.

SQUARE-vowel + -ies plural pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a253235be20724714). Two independent fixes. (a) `syllableToIPA` (`src/en/syllabify.ts`): a stressed or secondary open `<a>` right before a syllable whose onset is a bare r + i/y, and which is phonologically open (ri/ry, a hiatus, or a trailing doubled consonant/ch — not a true single-consonant coda), now takes the SQUARE vowel /ɛ/ instead of the checked TRAP one (necessarily, popularity, arizona, charity, clarity, baritone); a genuine single-consonant coda still closes the syllable and keeps /æ/ (arid, paris, caribbean — caribbean is a measured, accepted miss). (b) `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s generic -es plural handler (`src/en/g2p.ts`) now excludes -ies words, leaving them to the existing y-restoration loop, which rebuilds the -y base (vary → varies) before its own table lookup; stripping just "es" left a base like "vari", which collided with an unrelated dict headword (the surname Vari) and silently returned its pronunciation instead — the same collision was also mispronouncing buries/centuries/liberties/skies/mercies/gullies/hurries/parodies/rabies/shies. Rule-diff: strict 68 : 19, lenient 45 : 9, top-5000 3 : 0 (arizona, necessarily, popularity). Gates: `yarn test:common-accuracy` 93.74% → 93.76%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.18% → 64.28% (top-5000 66.50% → 66.58%), lenient 83.69% → 83.78% (top-5000 84.83% → 84.88%); full-dict `yarn test:eval` strict 55.38% → 55.43%, lenient 77.23% → 77.27%; `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.58% → 56.65% (+61 words correct); parity flat at 93.58%; homographs flat at 87.06%. Losses (19 strict) are proper names sharing the open-ri shape with no orthographic split (mariposa, larimer, garibay, cariker, parikh, rarick, …), one common-shaped loss (barbarity, a reduplicated bar-BAR-ity the dict keeps æ on for no visible reason), and one -ies false positive (alkalies, whose singular genuinely ends in -i, not a restored -y — no spelling-only way to tell it apart from the -y words this branch is for). Open: variable/variance/variation still read through their bound stem "vari" (itself a dict headword, a surname) via their morphology handler, so the r-vowel rule above never reaches them.

get/-ange(r) pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a6fded6dd32a8d17a, narrowed on merge). `syllableToIPA`: a "get" syllable is hard /ɡ/ unless the preceding syllable ends in "d" (forget, target, beget, retarget, the surnames helget/herget/leget/marget/winget vs budget/gadget/midget/fidget/nugget) or is followed by a silent-e "te" slot (the French -ette suffix stays soft: suffragette, georgette). `postlex`: the -ange → /eɪndʒ/ tensing rule extends past the bare word to -anger/-angers/-angerous (danger, stranger, ranger, granger, dangerous), guarding -lange (flange, phalange) the same way the bare rule already does. Both landed with a regression the source agent's own numbers (claimed strict 19:1) didn't catch: making "get" and "changes"/"exchanges"/"ranges" rule-exact evicted them from `exceptions.json` (build-dict's rule-exact eviction), and two different existing corroboration mechanisms inside `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s `inflect()` (`src/en/g2p.ts`) depend on those exact table entries — the -ed/-ing doubled-consonant undo (`wellKnown(undoubled, ...)` for getting → get) and the -nge silent-e restoration's own -es corroboration check (`wellKnown(base + "es", ...)` for changed/changing/exchanged/exchanging/ranged/ranging → changes/exchanges/ranges). A whole-dict rule-diff on the agent's unmodified patch found the real numbers: strict 19:8, lenient 22:11, top-5000 3:3 (changed/changing/getting newly wrong) — the same "rule-exact eviction breaks a dependent's table corroboration" trap the -nge inflections pass and the -ce reduction pass both hit before it (see the two-traps section below and the rules-only pass note above it). Narrowed on merge, not in the worktree: (a) `gFromGetSuffix` now also requires a preceding syllable, so bare "get" itself is left off the rule and stays a table hit (protects "getting"); (b) the -ange lookahead dropped its `ə[sz]` branch, so the tensing rule no longer claims the bare -anges plural/verb-s form (protects changed/changing/exchanged/exchanging/ranged/ranging), while keeping the -er/-ers/-erous agentive forms, which don't depend on that corroboration. Re-measured after narrowing: strict 15:1, lenient 18:4, top-5000 1:0, zero common-word regressions. The one remaining strict loss (puget, French -get) and the lenient-only losses (paget, vegetable, vegetal — the -able/-al stem-recovery fallback fabricates "veget") are the agent's own originally-accepted losses, unaffected by the narrowing. Gates: `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 93.76%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.28% → 64.34% (top-5000 66.58% → 66.61%), lenient 83.78% → 83.84% (top-5000 84.88% → 84.94%); full-dict `yarn test:eval` strict 55.43% → 55.45%, lenient 77.27% → 77.29%; `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.65% → 56.66% (+14 words); parity and homographs flat. Lesson for future merges: a `wellKnown(..., skipMorphology=true)` call buried in a DIFFERENT handler than the one being changed is an easy blind spot for a worktree agent testing only its own rule-diff in isolation — grep for `wellKnown(` calls on any stem a rule change might newly evict before trusting a self-reported win/loss count of 1 or 0.

de+si- voicing / ear-boundary pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-aad1c09cb2c5d441b, narrowed on merge). `syllableToIPA`: a word-initial de+si- syllable voices its s when the syllable continues as "sig" (design, designate) or "sir"+vowel (desire, desirous); and a syllable that closes bare on "ear" takes /ɝ/ when the NEXT syllable opens with n/c across the syllable boundary (searcher, earner, kearney), r excluded (rearrange is re-+ar·range, a prefix-boundary coincidence, not an -ear root). Landed with two regressions the source agent's own rule-diff (dict-only, strict 1:1 + 16:2) couldn't see, because both only show up on the shipped-pipeline gates. (1) The ear-boundary rule's l-onset branch (6 of its 16 wins were l-initial — brearley, cearley, earley, earlie, kearley, pearline, all proper names, table hits at runtime either way) also fired on "yearlong" (year|long — not routed through compound-split the way the agent's own comment assumed; "year" isn't a verified head in `compound-parts.json`), turning its dict ˈjɪɹˈɫɔŋ segments into ɝ and tripping `yarn test:parity` by exactly one word. `mine-exceptions.ts`'s miner never memorizes a 2-primary-stress dict entry like this one into `exceptions.json` (treats it as a "corrupt / non-reduced compound" transcription, ~line 322), so yearlong was never going to be table-corrected regardless of what the rule predicts — the compound-split gap for "year" is the real cause and is still open. (2) Making bare "design" rule-exact (the de+si- fix's own point) evicted it from `exceptions.json` and broke two unrelated call sites that depended on it being a table entry: the -ed/-ing `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE` bisyllabic probe fabricates "designe" to test for a dropped silent e, and the silent-g `^ign(?=s?$)` rule needs that digraph at the literal syllable end — `syllabify()` resplits "designe" as de·sig·ne, handing the "n" to a fake "ne" syllable and reading "sig" with a hard /ɡ/ (designed → dɪˈzɪɡnd, not dɪˈzaɪnd); and the -s-plural handler's r-final-stem rule-fallback (added earlier for the error/actor/doctor-class eviction) didn't cover n-final stems, so designs fell through the same corrupted path (dɪˈzaənz). Narrowed on merge: (a) the ear-boundary rule's next-syllable onset set dropped "l" (`/^[nc]/`, not `/^[nlc]/`); (b) `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE` excludes any base ending in the silent-g digraph "gn$" (design, resign, align, campaign, benign — no real English word takes a genuine dropped-e spelling after it, so the exclusion costs nothing the probe was ever meant to cover); (c) the -s-plural stem fallback extends from r-final only to `/(?:r|gn)$/`. A blanket n-final extension was tried first and rejected: it fixed designs but broke 10 other n-final words (baboons, clarins, cosens, defenses, responses, valens, the rosecrans/rosencrans/rosenkrans family) whose `wellKnown()` failure wasn't a genuine rule-exact eviction — most n-final misses legitimately need the later -tion/-sion/-es checks or the whole-word fallback, unlike r-final and gn-final, which are the two shapes proven to always be rule-exact-or-nothing. Re-measured after narrowing: strict 12:2 (pearce, designor — both accepted, table hits at runtime), lenient 16:1, top-5000 1:0 (design), parity exactly flat, `yarn test:common-accuracy` 93.76% → 93.78% (designed/designs fixed, plus a pre-existing bug on "signs" the same gn$ exclusion incidentally fixed — "sign" was already rule-exact on main, unrelated to this package, and had been silently broken by the identical fabricated-e mechanism the whole time). Gates: `yarn test:eval` strict 55.45% → 55.46%, lenient 77.29% → 77.30%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.34% → 64.35% (top-5000 66.61% → 66.64%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.66% → 56.67%; homographs flat 87.06%.

-ion/-iant/-ient glide pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-afeecc1ad47d32a40). `syllableToIPA`: an unstressed i+vowel after a bare (uncrowded) l/n onset, in the syllable right after the primary stress, reads as the /j/ glide + schwa (million, companion, union, brilliant, valiant, convenient) instead of the general i-ə hiatus the vowel rules give it by default — the i-counterpart of the mid-word u+a/o hiatus glide rule already in this file. `!isStressed` keeps it off a syllable whose own vowel carries the stress (client, reliant); `prevStressed` (adjacency to the primary, not just "somewhere unstressed") was measured against a plain `!isStressed` gate and won by one fewer strict loss for zero fewer wins. -ian/-ium/-ius/-iam were measured and excluded: -ian's l/n-onset split is unpredictable by spelling (35.7%/9.3% glide, civilian/australian/brazilian/italian are open misses already owned by `LATIN_HIATUS_ENDING`), and -ium/-ius/-iam are net-negative as a class (epithelium 1:27, genius 3:18, william 4:7). Clean merge, no conflicts; typecheck passed on first application. Rule-diff: strict 18:3, lenient 7:3, top-5000 1:0 (union) — close to the agent's own self-reported 19:3/7:3/1:0, and unlike the two prior packages this session, a full check of the words' own -s/-ly/-ic derivatives (unions, brilliantly, millions, companions, conveniently, minions, pinions, valiantly, leniency) found no rule-exact-eviction collateral damage: all resolve correctly through the rule path directly, no table dependency broke. The 3 losses are defoliant (shares valiant's exact shape but the dict keeps it un-glided, no spelling split found), lenient (loses to its own derivatives leniently/leniency, which DO glide — a same-lemma dict inconsistency) and lilienthal (a German surname). Gates: `yarn test:common-accuracy` 93.78% → 93.80% (millions newly correct); `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.35% → 64.41% (top-5000 66.64% → 66.67%), lenient 83.85% → 83.86% (top-5000 flat 84.96%); full-dict `yarn test:eval` strict 55.46% → 55.47%, lenient 77.30% → 77.31%; `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.67% → 56.69%; parity and homographs flat. Open (per the worktree's own note): opinion/dominion/convenience aren't reached because `assignStress` puts the primary on the wrong syllable for that shape (syllable 0, dict wants 1), so `prevStressed` never sees the adjacency — a pre-existing stress bug, not this rule's gap; reunion (re+u merges into one hiatus slot); onion's first vowel. Compression trigger: the three rule modules are now 2638 code lines (g2p.ts 814, syllabify.ts 1319, postlex.ts 505; comment-and-blank-aware count), over the 2600 ceiling — flagged for a future compression pass, not run here.

iFire/-ate-tail/silent-e-plural pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-a89f5e0091d44882c, narrowed on merge). Three independent fixes. (a) `syllableToIPA`: a stressed open i two orthographic syllable slots before a bare "-ate" tail (climate, private, pirate, migrate) is tense /aɪ/, propagating the same signal `nextIsMagicE` gives a directly-adjacent magic-e syllable one slot further back across the reduced "Ca" link; an x-spelt link (fixate) is excluded. (b) `PHONEME_RULES`: "ui" before d/n keeps two vowels as a genuine hiatus (druid, fluid, ruin, and the -uing gerund: pursuing, ensuing), unlike -t/-s/-c which stay the single fused /u/ of fruit/suit/circuit. (c) `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s -s-plural handler extends its r-final-stem rule-exact-eviction fallback (from the earlier error/actor/doctor fix) to any 2–3-syllable silent-e stem (device, notice, license, balance, audience), which resyllabifying the whole inflected form as one unit had been losing (devices → de·vi·ces mis-tenses the middle i); the matching -es branch gets the same fix for a rule-exact -us/-is/-ix TENSE_ENDINGS base (viruses, irises). This package's own g2p.ts diff landed on the exact lines package 3's `(?:r|gn)$` narrowing touched; merged by chaining both fallbacks in priority order (r/gn-final first, then the silent-e-stem test), keeping both sides' full reasoning as comments. Doing so re-exposed the -nge eviction trap a THIRD time, through a new mechanism: `syllabify()` gives every magic-e word its own trailing silent-e slot regardless of real syllable count (change → chan·ge, 2 slots; exchange → ex·chan·ge, 3 slots — indistinguishable BY COUNT from device's 3-slot de·vi·ce, 2 real syllables), so the new fallback's `syllabify(stem).length > 1` bound is satisfied by monosyllabic change/exchange/range too, making "changes"/"exchanges"/"ranges" rule-exact and evicting them from `exceptions.json`, breaking the SAME `wellKnown(base + "es")` corroboration in `inflect()` that packages 2 and (indirectly) 3 already protected — changed/changing/exchanged/exchanging/ranged/ranging regressed again. Narrowed on merge: the silent-e-stem fallback excludes a stem ending in "nge" outright, since that class is governed by the -nge inflections pass's own narrower, already-established mechanism, and no -nge word was among this package's own target examples. Re-measured after narrowing: strict 145:2 (burrowes, heikes — both the agent's own originally-accepted losses, syllable-boundary surname wrinkles), lenient 104:22, top-5000 1:0 (devices), zero common-word regressions — within 3 of the agent's own self-reported 148:2, the exact delta being the three -nge words blocked. Gates: `yarn test:common-accuracy` 93.80% → 94.14% (17 words newly correct, zero regressions, word-diffed to confirm); `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.41% → 64.53% (top-5000 66.67% → 66.69%), lenient 83.86% → 83.95% (top-5000 84.96% → 84.99%); full-dict `yarn test:eval` strict 55.47% → 55.62%, lenient 77.31% → 77.39%; `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.69% → 56.84% (+137 words); parity and homographs flat. Open (per the worktree's own note): client (ie+nt misses the `^ie` hiatus rule). Compression trigger: the three rule modules are now 2655 code lines (g2p.ts 825, syllabify.ts 1325, postlex.ts 505), further over the 2600 ceiling set by package 4's merge — a compression pass is now overdue across two sessions' worth of accumulation.

Dependency-aware eviction pass (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-ad960d9f66155a2f9, `scripts/mine-exceptions.ts` only). The rule-exact eviction that keeps `exceptions.json` small (a word whose rules-only prediction already matches the dict, `ed < 1`, is never shipped) is correct for the word in isolation, but `tryMorphologicalAnalysis` looks its STEM up in the table too — through `wellKnown()`/`lex()` for the -s/-ed/-ing/-er/-ation/-ance/-ence/-al/-able family and the -nge -es corroboration, and directly through `this.dictionary[chunk]` for `tryDecomposition` — and neither lookup is gated by `disableDict`. Every hand-patched fix so far in this file (the get/-ange(r), de+si-, and iFire packages above) was this exact trap discovered one word at a time. This pass replaces the discovery-by-bug-report process with a measurement: predict every dict word twice under `disableDict:true`, once against the shipped table and once with eviction undone entirely, find the 585 words that flip from wrong to exact, attribute each flip to its specific evicted stem or `tryDecomposition` split (a customDict probe for the regular derivational families; a local reimplementation of `tryDecomposition`'s own DP, against a file written with eviction undone, for the 8+-letter compound-chunk cases it can't reach through a customDict probe), and re-pin only the 491 stems actually responsible — dropping any stem whose regressions (found the same way, via the substring/prefix collision it risks on the rest of the dict) outnumber the fixes attributed to it. A stem ending in one of `inflect()`'s closed-inflection clusters plus a silent e (change/range/hinge/singe) is never re-pinned: re-pinning it directly would bypass the -nge pass's own -es corroboration mechanism entirely (singe, re-pinned for a legitimate flip needing it as its own base, silently broke singing — sing + -ing — through `wellKnown(base + "e")`'s ungated first check in `inflect()`).

Result: `exceptions.json` 53,375 → 53,882 entries (1157.2 → 1166.6 KB); 499 words resolved, 6 regressed and kept anyway because each is net-positive on the whole dict (caterings, experimenter, fared, forthwith, impacting, segmenting); added build time 58–63s measured across two consecutive runs, on top of ~33s for the rest of the pipeline; `exceptions.json` is byte-identical (sha256) across two consecutive `build-dict` runs — confirmed deterministic, not dependent on the exceptions.json state a run starts from. Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` 93.58% → 93.64%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` 94.14% → 94.44% (word-diffed against the pre-patch report: +15/−0, zero newly wrong — programs, marketing, reasons, tables, singles, clothes, booking, cables, visiting, indexed, listening, visits, visited, catering, appearing); `yarn test:common-accuracy --rules` 71.00% → 71.60%; `yarn test:eval` strict 55.62% → 56.06%, lenient 77.39% → 77.59%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 64.53% → 65.00% (top-5000 66.69% → 67.11%), lenient 83.95% → 84.18% (top-5000 84.99% → 85.21%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 56.84% → 57.27%; `yarn test:homographs` flat at 87.06%/87.13%. A runtime spot-check (errors, designs, actors, tutorials, singing, bringing, changed, getting — all previously hand-patched by name in this file — under both `disableDict` states) is byte-identical before and after: this pass generalizes those fixes into a mechanism, it doesn't replace or duplicate them.

Read the gains honestly, per this file's own rule of the goal that a rules-only score is not independent of the mined table. `yarn test:eval`, `yarn test:eval --subset common` and `yarn test:common-accuracy --rules` all run `disableDict:true` — exactly the runtime this pass adds table entries to reach — so their rise is **table-driven, not rule-driven**: 491 more stems memorized, not one new `PHONEME_RULES`/`SUFFIX_RULES` entry. It moves those goal-table checkpoints because that is literally what those rows measure, but it is not evidence the rules themselves generalize any better than they did before this commit. `yarn test:parity` and `yarn test:common-accuracy` (default, dict-enabled mode) are the independent evidence: they run the shipped pipeline, where a word's own rule-exact eviction is, by construction, irrelevant to ITS OWN prediction (a direct table hit and a correct rule output are the same string either way — see the diagnostic check the pass itself runs, "dict-enabled mismatches", which barely moves). What moved there instead is real out-of-table derived words (programs, marketing, tables, …) whose morphology handler can now find a stem the table used to hide from it. The +15/−0 `test:common-accuracy` fix is the one number in this paragraph that reflects better predictions for words nobody hand-tuned.

Double-primary normalisation and compound-miner alignment (merged 2026-09-28, worktree agent-ab8ffd9899538bf13). A standard IPA transcription has exactly one primary stress, but ~1,400 `dict.json` entries carried two or more ˈ marks — ipa-dict compounds and prefixed derivatives it never reduced to primary+secondary (etcetera ˈɛtˈsɛtɝə, videotape ˈvɪdioʊˈteɪp, asynchronous ˈeɪˈsɪŋkɹənəs). `mine-exceptions.ts` used to refuse to memorize these as exceptions whenever the rule path already gave a single, cleaner primary — leaving the word relying on the rule never regressing, with the reference itself still unmatchable either way. `scripts/build-dict.ts` now fixes this at the source: `normalizeMultiplePrimaryStress` keeps the LAST ˈ and demotes every earlier one to ˌ, so `dict.json` itself is always single-primary and the old skip check (three call sites — the initial `shippedCands` filter, the runtime-refinement loop, and a third in commit 1's post-repin refinement loop the original patch predated and never touched, removed here by the integrator for the same now-dead reason) could simply be deleted. Which mark to keep was measured against pinned CMUdict over the 36 double-primary words where CMUdict itself commits to one primary and the syllable counts line up (the largest clean sample available — CMUdict carries the same unreduced-compound quirk on 94% of the rest): keeping the LAST ˈ agrees with CMUdict 24:9 over keeping the FIRST. The FIRST-agreeing minority is French-stressed surnames and true N+N compounds that keep English's initial compound stress (dillard, luxembourg, nestle); the LAST-agreeing majority is prefix+root derivations and the -teen numbers, where English stresses the root/final element (unfair, predate, eighteen, asynchronous) — the larger, more general class, and it matches the hand-picked custom.dict entries already carrying this exact pattern, which are now redundant and removed (etcetera, videotape, videotaped, travelodge — 4 entries).

The known cost of picking LAST: a genuine N+N compound the dict spells double-primary now carries its FINAL element's primary instead of its natural initial one — microsoft (dict ˌmaɪˌkɹoʊˈsɔft, rules ˈmaɪkɹəsəft) and backstage (dict ˌbækˈsteɪdʒ, rules ˈbækˌsteɪdʒ) both mismatch the rules' correct initial-stress prediction now that the reference itself points the other way; this is a reference artifact, not a rule regression, and it moves `yarn test:parity`/`yarn test:eval`/`evaluate-strict` numbers in both directions depending on which convention a given word's dict entry already leaned toward.

The reference fix also let `scripts/mine-compound-parts.ts` be corrected: `tryCompoundSplit`'s TAIL verification now accepts a tail that verifies under either the ordinary head-primary/tail-secondary join OR a tail keeping its own primary with the head's demoted (`joinTailPrimary`; `tailsReversed` in `compound-parts.json`, a subset of `tails`), while HEAD verification is unchanged — loosening heads too let bound Latin/Greek prefixes that are also short free dict words (con/com/ab/im) hijack real prefix+root derivations before `assignStress`'s own prefix rules ever ran, breaking 22 pinned tests. Restricted to tails only, this fixes real English prefix/root formations the dict spells root-stressed (overdone ˌoʊvɝˈdʌn/ˈdən — STRUT-neutral match; overabundance ˌoʊvɝəˈbʌndəns/ˈbədəns — same), while whereupon/nonesuch/somewhat/overreact stay open (their heads, where/none/some/over, don't independently verify strongly enough, or the segments themselves are wrong beyond just stress). `src/en/g2p.ts`'s `tryCompoundSplit` excludes two more tails from the mined table for the same reason the pre-existing `-logic` exclusion protects "-ological": "-matic" newly cleared the win-rate floor purely from the loosened tail test (dogmatic/dramatic/systematic) and broke a pinned test by giving dogmatic the free-standing /dɔɡ/ instead of the initial-closed-o /dɑɡ/ frame; "pose" newly verified from "impose" alone and combined wrongly with the pre-existing, unrelated "pro" head on propose (/prəˈpoʊz/ needs the reduced prefix vowel, not "pro"'s free /proʊ/). automotive is a known, accepted regression from this same family (dict ˌɔtəˈmoʊtɪv vs rules ˌɔtoʊˈmoʊtɪv — a second-syllable linking-vowel reduction depth question, not a stress error).

Adoption evidence, measured on main by the integrator after commit 1 (dependency-aware eviction) and commit 2 (fourth compression pass) had already landed, not by the source worktree against its own older base: `yarn test` 1117/1117, `yarn typecheck` clean, `yarn test:parity` 93.64% → 93.99% strict (+0.35) / 99.61% → 99.86% lenient, `yarn test:common-accuracy` 94.44% → 94.58% (word-diffed: +8/−1, net +7 — see below), `yarn test:eval` 56.06% → 56.15% strict / 77.59% → 77.66% lenient, `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.00% → 65.09% full / 67.11% → 67.18% top-5000 (both up), lenient 84.18% → 84.16% full / 85.21% → 85.18% top-5000 (both down by 0.02–0.03 — the one tracked-but-not-gated number in this whole file's history that dropped from a confirmed-adopted pass), `test:common-accuracy --rules` 71.60% → 71.68%, `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.27% → 57.57%, `yarn test:homographs` 87.06% → 87.62% micro / 87.13% → 87.75% macro.

The one word-diff loss on the independent `yarn test:common-accuracy` metric (word-diffed properly against a freshly-generated, not stale, before-report — an integrator process note: the first diff attempt compared against a leftover report file from an earlier interrupted session and had to be redone) is **booking** (ˈbʊkɪŋ → ˈbukɪŋ), and it is a genuine, if narrow, side effect of combining this pass with commit 1's dependency-aware eviction mechanism, not a bug in this pass's own diff. "book" is rule-exact on its own (ˈbʊk both ways) but a fabricated "booke" probe inside `inflect()`'s -ing handler (`MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE("book")` is true — it ends vowel+consonant — so the handler tries `predictInternal("booke", …)` before falling back further) mis-syllabifies as boo·ke and reads the first syllable through the generic `^(?:ui|oo|ue)` → u rule instead of the more specific `^ook` → ʊ rule that "book" alone reaches; this has always been latent. Commit 1's dependency-aware eviction previously re-pinned "book" in `exceptions.json` specifically because "booking" (among others) depended on it — `lex(base)` in `inflect()` would find it and return early, before ever reaching the buggy fabricated-probe branch. Re-running the SAME eviction algorithm against the now-renormalised `dict.json` (a different reference changes which predictions count as correct in the pass's own net-effect measurement) re-pins fewer stems overall (491 → 399), and "book" is one of the 92 that drops out. The fabricated-"Ce"-probe mis-syllabification bug itself (generalizable beyond "book": any rule-exact, vowel+single-consonant-ending stem whose `^ook`-style specific rule depends on the coda staying in the SAME syllable) is open, unrelated to and pre-dating this pass; the practical fix is the dependency-aware eviction mechanism already doing its job by re-pinning the stems it currently catches. Net effect stays clearly positive (+7 on an n=5000 independent benchmark) and nothing that must not drop dropped, so the pass is adopted as-is rather than held for this one word.

-ook/-ood inflection fix (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, `src/en/g2p.ts` only). Closes the fabricated-"Ce"-probe mis-syllabification bug flagged as open in the paragraph above, for its two most common rimes. `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE` now also excludes a base ending in `ook`/`ood`, the same shape as the existing `gn$` exclusion: `inflect()`'s -ed/-ing silent-e fallback was trying `predictInternal("booke", …)` for "book" (and "looke" for "look", "hoode" for "hood", …), and the fabricated probe resyllabifies as boo·ke, landing on the generic open `^(?:ui|oo|ue)` → u rule instead of the more specific closed `^ook`/`^ood` → ʊ rule the bare stem reaches — booking/looking/looked/overbooked/overbooking were shipping with /u/ instead of /ʊ/. None of the affected -ing/-ed forms are `dict.json` headwords, so the exceptions-table rule-diff comparison is a no-op (0 changed both ways) either way; the real evidence is runtime. `yarn test:common-accuracy`, word-diffed against a before-report: +3/−0 (looking, looked, booking newly correct, zero regressions), 94.58% → 94.64%. `yarn test:parity` (93.99%/99.86%), `yarn test:eval` (56.15%/77.66%), `yarn test:eval --subset common` (65.09%/84.16%, top-5000 67.18%/85.18%), `evaluate-strict.ts` headline (57.57%) and `yarn test:homographs` (87.62% micro) all held exactly flat, since none of the touched words reach any of those references either.

Two wider scopes were measured and rejected. Excluding every vowel-digraph-ending base (not just ook/ood — oo/ui/ue/ea/… generally) scores 18 : 68 over the dict: most magic-e words ending in those digraphs (rope, cake, ate, …) are genuine dropped-e spellings, unlike ook/ood, so the wide exclusion blocks the probe from ever correcting them. Adding `ead` specifically (to fix dreaded/treaded/spearheaded, the same bug's next-most-common rime) trades one strict win (unleaded) for a common-word regression: "leading" flips ˈɫidɪŋ → ˈɫɛdɪŋ, because "lead" alone — read through this same fallback with no homograph/POS context available — defaults to the metal's closed /lɛd/, not the verb's /lid/; measured strict 1 : 1, lenient 4 : 0, but common-subset strict 0 : 1 on the one metric this fix actually gates, so `ead` stays out and dreaded/treaded/spearheaded are still open. cooking and hooking are unaffected by this whole bug class, but only because "Cooke" and "Hooke" are `exceptions.json` table hits for those exact surname-spelled strings; run through the bare rule path (`disableDict: true`) they show the identical mis-syllabification, so the fix is runtime-only luck for those two words, not a rule that reaches them.

3+-syllable -ion penult stress pass (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, worktree agent-a4fd9bfccb786c1b3). A word-final -ion syllable that is NOT the -tion/-sion suffix (opinion, battalion, pavilion, dominion, communion, centurion, collodion, malathion) takes the penult at 3+ slots: 34 penult : 10 elsewhere over the dict (77.3%). At 2 slots the same ending goes the other way (union, onion, legion, scorpion, champion: 6 penult/initial : 57 final) and is excluded by the length check; the doubled-l subset of that 2-slot population (million, billion, stallion) is already handled by the existing `^lion$` SUFFIX_RULES entry, unaffected either way. `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s -s-plural rule-fallback regex (`src/en/g2p.ts`, the r/gn rule-exact-eviction guard) widens from `/(?:r|gn)$/` to `/(?:r|gn|ion)$/` so opinions/dominions/battalions still compose once their singular becomes rule-exact and leaves `exceptions.json` — the same eviction trap this file has hit repeatedly (see the dependency-aware eviction pass above). Rule-diff: strict 8 : 0, lenient 8 : 0, top-5000 1 : 0 (opinion). Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` flat 93.99%/99.86%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 94.64% (0/0 word-diff — this rule's population sits outside the top-5000); `yarn test:eval` strict 56.15% → 56.16%, lenient 77.66% → 77.67%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.09% → 65.12% (top-5000 67.18% → 67.21%), lenient 84.16% → 84.18% (top-5000 85.18% → 85.21%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.57% → 57.58%; `yarn test:homographs` flat 87.62%/87.75%.

Rejected in the same pass, per the source worktree's own report and re-measured by the integrator: a pro- lax-root carve-out (stressed word-initial "pro" tenses to /oʊ/ only before a light open syllable, else stays /ɑ/). Reproduced strict 17 : 9, top-5000 1 : 2, common-subset lenient −0.17 (top-5000) — the stressed open "pro" vowel is lexically split between /ɑ/ (problem, process) and /oʊ/ (provost, prozac) with no orthographic discriminator found. A separately reported 37 : 4 figure for the same carve-out did not reproduce and is not trusted.

ear + bound -l tail, ie + t/nt hiatus pass (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, worktree agent-a65c70b8ea9675ff8). Two independent fixes to `src/en/syllabify.ts`. (a) The cross-syllable `ear` → /ɝ/ boundary rule (searcher, earnest) re-admits an l-initial next syllable, previously excluded because a bare next-syllable-onset test can't tell a bound, unstressed continuation (earley, pearline) from a genuine free compound (yearlong, "year" + the free word "long"). Scoped by `tail` (every syllable after this one, joined) instead: the bound continuations spell one of a small closed set of shapes (-ey/-ie name suffix, -ine suffix, bare silent-e -le), which a genuine free compound tail never does — yearlong stays untouched. (b) The stressed monosyllabic `^ie$` hiatus rule (client, diet, quiet: syllabify can't split "ie" from what follows when nothing separates them) extends from its existing s/d/r codas to t/nt with a linking schwa (daɪət, kwaɪət, kɫaɪənt), excluding a doubled final consonant (gemination marks the vowel checked/short, same signal `MAGIC_E_CANDIDATE` reads elsewhere). This conflicted with the previous commit's -ion pass on the same `tryMorphologicalAnalysis` -s-plural rule-fallback regex in `src/en/g2p.ts` (both add an alternative to the r/gn eviction guard, once "client" itself goes rule-exact and is evicted from `exceptions.json`, "clients" needs the same stem-level rule-path fallback); resolved to `/(?:r|gn|ion|ient)$/`, keeping both sides' comments. Rule-diff: strict 8 : 1 (vliet, a Dutch surname element whose "ie" is the plain digraph, not a hiatus — no spelling signal separates it from diet/quiet), lenient 12 : 4, top-5000 3 : 0 (client, diet, quiet). Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` flat 93.99%/99.86%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 94.64%, word-diffed 0 newly wrong (client/diet/quiet fall outside the CMUdict top-5000 set this benchmark scores); `yarn test:eval` strict 56.16% → 56.17%, lenient 77.67% → 77.68%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.12% → 65.16% (top-5000 67.21% → 67.29%), lenient 84.18% → 84.21% (top-5000 85.21% → 85.26%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.58% → 57.59%; `yarn test:homographs` flat 87.62%/87.75%. Open, per the worktree's own note: early and pearly are split by the existing -ly handler as ear + ly / pear + ly, which would need full-lexicon existence at runtime to fix (not available by design); yearling's -le schwa is a dict-level split, not reached by this rule.

a + io/ium/ency hiatus tensing, -or laxing after l/m/n, -er th-base pass (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, worktree agent-abcde77a7344170a2). Three independent fixes. (a) `syllableToIPA` (`src/en/syllabify.ts`): a stressed open a immediately before a genuine Latin/Greek -ium/-io/-ius hiatus tail is now tense (radio, radius, stadium, valium, palladium, titanium, vanadium; contagion, savior, behavior), gated off t/s/c/x onsets on the hiatus syllable (already owned by the -tion/-sion/-ation family — measured separately at 474 : 6 already-correct, i.e. already-solved territory this rule doesn't need to touch) and off r/l/n onsets (the SQUARE-vowel -ario/-arious hiatus and the l/n-onset glide frame elsewhere in the file already own that population; the bare l/n-onset slice here is 4 tense : 10 lax, the opposite majority). (b) A stressed open a before an unstressed -ency/-ancy tail is also tense (agency, latency, vacancy, vagrancy, complacency): 5 tense : 0 lax, the whole a-initial-syllable population found — its own disjunct rather than an `A_TENSE_ENDINGS` entry, since the a need not be syllable 0 (fancy/dancer/cancer/lancer never reach here at all; their "a" is a closed "an"-syllable nucleus, a different frame). (c) The existing two-syllable -or tensing (favor, gator, jacor, major, razor, savor, tabor, vapor…) is wrong for a sonorant onset: l/m/n before -or is lax (amor, calor, falor, hamor, manor, valor), 7 lax : 11 tense over the dict, split cleanly by that one onset-manner feature. (d) `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s -er handler (`src/en/g2p.ts`) no longer tries a magic-e stem for a ≤4-letter base ending in "th": bather/lather/mather/rather/wither are unrelated dict headwords (surnames, "one who withers"), not genuine -the derivations the way breathe/breather is — there is no dict word for which the short-base -the reading is correct, and base.length ≤ 4 is exactly the bath/lath/math/rath/with population found (breath, at 6 letters, still gets the lookup it needs). Combined rule-diff over the whole dict: strict 27 : 1, lenient 30 : 11, top-5000 3 : 0 (agency, radio, rather). The one strict loss is cranor, a surname the dict keeps tense against the sonorant-lax -or majority — no spelling split found.

Rejected frames, measured and left out of the -io/-ium/-ency family: a + mb/mber (2 tense : 10 lax — chamber/amber/camber/timber majority lax); a + single consonant + ic (1 : 9 — attic/comic/panic/fabric majority lax); a + single consonant + on (13 tense : 23 lax — bacon/dragon/wagon majority lax, no split from apron/aeon); a + Cie (14 : 10, too close to call, no orthographic discriminator found). national/rational keep the dict's lax reading for lexical reasons, not a spelling-predictable class, and were left alone; have is a genuinely irregular word (hæv, not heɪv) with no productive frame around it, also left alone rather than special-cased.

The hiatusATense branch's own 10 lenient losses (none top-5000) are a third, unrelated vowel it doesn't touch: word-final "-Cio" Italian/Spanish surnames the dict writes with /ɑ/, not æ or eɪ (abio, akio, defabio, defazio, delgadio, fabio, fazio, flavio, savio, savion) — orthographically identical to radio's own "Ca·Cio" shape, so there is no spelling-only split between them; the old æ guess sat at Levenshtein 1 from /ɑ/ (lenient-correct), the new eɪ sits at 2 (lenient-wrong). Open: `scripts/foreign-filter.ts`'s `isForeign` heuristic has no rule for this Italian -adio/-azio/-avio/-abio surname-ending class, so these words are scored as English-subset failures by `yarn test:eval --subset common` instead of being excluded as foreign — the same kind of gap the Italian -ino/-ano/-ola surname-ending rules elsewhere in this file already live with.

Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` flat 93.99%/99.86%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 94.64%, word-diffed 0 newly wrong / 0 newly right (none of this pass's changed words fall in the CMUdict top-5000 set this benchmark scores, and none were already-wrong top-5000 words it could have fixed either); `yarn test:eval` strict 56.17% → 56.20%, lenient 77.68% → 77.70%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.16% → 65.25% (top-5000 67.29% → 67.38%), lenient 84.21% → 84.29% (top-5000 85.26% → 85.35%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.59% → 57.62%; `yarn test:homographs` flat 87.62%/87.75%. Code lines (block-comment-aware count, per the counting note above): g2p.ts 832, syllabify.ts 1293, postlex.ts 482 — total 2607, under the relative line-count trigger (next compression due above 2749 code lines); no compression run this pass.

-ply / dying-lying-tying-vying pass (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, worktree agent-a00440a34eb5b4aa9, narrowed on merge). Five related fixes, all downstream of one syllabification bug: `syllabify()`'s leftover-consonant merge step folds a bare-consonant final syllable into the previous one whenever every character in it is in `CONSONANTS` — and "y" is in `CONSONANTS` as well as `VOWELS`, so a syllable whose only nucleus is a syllable-final "y" (already consumed as that syllable's own vowel by the main loop) also matches and gets wrongly merged, collapsing re·ly → "rely" and ap·ply → "apply" into one syllable before `assignStress` ever sees two to choose between. (a) `syllabify()` now skips that merge when it would collapse a genuine two-syllable word to one AND the first syllable is a recognised weak Latin prefix (`KEEP_UNMERGED_AFTER_PREFIX = {com, de, re, un, ap, im, sup}`, module-scoped and exported); scoped to exactly that set because widening it to the rest of `PREFIXES_2SYL` (be-/dis-/ex-/ob-/pre-/pro-/sub-) regressed real or surname words that keep the OLD merged-blob reading (beryl, betsy, bevy all lost under "be-", with no compensating win — measured and rejected, not adopted). (b) `assignStress` excludes 4-slot -ience from the -ance/-ence rule (convenience, experience, obedience, impatience, resilience, ebullience, provenience fall through to the con-/ex-/ob- prefix loop below, which already stresses them right); -ience at exactly 3 slots (patience, science, conscience) is unaffected and stays on the -ance/-ence rule. (c) A 2-syllable word ending "ply" after ap-/com-/sup-/im- takes final stress (apply, comply, supply, imply) — checked ahead of the general com- laxRoot default; "re" is excluded from this specific check because reply already reaches final stress through an existing unstressed-prefix entry. (d) `syllableToIPA` skips the unstressed `^ly$` suffix rule (quickly, only) when the syllable is stressed, so a stressed "ly" syllable falls through to the general word-final-y handling and reads as tense /aɪ/ (rely, reply) instead of the suffix's lax /i/. (e) `inflect()` in `g2p.ts` restores "ie" for a 2-letter consonant+y base before -ing (dying, lying, tying, vying) — a shipped regression this package's own fix (a) exposed: "ly" used to be a table (exceptions.json) entry whenever rules-only mispredicted it, which `lex(base)` inside `inflect()` found and used; fix (d) makes "ly" itself rule-exact, evicting it from the table and silently breaking "lying" (the same rule-exact-eviction trap this file has hit repeatedly — see the dependency-aware eviction pass above).

The received package's -ly adverb-handler exclusion (`src/en/g2p.ts`) enumerated `^(?:ap|com|sup|im|re)ply$` — a literal alternation of exactly the 5 words apply/comply/supply/imply/reply, a whole-word list wearing a regex, which this project's rules forbid in code. Replaced with a structural composition: `KEEP_UNMERGED_AFTER_PREFIX` (fix (a) above, hoisted to module scope and exported from `syllabify.ts` for this reuse) tested as `<prefix> + "ply"`. Since "deply" and "unply" aren't English words, that composition names exactly the same 5 words the literal regex did — confirmed by a byte-identical rules-only dump before/after the substitution (`yarn rule-diff compare`: strict 16 : 4, lenient 13 : 4, top-5000/common strict 3 : 0, lenient 3 : 0, matching the received package's own self-reported numbers exactly) — so this is a reuse of an existing morpheme rule, not a new lookup table, per the -ation/-ity precedent of composing general prefix/suffix sets elsewhere in this file. A stem-plausibility alternative (skip -ly stripping when the remaining stem is a doubled consonant or an implausible final cluster) was measured and rejected first: it correctly flags "app"/"supp" as implausible, but "imp" and "rep" are themselves ordinary dict headwords, so no stem-shape test can tell imply/reply's fabricated stem apart from a genuine short word — only the prefix-set composition generalises cleanly.

Two more variants from the received package were measured and rejected, not adopted (recorded here per the user's instruction to keep this evidence, not just the winning rule). A blanket "don't merge when it would collapse to one syllable" fix, with no prefix restriction at all, scored 148 : 240 on the whole dict — a net loss, because ordinary bisyllabic -Cy nouns of the identical orthographic shape (baby, lady, copy) are lexically split on the open-vowel tense/lax question with no spelling signal, and the OLD merged-blob path happens to already read that ambiguous population right via its own per-letter rules; unmerging it for no reason loses that accidental correctness. A position-aware version of fix (d)'s guard (skip the lax `^ly$`/`^y$` default when the syllable is stressed OR word-initial, not just when the whole -ply word is in the narrow prefix set) correctly separates more cases on its own rule-diff (rely/apply vs comfy/proxy, dynamic/hygiene) but touches far more "Cy"-shaped syllables dict-wide than that count shows, and destabilised `scripts/mine-exceptions.ts`'s dependency-aware eviction pass: re-pinned stems dropped from ~400 to ~15 on a full `yarn build-dict`, regressing 14 top-5000 words (programs, marketing, table and others — none of them Cy-shaped, purely collateral from the eviction miner's own net-check landing differently over a much smaller candidate population once so many more words became rule-exact at once). **General caution, generalised into the traps section below**: a broad rule change can shift the eviction miner's equilibrium by itself, independent of whether the rule change is otherwise correct — check the re-pin count and the runtime `test:common-accuracy` word-diff on every package, not just its own rules-only win/loss count. Open, not reinvestigated further here.

Confirming the adopted package does NOT trip that same caution: `yarn build-dict` re-pinned 399 → 403 stems (resolved 430 → 434, regressed 6 → 6, the exact same six words kept net-positive both before and after — caterings, experimenter, fared, impacting, probation, segmenting), a normal-sized shift for a package that makes 39 dict words newly rule-exact, not a collapse. Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` flat 93.99%/99.86%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 94.64%, word-diffed 0 newly wrong / 0 newly right; `yarn test:common-accuracy --rules` 71.88% → 71.94% (this row's own recorded 71.82% had again lagged by one commit — a fresh stash/rebuild/remeasure cycle confirmed the true pre-patch value before comparing; word-diff +3/−0: apply, reply, supply); `yarn test:eval` strict 56.20% → 56.21%, lenient 77.70% → 77.71%; `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.25% → 65.35% (top-5000 67.38% → 67.46%), lenient 84.29% → 84.41% (top-5000 85.35% → 85.43%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.62% → 57.63%; `yarn test:homographs` flat 87.62%/87.75%. Accepted losses (4 strict, from fix (a)'s merge-avoidance, not the -ply exclusion): comby, dery, deryck, remy — surname false positives no spelling signal separates from decry/deny/defy/reply/retry. Code lines: g2p.ts 838, syllabify.ts 1309, postlex.ts 482 — total 2629, under the relative line-count trigger (next compression due above 2749); no compression run this pass.

-our and -Cre rhotic rimes pass (merged 2026-09-28, integrator, worktree agent-a617b730c02085cf8, narrowed on merge). Three independent fixes. (b) `PHONEME_RULES` gains a bare word-final `^our$` → /aʊɝ/ (flour, hour, our, devour, dour, scour, sour: 9 ɝ : 0 ɹ in the dict, excluding the existing `^our(?=[cst])` environment), guarded in `syllableToIPA`'s skip logic to fire only when stressed AND word-final — unguarded it also promoted the unstressed British -our = American -or agentive suffix (arbour, armour, barbour, clamour, glamour, harbour) from its correctly-reduced schwa+r to a wrongly restressed diphthong. Rejected: -ire has no orthographic split (19 tense-restressed wins : 48 losses — mire/admire/spire/inspire are one shape, lexically mixed, no rule reaches it) and is not attempted. -our itself stays a lexical split past the guard: four/pour/tour/your/cour/amour/detour spell ɔɹ/ʊɹ with no onset-consonant discriminator from the ɝ-taking set (f/p/t/k appear on both sides) — those stay table hits. (c) A silent-e "-re" ending (the French/British spelling of -er: acre, centre, fibre, theatre, massacre) reads as its onset consonant + /ɝ/, scoped to the three onsets with common-word support (`^cre$`/`^tre$`/`^bre$` in `PHONEME_RULES`, plus matching entries in `endsWithSilentE`'s exclusion and `syllableToIPA`'s skip logic so the rule only fires syllable-finally — mid-word "cre"/"tre"/"bre" is a genuine pronounced open syllable, secretary/accretion, not this silent-e rime). `stemPron`'s rule-fallback branch in `tryMorphologicalAnalysis` is gated by a new `FRENCH_RE_STEM_RE` (`/[cbt]re$/`) so a suffix-stripped fabricated stem ending in that shape (increment − ment → "incre", creal − al → "cre", mccreless − less → "mccre" — none of them English words) doesn't get read through the newly-added word-final rule as if it were a genuine "-cre"/"-tre"/"-bre" ending; the one accepted strict loss, mccreless, is a proper name this guard correctly declines to read as a French silent-e word but the whole-word fallback then mis-syllabifies anyway — a table hit at runtime either way. French names whose "re" is genuinely pronounced (maitre, lemaitre's own stem "maitre") are not reached by this rule at any point in the pipeline and stay lexical, confirmed unfixable by spelling alone — no rule distinguishes a pronounced vs silent French "re" from the Latin alphabet alone. (d) `inflect()`'s -ed/-ing silent-e fallback and the -s-plural rule-derived-stem fallback both exempt a stem ending in /ɝ/ from their "must stay consonant-final" check (configured, disfigured, measured, gestured, fissured all restore their own -ure stem's /j/-glide or /ʃ ʒ tʃ dʒ/ palatalization, which the bare no-e stem doesn't carry), scoped to a stem under 4 syllables (`syllabify(base).length < 4`): caricature/caricatured/caricatures is a genuine, isolated lexical exception where CMUdict agrees with ipa-dict that the verb shifts its primary stress to the 2nd syllable, unlike every other -ture verb measured (capture, picture, feature, fracture, gesture all keep 1st-syllable stress in CMUdict) — the bare-stem fallback's own stress-assignment quirk at exactly 4 syllables happens to already reproduce that CMUdict-confirmed shift, so the ɝ exemption is withheld there rather than overriding a rule that's accidentally already right; unscoped vs 4+-syllable-scoped was measured directly (unscoped strict 16 : 1, losing caricatured; naively scoped by "ends in tur" strict 14 : 0 but also loses gestured/gesturing, 2-syllable words wrongly caught by that looser test) — the syllable-count cut on the base keeps both wins. Combined rule-diff over the whole dict: strict 43 : 1 (mccreless), lenient 16 : 17 — the full-dict raw lenient correct COUNT stays exactly flat (81645 → 81645 under `yarn test:eval`, not just close in rounded percent) even though rule-diff's own narrower canon-based lenient metric shows non-zero win/loss on both sides, top-5000/common strict 3 : 0 (centre, measured, our), lenient 1 : 1 (net zero).

This package's own g2p.ts diff landed on the same three call sites the -ply / dying-lying-tying-vying package above had just changed (the import line, the -ed/-ing silent-e fallback, and the -s-plural rule-fallback), and syllabify.ts's `skip.add` block sat immediately after the exact comment block that package's own rejected-variant note occupied (removed when that note was folded into the traps section). `git apply --reject` cleanly took every hunk except the one `skip.add` block; that one hunk was reapplied by hand from the `.rej` file with no changes to its content, keeping both packages' additions. `yarn build-dict` re-pinned 403 → 403 stems (resolved 434 → 434, regressed 6 → 6, the same six words), confirming this package alone doesn't move the eviction miner's equilibrium either — the caution recorded in the traps section below is about a DIFFERENT, rejected variant from the previous package, not this one. Gates: `yarn test` 1117 pass, `yarn typecheck` clean; `yarn test:parity` flat 93.99%/99.86%; `yarn test:common-accuracy` flat 94.64%, word-diffed 0 newly wrong / 0 newly right; `yarn test:common-accuracy --rules` 71.94% → 71.98% (word-diff +2/−0: centre, measured); `yarn test:eval` strict 56.21% → 56.25%, lenient flat 77.71% (81645/105067 both before and after); `yarn test:eval --subset common` strict 65.35% → 65.48% (top-5000 67.46% → 67.54%), lenient 84.41% → 84.42% (top-5000 flat 85.43%); `evaluate-strict.ts` headline 57.63% → 57.68%; `yarn test:homographs` flat 87.62%/87.75%. Four new `PHONEME_RULES` entries this session (`^our$`, `^cre$`, `^tre$`, `^bre$`) trip the session-growth compression trigger (≥ 3 entries added in one session); a compression pass follows separately, not run here. Code lines: g2p.ts 840, syllabify.ts 1319, postlex.ts 482 — total 2641, still under the relative line-count trigger (next compression due above 2749) by line count alone, but the session-growth trigger fires independently of line count.

Bound-morph table adopted as a behaviour-neutral refactor (2026-09-30, user decision, source worktree `.claude/worktrees/agent-a4f5955c042f258a0`, experiment "E3"). `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`'s generic suffix loop in `g2p.ts` used to carry 15 stress-neutral-ish rows inline (`ify`/`tual`/`tuous`/`ulation`/`ulator`/`ulate`/`ular`/`ment`/`ness`/`less`/`ful`/`ize`/`ist`/`ism`/`al`). 13 of them are genuinely stress-neutral — Church (1986)'s "#"-boundary class, where the stem keeps whatever stress it already has and the suffix just concatenates on. `-al` and `-ular` are NOT in that class: `-ular` forces its own stress onto the bound root's last syllable (`molecular`, `particular` — see the `-ular` pass note above) and `-al` needs doubled-consonant undo, magic-e stem recovery, and (for `-ial`) its own stress shift, none of which the table's plain "look up the stem, concatenate the suffix" model can express — so those two stay in a (now much shorter) loop in `g2p.ts`, and only the 13 truly stress-neutral rows moved into a new declarative table, `src/en/morph-table.ts` (`STRESS_NEUTRAL_ROWS`), applied by one shared composition function in a new module, `src/en/morph-parser.ts` (`tryStressNeutralSuffix`). The source worktree measured what bypassing each handler entirely (falling straight to the whole-word rule path) would cost dict-wide, confirming the existing handler logic — not just its data shape — is worth keeping: `-ment` 12:119, `-ness` 6:173, `-less` 0:64, `-ful` 4:41, `-ist` 23:94, `-ism` 2:150, `-ize` 29:72 against bypassing; `-cial`/`-tial` 10:8 and `-ual` 5:2 only marginal; the `-ed` /ɪd/→/əd/ allomorph shortcut was measured again and rejected again, 204:524. None of that logic changed — this refactor only moves the 13 rows' existing behaviour into a new home.

The worktree's own patch kept both mechanisms alive behind a constructor switch (`useMorphTable`, default `false`) so the two could be measured side by side; per the user's instruction, the switch was NOT shipped — dead flexibility this project's own rules forbid. The integrator removed it entirely: `tryStressNeutralSuffix` runs unconditionally, and the 13 corresponding rows were deleted from `g2p.ts`'s loop (which now holds only `["ular", "jəlɝ"]` and `["al", "əl"]`). The call site sits exactly where the worktree placed it — after the existing `-ual` block, before the trimmed loop — which is load-bearing, not cosmetic: `tual` is a substring-suffix of `al` (a word ending in `-tual` also, trivially, ends in `-al`), so the ORIGINAL 15-row loop only ever reached its `al` branch for a `-tual` word when the `tual` row's own stem lookup had already failed earlier in the same iteration. Calling `tryStressNeutralSuffix` (whose 13 rows run `tual` before returning) before the surviving `al`-only fallback reproduces that exact fall-through order; moving the call earlier (e.g. ahead of the `-ual` block) or later would not. `main` was already at the same commit (`fa54be4`) the worktree branched from, so this was a direct application, not a rebase across diverged history, despite the brief's expectation that it might need one.

Two further simplifications the integrator made beyond the worktree's own patch, both confirmed dead/duplicate rather than assumed: (1) the worktree's `tryStressNeutralSuffix` took a `{ lex, predictStem }` helpers object and re-wrapped `predictStem`'s call in its own `lex(stem) ?? (FRENCH_RE_STEM_RE.test(stem) ? undefined : predictStem(stem))` — but `predictStem` (g2p.ts's `stemPron`) already does exactly `lex(b) || (FRENCH_RE_STEM_RE.test(b) ? undefined : predictInternal(b, ...))` internally, so the parser's outer `lex` call and its own copy of `FRENCH_RE_STEM_RE` were provably redundant (the algebra: if `lex(stem)` fails once, calling it again returns the same failure, and re-testing the same regex against the same string returns the same result); collapsed to a single `predictStem` function parameter, no interface, no dead regex. (2) The worktree's `morph-parser.ts` imported `softenBaseFinal`/`preSuffixReduce` from `g2p.ts`, which itself now imports `tryStressNeutralSuffix` from `morph-parser.ts` — a real import cycle (workable at runtime here, since both bindings are only referenced inside function bodies invoked after module load, but worth not shipping). Both functions (and the `PRE_SUFFIX_ORTHO_RE` constant) moved into `morph-parser.ts`, exported from there, with `g2p.ts`'s surviving `-al`/`-ular` loop importing them from their new home instead — `g2p.ts → morph-parser.ts → morph-table.ts`, no cycle.
