Two more artifacts from the same worktree session, transcribed here since they inform the open list below rather than because they changed any code. `scripts/.tmp-classify-453.ts` (preserved, with its output `.classify-453.json`, in `scratchpad/experiments-2026-09-30/e3-morphtable-evidence/`) classifies the 453 common-subset words where the morphology route's rules-only prediction disagrees with the dict, using a crude longest-match suffix strip (not the real handler dispatch) purely to bucket the mismatch's likely cause. Its five buckets, counted directly from the script's own JSON output (453 total): **A_convention** 105 (23.2%) — the stress-stripped gap closes under a known unstressed-vowel swap (ɪ↔ə, ɪ↔i), the dict-convention split this file's own "Three traps" section already documents as unwinnable by rule; **B_stemError** 107 (23.6%) — the approximate stem IS a dict headword and the RULE's own prediction for that bare stem already disagrees with the dict, i.e. decomposition is right and the bug is upstream in the stem's own stress rule (often the 2-syllable prefix-stress class this file has repeatedly measured and left open — property/process/reference/yesterday/percent and the rest of that list); **C_falseParse** 162 (35.8%) — the approximate stem is NOT a dict headword at all, i.e. the crude classifier's suffix list matched a string that was never actually derived that way (this is the classifier's own false-positive bucket, not a count of real handler mistakes, and is the largest of the five — not the smaller "~25" this refactor's brief estimated, which doesn't reconcile with the script's own counts and is superseded here); **D_suffixGap** 63 (13.9%) — decomposition AND the stem's own pronunciation both check out, so the mismatch is purely in suffix composition (allomorph, stress class, glide/palatalization) — the script's own comment calls this "the table's real target population," and it's the one bucket this refactor's table can plausibly narrow, not close (moving the 13 rows to a table changed where the composition logic lives, not what it computes); **E_noStem** 16 (3.5%) — the crude suffix strip found no candidate stem at all. None of this changed any code; it's diagnostic context for a future pass on bucket D. Separately, and unaffected by this refactor (present identically before and after — the snapshot/trace gates above confirm it moved with the code, unchanged): a suffix handler's stem lookup has no POS to pass, so a heteronym stem defaults to whichever reading its own no-context prediction picks, regardless of what the suffix implies about part of speech. Confirmed directly: rules-only (`disableDict: true`), `used` → `ˈʌst` and `closed` → `ˈkɫoʊst` (both wrong; the dict-enabled path gets both right, `ˈjuzd`/`ˈkɫoʊzd`, from table hits that mask the bug at runtime today, the same "masked by the table until a stem goes rule-exact" shape as several evictions documented earlier in this file).

Two rejected experiments from the same 2026-09-30 session, run in separate worktrees, scripts preserved in `scratchpad/experiments-2026-09-30/` (not the repo) rather than wired into `src/`.

E1 — per-grapheme CART trained on the common population (worktree `agent-ae370bcb0686a5193`, plus a companion TBL trial sharing machinery with locked worktree `agent-aea163c87c485740f`). A CART tree using the DP grapheme↔phoneme alignment and atom vocabulary of `scripts/align.ts`, trained on frequency.txt ∩ !isForeign and scored by 10-fold CV: strict 45.8%, versus existing rules' 66.1% on the same population. Full-pipeline integration also failed: common-subset rules-only strict 49.6%, full-dict 37.2%. The trial used local atom contexts, predicted stress/position flags, and a ≥3 top-5000 TRAIN-word pruning rule. No feature-window or pruning ablation isolated why it failed; this is evidence against that configuration, not evidence that automatic rule learning or morphology features cannot work.

The companion common-population TBL selected 174 transformations **summed across ten folds**, not 174 distinct rules. Its scope filter retained 5659/6990 words (80.96%) when a separately rendered pure-rule output equalled predict(). Before the extra filter, pooled out-of-fold strict correctness fell 3672 → 3668/5659 (−4); none passed the extra gram-style filter. That filter required ≥20 changed TRAIN words, ≥75% of those words becoming fully correct, ≥3 top-5000 fixes and no top-5000 breaks/edit-distance increases. “75% of changed words fully fixed” differs from the gram table's modal-agreement test: a valid segment correction can leave another error in the same word. Also, the old top-5000 counts included HELD words, so the filter-passed score cannot be treated as untouched held-out evidence. The scope equality test can admit morphology/compound output that merely coincides with pure-rule output; changing the replica does not establish that the real dispatch changes. Neither mechanism adopted; see the 2026-10-03 actual-runtime replay experiment below for the corrected research entry point.

E2 — unified decomposition scorer (worktree `agent-a2100203609d23b59`). Intended to replace `tryDecomposition`'s ad hoc "fewest, longest chunks + a hand-tuned minimum-chunk-length patch" heuristic (the `fund+amen+tal` fix noted above) with one score per candidate segmentation: a Koehn & Knight (EACL 2003)-style geometric-mean part-weight term (mined from a real frequency list restricted to runtime-verifiable parts), a continuous length penalty in place of the hard 4-letter cliff, and a letter-bigram boundary-plausibility term. Measured against an oracle and a pre-change baseline: the common-subset gain was real but small (+0.17), while the full-dict rules-only strict score fell by 82 words and 9 pinned tests broke. The scorer is built to rank candidate splits against each other; it isn't built to decide the prior, more important question of whether the whole undecomposed word is itself the right answer, so it reaches for a plausible-looking split on ordinary dict words with no genuine internal boundary (e.g. compress, dogmatic) that the existing heuristic already leaves alone. It also has a narrow ceiling regardless: `tryDecomposition` itself is reached by only 1 of 6,990 common-subset words, so a strictly better scorer inside it cannot move that population much. Not adopted.

Explicit verb-reading stems in -ed/-ing (2026-10-03, continuation of Claude session `27f5aca7-e309-490a-a5e4-e3374e337441`). Adopted only the inflection fix from the interrupted POS experiment, narrowed to homograph entries explicitly tagged `V`. Every lexical probe in `inflect` (restored silent e, unchanged base, undoubled base) now tries that verb reading before its existing lookup. This fixes used/using (the fabricated base `us` previously won when `use` was rule-exact), closed/closing, living, and housing. A negative `!N` entry is insufficient evidence of a verb; ignoring that broader match preserves overrunning's citation stress and removes the original experiment's one-word parity regression. No changes to `matchPos`, bare-word lookup, the -s handler, or pronunciation data. The worktree's separate `disableDict`-only bare-word homograph default was rejected: it improved the rules-only score by reading a whole-word table without changing runtime behavior. Its changed invite expectation was not adopted. This does not reject invite's noun reading: /ˈɪnvaɪt/ (noun) and /ɪnˈvaɪt/ (verb) are both valid (Cambridge: https://dictionary.cambridge.org/pronunciation/english/invite). Existing runtime context selects both; regression tests now cover “We will invite them” and “I received an invite”. A single bare-word reference assesses a default choice, not whether the other POS reading exists. `disableDict` already allows lexical stem lookup through morphology, so it is a rule-path diagnostic rather than a fully data-free system; adding a whole-word homograph lookup only to that path would further obscure the measurement without improving shipped behavior.

### Three traps in the measurement itself

**Ranking error classes by single-edit signature overstates the opportunity.** The diagnosis recipe above counts the words a flip would fix but not the already-exact words it would break. Measured properly, the biggest-looking class in the benchmark — unstressed /ɪ/ where the dict has /ə/ — is not winnable by more ɪ/ə rules: `-ed` after t/d would fix 225 and break 473, final `-in` would fix 51 and break 1018, `-ical` would fix 0 and break 94. The dict is genuinely split on all of them and the rules already sit on the majority. Always simulate a candidate flip in both directions before briefing work on a class.

**A rules-only score is not independent of the mined table**, and the two interact in a way that can hide a regression: the morphology handlers look their stems up in the table even under `disableDict`, so improving a rule can evict a stem and change an unrelated derived word. That is why `scripts/mine-exceptions.ts` now evicts only on an exact stress-stripped match; the earlier canon-based test collapsed i/ɪ, ɑ/ɔ, ə/ʌ, ɛ/eɪ and æ/eɪ, so better rules quietly shipped the collapsed reading. Restoring the strict test was worth +1.11 parity and +0.80 on the top-5000 benchmark by itself. A 2026-09-28 pass (`scripts/mine-exceptions.ts`'s dependency-aware eviction, described above) turns this from a trap to keep discovering by hand into a mechanism: it measures which evicted stems a `disableDict:true` prediction actually depends on and re-pins only those, so the exact interaction this trap warns about is now found and fixed empirically across the whole dict at once, rather than one hand-patched word at a time after a bug report.

**A broad rule change can shift the dependency-aware eviction miner's own equilibrium, independent of whether the rule itself is correct** (found 2026-09-28, in a rejected variant of the -ply / dying-lying-tying-vying pass above — see that entry for the full numbers). A position-aware widening of a lax-vowel-suffix guard scored fine on its own rules-only win/loss count, but by making far more words rule-exact at once than a narrowly-scoped rule would, it changed which stems the eviction miner's net-check re-pins: re-pinned entries collapsed from ~400 to ~15 on a full `yarn build-dict`, regressing 14 unrelated top-5000 words that had nothing to do with the rule's own target population. A rule-diff win/loss count alone cannot see this, because it never runs `build-dict`. Check the re-pinned-stem count (`yarn build-dict`'s own "Dependency-aware eviction summary" output) and the runtime `yarn test:common-accuracy` word-diff on every package that touches `PHONEME_RULES`/`SUFFIX_RULES`/`assignStress`/`syllableToIPA` broadly, not just its own rules-only win/loss count — a re-pin count that moves by an order of magnitude from the pre-change baseline is itself a signal to investigate, even when the accuracy numbers look fine.

### The STRUT convention, and why parity has a ceiling

`normalizeStrut` in `src/en/phonotactics.ts` promotes a stressed /ə/ to /ʌ/. Removing it measures **+2823/−55 on the dictionary path and takes `yarn test:parity` to 95.10%** — the single largest parity number available anywhere in this codebase. Do not take it.

The promotion is an adapter between two references that disagree. `data/en/dict.json` is built from open-dict-data ipa-dict `en_US`, which writes STRUT as /ə/ even under stress: **zero /ʌ/ in 100,871 entries**. CMUdict, which `yarn test:common-accuracy` scores against, maps AH1/AH2 to /ʌ/ and AH0 to /ə/, and so does every mainstream American IPA transcription — *cut* is /kʌt/, not /kət/. Deleting the promotion would raise parity by making the output match a source with a known transcription quirk, while dropping top-5000 segment accuracy from 92.26% to 89.62% and shipping less standard pronunciations. 136 of the remaining top-5000 mismatches differ from CMUdict by ə/ʌ alone.

So parity is capped by that convention gap, and a parity number above roughly 93% should be read as suspicion that something re-adopted the base lexicon's convention rather than as progress. The same reasoning rejects skipping `applyPhonotactics` on verbatim lexical hits: it would remove ~2500 table rewrites at a stroke, but at the cost of freezing ipa-dict's conventions into shipped output and making dictionary words and rule-derived words disagree with each other.

## Commands

- `yarn build` — compiles dictionaries (via `prebuild` → `build-dict`) then rollup-bundles all entry points to `dist/`
- `yarn build-dict` — regenerates `data/**/*.json` from `src-data/` sources (run manually after editing `src-data/`)
- `yarn test` — Jest, picks up `__tests__/**/*.test.ts`. Single test: `yarn test -- en-rule-g2p`
- `yarn test:coverage` — Jest with nyc coverage
- `yarn typecheck` — `tsc -b` (no emit)
- `yarn test:eval` — `scripts/evaluate.ts`: Levenshtein distance of rule-based G2P vs. dictionary. `--subset common` (also `yarn test:eval:common`) scores only the English subset (frequency-list, non-foreign words); needs the `yarn test:common-accuracy --download` cache first
- `yarn rule-diff dump <out>` / `yarn rule-diff compare <before> <after>` — `scripts/rule-diff.ts`: rules-only per-word dump and win/loss report (strict, lenient, top-5000) between two dumps; the adoption test for a rule change
- `yarn test:parity` — `scripts/evaluate-parity.ts`: shipped pipeline (dict enabled) vs. dictionary; `--dump <file>` writes per-word output for diffing two states
- `yarn test:homographs` — `scripts/evaluate-homographs.ts`: heteronym reading in context against WikipediaHomographData (shipped pipeline). `--split train` to diagnose, `--update-baseline` after a confirmed improvement; exits non-zero when eval accuracy drops. First run needs `--download`
- `yarn test:common-accuracy` — `scripts/evaluate-common-accuracy.ts`: top-5000 frequency words vs. CMUdict through the public API (`--rules` for rules-only). First run needs `--download` to fetch the pinned, hash-checked inputs into `scripts/.common-accuracy-cache/`
- `yarn test:ai-eval` — `scripts/eval-with-ai.ts`: AI-scored eval over `scripts/eval-data/*.txt`. Flags: `--provider codex|openai`, `--model <name>`, `--lang <codes>`. Codex provider shells out to the `codex` CLI (no API key needed); openai provider needs `OPENAI_API_KEY`.

## Architecture

### Pipeline (`src/tokenizer.ts`)

`phonemize(text)` flow:

1. `analyzeText(text)` (`src/g2p.ts`) computes the document's primary language and whether Han chars should route to `ja` or `zh`.
2. `preProcessByScript()` splits text into script-based runs (CJK Han / kana / Hangul / Cyrillic / Latin), absorbing neutrals (digits, punct, whitespace) into adjacent runs, then runs each through its `LanguageProcessor.preProcess` (number / abbreviation / currency expansion). Pure-neutral runs fall back to the user's `language` option, then to the detected primary.
3. Optional `anyAscii` Latinization (preserves Han for G2P).
4. Regex tokenize → per-token G2P dispatch → format conversion (IPA / ARPABET / Zhuyin).

### Language registry (`src/g2p.ts`)

All language plugins implement `LanguageProcessor` (`id`, `supportedLanguages`, optional `preProcess(text)`, `predict(word, lang?, pos?)`, `addPronunciation(word, ipa)`). `LanguageRegistry` resolves a request via BCP 47 fallback: exact dialect (`en-GB`) beats parent (`en`), most-specific prefix wins among parents. Scripts that unambiguously identify a language (Han → zh, Hangul → ko, Cyrillic → ru, …) are routed by `detectLanguage()` / `analyzeText()` regardless of the user-supplied `language` option.

### CJK Han disambiguation (zh vs ja)

`analyzeText()` flips Han routing to Japanese when the text has **two or more separate hiragana clusters** — the structural signature of Japanese prose. Single isolated kana (Taiwan-style `植物の優`, `我推薦東京のラーメン`) stays on the Chinese path. The heuristic is empirically motivated by the dominance of decorative single-`の` borrowing in Taiwan Mandarin; see the comment block at `analyzeText` for full rationale and trade-offs.

### Single-instance vs. multi-instance

- `src/index.ts` exports the public API bound to a **global** registry. `src/all.ts` / `src/zh.ts` re-export the same surface but pre-register additional language processors as side effects of import.
- `createPhonemizer({ processors })` (`src/core.ts`) creates an isolated `Phonemizer` with its own `LanguageRegistry`. Use this whenever multiple language configurations must coexist (the test suite and `scripts/eval-with-ai.ts` use it).

### Build layout

Package entry points are declared in `package.json#exports` and rollup builds one bundle per entry (`index`, `core`, `zh`, `all`, `en-g2p`, `zh-g2p`, `ja-g2p`, `ko-g2p`, `ru-g2p`). Each `*-g2p` entry can be imported standalone so consumers only pay for the languages they need.

Rollup's `externalDataPlugin` (`rollup.config.mjs`) keeps `data/**/*.json` as separate files in `dist/` rather than inlining them — required because Hermes (React Native) can't bytecode-compile a 2.8 MB JS object literal. **Don't change this without considering RN consumers.**

### Dictionary sources

- `src-data/` — hand-edited sources (`en/custom.dict`, `en-gb/lexical.json`, `zh/dict.json5`, …)
- `data/` — generated, committed JSON consumed at runtime
- Run `yarn build-dict` after editing `src-data/`. `prebuild` does it automatically.

### Source layout (by language)

Language-specific modules live under `src/<lang>/` (`src/en/`, `src/zh/`, `src/ja/`, `src/ko/`, `src/ru/`) — e.g. `src/en/g2p.ts`, `src/en/syllabify.ts`, `src/zh/g2p.ts`, `src/<lang>/expand.ts`. Shared/cross-language code stays flat in `src/` (`g2p.ts` registry, `tokenizer.ts`, `core.ts`, `index.ts`, `all.ts`, `zh.ts`, `utils.ts`, `consts.ts`, `anyascii.ts`). The public `phonemize/<lang>-g2p` export names are unchanged — rollup maps each `<lang>-g2p` entry to `src/<lang>/g2p.ts`.

### English dialect handling (`src/en/gb.ts`)

en-GB is *not* a separate dictionary — it's a rule-based post-processor over the AmE base (non-rhotic conversion, NURSE split, SQUARE/NEAR/CURE diphthongs, word-level overrides). Adding `en-GB` pronunciations means extending the rules or `src-data/en-gb/lexical.json`, not duplicating the AmE dict.

### Text expansion (`src/expand-*.ts`)

Each language has its own expander module that `LanguageProcessor.preProcess` calls:

- `src/en/expand.ts` — numbers, abbreviations, currency, dates, times, ordinals, phone numbers.
- `src/zh/expand.ts` — positional Chinese cardinals with 零 fill, `年` digit-by-digit, `点/分` time, currency (¥/$), percent, decimal, `第N` ordinal.
- `src/ja/expand.ts` — positional hiragana, no rendaku (the ja G2P syllable map lacks the palatal voiced rows).
- `src/ko/expand.ts` — Sino-Korean Hangul positional.
- `src/ru/expand.ts` — positional Cyrillic with feminine forms for тысяча and 1 / 2-4 / 5+ plural agreement on тысяча / миллион / процент / рубль / доллар.

## Conventions

From `.cursor/rules/`:

- **`src/<lang>/g2p.ts` files are rule-based.** Don't add word lists or per-word special cases — that defeats the point of rule-based G2P. Adjust general rules instead. Debug a G2P module with `new EnglishG2P({ disableDict: true })` to see what the rules alone produce.
- **`src/en/pos-tagger.ts`** — keep the algorithm general, no per-word lookup tables.
- **Tests** — don't tweak tests to pass. If an expected IPA value looks wrong, confirm with the user before changing it.
- Don't leave dead comments or vestigial explanations in code.


## Batch rule-learning research (2026-10-03)

User priority: grow general rules efficiently and continuously, rather than dispatch one agent per word/class. `scripts/learn-rule-corrections.ts` (`yarn learn:rules`) is the reusable offline entry point adapted from the interrupted common-population TBL trial. It generates many conditional corrections from errors, scores both repaired and broken words, greedily ranks TRAIN net word fixes with a fixed cap, updates overlapping candidate scores, and emits the ordered proposals and evidence. `--structural` adds syllable count, slot-from-end, weight, existing suffix/stress class and prefix contexts, including two-condition templates; these are spelling features, not gold morphology. Training may use existing pronunciation references offline; output is reviewable conditional rules, not a new runtime whole-word table or opaque model. Brill's initial-policy + conditional-corrections framework is the methodological reference (https://aclanthology.org/J95-4004/); Bouma's finite-state + TBL Dutch G2P study is a relevant precedent (https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0003074), not an English accuracy forecast.

Compared with the abandoned trial: attested spelling relatives are connected into components that stay in one fold (heuristic morphology, not a gold family annotation); selection and stopping never use HELD scores; top-5000 legacy-filter diagnostics now use TRAIN only; the full ordered candidate sequences and both fixed/broken examples are emitted. Counterfactual outputs run through the actual `predict()` method on an isolated lab instance. A temporary `ruleSyllables` hook substitutes the candidate syllable IPA and is restored in `finally`; the duplicate postlex/stress/phonotactics tail was removed. The hook is private research instrumentation, not a new public or runtime extension API. Data/baseline/tool hashes are included in new reports. Default filters match `evaluate.ts` (including <=12 letters); `--include-long` removes that word-length restriction for research without changing the project's baseline definition.

A controlled ten-fold comparison on the current `a332c36` runtime/data, seed 20260928, min-support 5, cap 30 rules/fold: 6990 common words; 5148 words reach the real own-word rule renderer and reconstruct their actual prediction exactly (73.65% coverage). Pooled out-of-fold strict correctness, each covered word scored once: baseline 3241/5148 (62.9565%); local templates 3244 (+3, 63.0148%); structural templates 3243 (+2, 62.9953%). Local selected 179 transformations summed across folds / 36 distinct signatures; structural 241 across folds / 54 distinct. Both have mixed positive and negative fold deltas; structural is not better than local, and neither tiny positive total establishes a reliable production improvement. No candidate adopted, no runtime or score baseline changed. These are scope-restricted rule-path CV scores, not runtime parity, independent CMUdict accuracy, or the whole English-subset score. Candidate dispatch after exception-table rebuilding and effects on derived words still require the normal adoption gates.

The equality-only scope filter was materially misleading: on current data it admitted 5669 words, 521 more than actual renderer reachability; 510 of those trace as morphology, and 11 trace as rules despite bypassing this renderer (trace itself omits a compound branch). Thus matching baseline strings does not establish that a pure-rule intervention affects the real route. A preliminary replica comparison gave −23/−9 out-of-fold word deltas on those 5669 words; its population and folds differ from the final actual-replay experiment, so the sign change is not an isolated causal ablation. Do not cite it as a validated pronunciation-rule effect.

Evidence: `/tmp/phonemize-rule-growth/replay-local/` and `replay-structural/` contain the full CV reports and family assignments; `local/` and `structural/` retain the preliminary replica reports. Rerun from the repository with `yarn learn:rules --out /tmp/phonemize-rules-local` and `yarn learn:rules --structural --out /tmp/phonemize-rules-structural` (or `node --import tsx scripts/learn-rule-corrections.ts ...` when tsx CLI IPC is sandbox-restricted). Both actual-replay runs passed ten-fold execution; explicit script TypeScript strict checking passed; default/long-word bounded smoke runs passed. Invite and inflection regression suites: 128/128 tests.

The scalable improvement goal remains open. This learner proposes local IPA corrections and evaluates the direct rule route; it does not learn actual morphology dispatch, decomposition-versus-unsplit decisions, suffix composition or contextual POS/sense. Next experiments must observe those real decisions and replay competing alternatives through the complete pipeline, including the unsplit alternative, rather than add another local window or infer a handler from a suffix-looking spelling. Existing full-dict/parity/CMUdict/homograph gates stay intact; use homograph TRAIN to diagnose context changes and keep eval untouched. Do not turn these exploratory CV folds into a repeatedly tuned final acceptance benchmark.

### Actual-route diagnosis and rhotic stem repair (2026-10-03)

The current goal is English-subset rules-only strict ≥70% and lenient ≥90%; these are not runtime CMUdict scores. On the same 6990 words, a per-word oracle over every usable primary-stress slot on the actual own-word renderer gives at most +201 strict / +241 lenient (not a deployable rule or a target score). Actual return-point instrumentation finds 1528 outer-word morphology routes. Choosing the unsplit alternative independently for each of those words gives +108 strict / +63 lenient; blanket handler bypasses are generally negative. A five-fold, family-grouped stress experiment (existing spellings/slot features, first/second/last/penult/antepenult actions, TRAIN joint strict+lenient selection) scores −2 strict / +1 lenient out of fold and is rejected. Stress alone does not close either gap. These are diagnosis experiments, not adopted runtime word-choice tables.

One shared eligibility inconsistency is adoptable: the lexical dropped-e probe for -ed/-ing used `ENDS_IN_VOWEL_RE` directly and rejected a genuine rhotic /ɝ/ stem (desire, injure, acquire), while the later rule-derived probe already exempted it with `isUsableConsonantStem`. Both now use that existing helper, retaining its syllable guard and pronounced-e loanword rejection. No added pronunciation data, POS-default lookup or per-word runtime code. Rules-only full-dict alphabetic diff: 42 changed, strict +32/−6, lenient +18/−1; the dict top-5000 slice is +2/−0 strict, +1/−0 lenient. Losses include attired/hiring/tiring/wiring's rhotic notation and the table's fracture verb reading. The rebuilt shipped path is byte-identical for all 100806 alphabetic words, so these losses are confined to the diagnostic rules-only route.

After rebuilding from the same sources: full rules strict 59145 → 59171/105067, lenient 81671 → 81688; English subset strict 4587 → 4595/6990 (65.62 → 65.74%), lenient 5914 → 5917 (84.61 → 84.65%); top-5000 subset strict 2453 → 2455/3617, lenient 3100 → 3101. Independent CMUdict runtime stays 4733/5000 (94.66%), rules rises 3613 → 3614 (72.26 → 72.28%), desired alone, no regressions. Parity and homograph eval/train stay unchanged. evaluate-strict phonemic 52142 → 52168/90310 (57.74 → 57.77%), meanED 1.2358 → 1.2352. Full tests 1147/1147 before adding 12 rhotic/loanword regression checks; final inflection suite 122/122 and typecheck pass. Common and stress-aware baselines updated; historical full-dict target retained.

Other batch proposals rejected or deferred: a generic lexical un- decomposition is +92 strict / +97 lenient on the full dict but falsely parses unanimous and its relatives; unrestricted dropped-e recovery in the shared stem callback is −51/−35; dropping all short table stems is −26/+5. A short -ist bypass and blanket bound-consonant coalescence have small positive totals but damage valid derivatives or legitimate boundary lengthening, so they are not adopted. Evidence/scripts/dumps in `/tmp/phonemize-goal-70-90/` retain the actual-route counterfactuals and complete changed-word lists. Next passes should improve stem-boundary evidence and shared composition, using family-held-out candidates followed by all rebuild/runtime gates; repeatedly retuning these experimental folds is not independent acceptance.

### Accumulated working changes and commit threshold (2026-10-03)

User instruction: each subsequent commit needs ≥1% improvement; use the conservative interpretation of **≥1 percentage point on English-subset strict accuracy**, with the other adoption gates nondecreasing. The last committed starting point is `8b36cba`: 4595/6990 strict (65.7368%), 5917/6990 lenient (84.6495%). With unchanged n, a commit needs at least 4665 strict-correct words (+70). Keep validated smaller improvements in the working tree and accumulate them; do not make a commit for each small class, and do not update persisted score baselines before the accumulated improvement qualifies. The original 70% strict / 90% lenient goal remains unchanged.

Current uncommitted change: one privative-prefix branch before suffix dispatch, sharing the existing lexical lookup and compound-tail attestation. It composes un- before a consonant, or non- before any root, with an independently known root of ≥4 letters, keeping the root's original reading/stress. It leaves vowel-initial un- ambiguity and the longer under- prefix alone. A wholesale prefix policy was measured in a batch and rejected: re- and pre- lower the English subset; un- before vowels falsely parses unanimous; forcing explicit V on un- loses unread's participial reading. No new table/dataset, per-word code or disableDict-only route was added. No new suffix handler, rule-table rows or compression trigger.

Rebuilt isolated final candidate: English subset 4600 strict (65.8083%, +5) / 5921 lenient (84.7067%, +4); top-5000 subset 2457 strict / 3103 lenient. Full rules 59272 strict / 81761 lenient over 105067. Alphabetic rule diff 272 changed, strict +149/−24, lenient +107/−10; dict top-5000 +2/−0 on both. CMUdict runtime stays 4733/5000, rules 3614 → 3616 (+2/−0: unknown, unless). Runtime strict parity improves by one word (nonprescription), lenient flat; the 19 changed outputs include secondary-mark differences and two affrication variants, not a blanket output-identity claim. Homograph eval unchanged 1415/1615; stress-aware phonemic headline 52168 → 52308/90310, meanED 1.2352 → 1.2304. Full existing tests 1159/1159; 18 new prefix/root-boundary tests pass. These are working-tree scores, not new committed baselines. Current batch is below the commit threshold and remains uncommitted. Evidence and ablations in `/tmp/phonemize-prefix-batch/`.

A separate batch intervention changing -ist to /əst/ or -ism to /əzəm/ is rejected: full rules strict −93 / −134 respectively, with English-subset strict 0 / −5 and lenient −2 / 0. Evidence in `/tmp/phonemize-morph-batch/`. Do not use unstressed-vowel variants to inflate scores without measuring the full class. Extending pre-suffix i-reduction to -ical is also rejected: full rules −62 strict / −18 lenient, English subset −17 / −1; the prefix subset of known stress-neutral frames cannot be generalized to all -ical endings.

Further uncommitted boundary batch (2026-10-03): the existing -al loop now leaves final -eal/-oal vowel sequences intact, and the shared stress-neutral suffix callback receives its suffix/IPA so an unknown non-qu, u-final stem before -ment is rendered with its suffix in view, retaining the stem stress and accepting only a regular suffix match. Lexical stems keep their readings. Blanket contextual rendering was rejected (full rules −195 strict / −156 lenient; common −17 / −9); silent-e stems such as achievement/basement must remain free-stem compositions. The narrower combined candidate was rebuilt before acceptance. No new table, rule-table rows or parallel suffix handler; block-comment-aware code counts: g2p 834, five-module total 2675 (below 1150/2809). Common clustering exposes existing e/o rule overlaps; inspect these before a qualifying commit's compression review, and honor the user's latest minimum-improvement rule for every commit.

CMUdict runtime unchanged 4733/5000; rules 3616 → 3619 (+3/−0: appeal, argument, instrument). Homograph eval unchanged 1415/1615; stress-aware phonemic headline 52308 → 52321/90310. The shared evaluator now honors `--subset common --cluster`, labels its rule attribution approximate (pre-phonotactics traces exclude morphology/compound routes), normalizes STRUT and dark-l only for this diagnostic, and returns non-zero on missing cache/error. Scoring is unchanged. Smoke checks confirm both the unchanged scores/cluster output and exit 1 for a deliberately missing common cache. No standalone tooling commit under the 1-point threshold.

Mixed fold results and zero legacy-filter passes mean none of these drafts is adopted into runtime. No HELD metric ranks or stops candidate selection. Treat these already-inspected folds as development evidence, not an independent final gate for future tuning. Candidate families (e.g. rhotic -ire, unstressed medial vowels) still need semantic rule review and full-corpus/runtime/CMU validation; do not deploy a raw learned mapping or count this research gain as current score improvement. ### Weak-vowel batch and terminal-rhotic boundary (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The next batch actually replayed three frequent offline candidate families over all 105067 testable dict words: -ire /ɹ/→/ɝ/ (+6 strict common but full −22 strict / −1 lenient), weak i after soft c (+10 common strict but full −19 / −9), and weak e after m/soft g (full +48 / +2, common +12 / +3). The first two are rejected; a posttonic restriction on soft-c i still loses full −7 / −1. Blanket weak-e centralization is worse (full −529 / −158; common −79 / −11); medial-only loses full strict −27. These are complete counterfactuals, not adopted word-specific exceptions. Evidence in `/tmp/phonemize-vowel-batch/`.

Accepted class: inside the existing unstressed/non-secondary reduction loop, a literal e whose current vowel is /ɪ/ after m or soft-g /dʒ/ centralizes to /ə/. It reaches initial weak me-/ge- too, but preserves stressed/full vowels and be-/de-. The same batch fixes one composition inconsistency: POST_PROC_RULES' terminal /ɹɪtʃ/ hardening now recognizes coalesced /ɝ/ and requires an ordinary /ɹ/ to have left context, preserving the bare English root's affricate. Narrowing it to vowel+rich spellings was rejected (full −11 / −12); the phonetic boundary/rhotic extension is +5 strict / +5 lenient with no losses before the weak-e join, and prevents emergent emerich/emmerich/helmerich/hemmerich corruptions caused by rhotic coalescence. No whole-word IPA table, new dataset, rule-table row or parallel handler. Final reduction implementation reuses the existing loop; 1296352 predictions over dict/rules/novel derived forms/POS contexts are byte-identical before and after sharing it. Code count g2p 834 / all five modules 2679, still below the committed 2809 growth threshold.

Full rules 59349 strict / 81791 lenient over 105067 (+56 / +10 from the preceding tree); alphabetic rule diff 434 changed, strict +73/−25, lenient +40/−31; top-5000 +6/−0 strict and +1/−0 lenient. Baselines remain untouched; no commit made.

An additional paired stress/vowel counterfactual on the pre-batch snapshot confirms a coupling bottleneck: pro over a lax root given initial stress alone is common strict 0 / lenient −6; changing its stressed vowel alone has no common gain; jointly forcing initial stress and /ɑ/ is common +4 / +3, full +13 / +7. Still deferred because it breaks the correct proposal/protect/provincial class. Some of those effects occur inside fabricated stems, and the public trace's apparent own-word syllables do not reliably identify the prediction route (profit is an example). Future candidates must observe actual morphology/compound dispatch and stem context rather than adopt a pro-wide lexical-stress shortcut. Already-inspected CV folds remain development evidence, never final acceptance for retuning.

### Stress-bearing -ese/-eer with weak ne (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

Current-path single-edit grouping still ranks weak-vowel mismatches first. ### Open vowels and prefix/plural boundaries (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

Accepted batch keeps the existing rule/composition paths: two orthographic slots ending in a silent-e slot cannot put stress on that consonant; -cient/-ciency stress the preceding nucleus, -ciency palatalizes to /ʃənsi/, and weak assimilated ef- raises like other weak e. Open e tenses before closed qu- tails and dental + rhotic endings; the existing a frames extend to -con, stressed -asic and two-real-syllable -ative. The m/pr + o frame before final -ve/-ven/-vie takes GOOSE, including p split across the syllable boundary. The existing -ange lookahead now actually includes the plural's raw ə+s/z, matching its already-stated intent. dis- shares the privative-prefix block and prefers an explicit V root; pre- with an attested root is tried after outer suffixes so preference is not split into pre + ference. No dataset, whole-word IPA mapping, scoring change or parallel suffix handler.

Whole-corpus ablations and sources are in `/tmp/phonemize-tense-batch/`. Rejected: broad e before qu (−5 strict), broad e before -er (−3 strict), e before -Co (−7 strict), weak e in all doubled initial codas (−43 strict/−29 lenient), and weakening en/em (−9/−6 for en). The narrowed qu-tail rule preserves equity/cheque and final bare -qua; the dental -er/-ar frames preserve ever/never/clever. -cient stress alone costs one full-dict lenient word (coefficient); palatalization + weak ef- as a batch is +7 strict/+3 lenient. The -con probe's first overly broad prototype is superseded by a-con-final.log and the narrow A_TENSE_ENDINGS integration; narrow result +2/+2 full, +1/+1 common. Individual a-sic, a-tive, dental e and -con frames have limited dictionary support and stay part of this measured batch, never evidence for broad vowel tensing.

The accepted fallback is narrowed to e/i/o/u before -vie, preserving known y stems and the separate aCy lengthening frame. This recovery is prediction-identical on all 105067 testable dict words, but restores movies in the independent public CMUdict set. ### Medial-y boundaries and rhotic a- roots (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The shared syllabifier now keeps a genuine medial y nucleus immediately before -ic or a silent-e coda (acrylic, analytic/catalytic, analyze/catalyze, enzyme, genotype). It extends the existing two-syllable prefix guard rather than adding a pronunciation table or a parallel handler. -ity/-ysis and ordinary final-y words retain their established merged slots. Bare a- also gives stress to a closed rhotic root (alarm, alert, apart); assimilated prefixes retain their previous test, since extending that rhotic signal to them misreads compounds such as allergist.

Whole-dict counterfactuals: unrestricted medial-y preservation is rejected (strict +64/-33 but lenient +61/-81, English subset +4/-1 strict and +3/-4 lenient). Keeping y anywhere before -ic/-ics also misreads polyphonic/metaphysics; the adopted condition requires y immediately before the final suffix slot. The y boundary alone measures full strict +51/-1 and lenient +33/-5, English subset +3/+1 net. Broad a- plus a consonant-rich coda loses agent/analyst/aspect and full lenient falls; unconditional ad- and a global closed-rhotic extension are likewise rejected. The bare-a closed-rhotic condition alone is full strict +11/-6, lenient unchanged, English subset +3/-0 strict. Remaining whole-dict losses are proper names/compounds with lexical stress, protected by the runtime lexicon. Evidence: /tmp/phonemize-y-boundary-batch/.

### Inflection root collisions (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

Existing morphology handlers now give an attested y stem precedence over a coincidental -ie headword before -ies (carry/Carrie), permit consonant-only Cy roots for -ied/-ies (shied/spies), and keep a pronounced-e -sse loan from intercepting a regular -ss plural (passes). The -er doubled-base branch also preserves literal monosyllabic ll/ss roots (small/less) instead of stripping their actual final consonant; longer and other doubled stems retain the previous restoration. Preferring every attested doubled -er root loses reveller/jewellers; the adopted literal-ll/ss condition is restricted to a single written nucleus. Evidence: /tmp/phonemize-attested-stem-batch/.

### Bound -ment roots and singular endings (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The existing stress-neutral callback now retains whole-word context for an assimilated-prefix + bound -ple root before -ment, reusing KEEP_UNMERGED_AFTER_PREFIX, and for a -le remnant with no independent consonant-vowel-consonant nucleus (consonants only, or a lone vowel, before le). These restore the medial onset/weak vowel in element/implement/complement/supplement, while free silent-le stems keep their citation reading. The plural handler also leaves Greek -asis/-esis/-osis/-ysis nouns intact, and -al no longer restores an unrelated soft-c -ice headword for an -ic stem. These modify existing shared handlers, without word-to-IPA maps, new datasets, rule-table rows or a parallel suffix handler.

Rejected counterfactuals on the current 105067-word policy: blanket final -ic vowel reduction is full strict -61 / lenient -21 and common -17/-2; preserving /ɪ/ is majority usage here. Rendering every unknown -ment stem contextually is full -31/-14 and common -10/-3; final-e scope is -26/-15 and common -10/-4, losing achievement/basement/statement. Literal final-m scope adds one lenient word but is deferred; consonant-final-l scope changes nothing. Broad -le scope can score net-positive but misreads battlement/encirclement/entanglement/entitlement, so it is rejected in favor of the bound-root/frame test. Skipping every final-is plural also misreads real antis/nazis and naturalized loan plurals, despite positive full totals; the adopted Greek suffix condition preserves those. -ical hard-c repair is partial for helical: its false soft consonant is fixed, but the bare stem still picks a tense initial e rather than the lexical lax vowel. It remains non-exact and is not counted as a strict gain. Evidence: /tmp/phonemize-next-class/.

The final rebuild restores implementation. New tests exercise owned segments and boundaries; two preliminary assertions assumed skis was already correct and taxis necessarily meant taxi's plural. ### Greek th+e scope and attested loan plurals (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The existing voiced-th/article guards now recognize initial Greek thea-/theo- and ther+vowel/therm frames. ### Source-aligned pre-l rounding (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The shared syllable renderer now rounds an a before l+d/s/t dental frames (salt, bald, false, alter) and onsetless al+w (always). It keys on the original a in a single-nucleus slot, so a merged slot such as royalty cannot acquire a spurious rounded second vowel. Onsetless alt+i, alt+o/r and consonant-onset al+w retain their learned/prefix readings (altitude/alto/altruism/malware); the existing GERMANIC_NAME_ENDING guard protects native name vowels. There are no new mapping tables, datasets, suffix handlers or rule-table entries.

The earlier raw postlex replacement is rejected: it corrupts royalty and cannot distinguish the source nucleus. Broad dental rounding loses alto/altitude/altruism; merely restricting to a closed coda still loses maltreated. Adding al+s/w to the narrow coda-only variant lowers full lenient by two and is rejected. The aligned final condition is positive on both full scores, with no English-subset losses. Remaining rule-only losses are chiefly surnames plus lexical exceptions/variants such as shalt/caldera; rebuilt runtime preserves their supplied segments. Baldric's rounded native reading is independently attested by [Merriam-Webster](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/baldric), although dict.json selects the lax variant; the reference was not changed to inflate the score. The first candidate failed the existing aldinger native-vowel test; reusing the existing Germanic ending guard fixes it. A newly written test initially assumed the supplied lexical aldinger vowel must match the rule vowel, but the untouched workspace already has lexical /ɔ/ versus rule /æ/. The new assertion now preserves both established readings. No existing test expectations changed.

### Resyllabified ion and ea rimes (2026-10-03, cumulative batch)

The existing SUFFIX_RULES loop now recognizes unstressed tio/sio/stio when a vowel-initial derivative moves the n onto the next onset (practitioner/missionary/visionary). It consumes the same suffix-table reading without its final n, leaving that consonant for the next slot; nasal+sion remains voiceless, while stressed io retains its hiatus (cationic). The existing open-a branch extends to primary-stressed -tioner/-tionary roots, and -ionery retains the strong final rime shared with -ionary (stationery). The existing lax-ea branch admits voiced-stop(+r) onsets before f/th (deaf/death/breath); a stop+r ea rime before k/t lengthens (break/great), including opened derivational boundaries. Heath/sheath/wreath and freak/creak/bleak retain their established vowels. There are no new word-to-IPA tables, datasets, rule-table rows or morphology handlers.

Rebuilding exposed a genuine eviction interaction: once break became rule-exact, -ing fabricated breake, re-split its final k and selected /i/. The new breaking test and independent CMU word diff both caught it, although mentioned's simultaneous repair concealed the loss in the aggregate runtime score. Fresh return-point instrumentation reproduces the live morphology output before diagnosis; declining outer morphology has an oracle ceiling of +94 strict/+45 lenient words on the current 6990-word subset. Initial labial+ear keeps /ɛɹ/ in bear/pear and their derivatives, with beard/pearl/appear/fear/spear preserving their contrasting frames; the shared dropped-e paths exclude -ear so an unrelated beare lookup cannot corrupt bearing/bearer.

### Weak hiatus, labial rimes and qualifying checkpoint (2026-10-03)

The existing i-hiatus renderer retains a weak second nucleus in ia before l/s/nt and the rhotic tail (dial/bias/giant/liar/diary), preserving full vowels in triad/fiat and original doubled codas. ### Checked o/r roots and suffix-base recovery (2026-10-03, pending cumulative batch)

The existing open-o skip now keeps the checked vowel before -del/-vel/-dern, sp+weak vowel and -stel, preserving hotel/motel/poster/host. It applies to the stressed root at any position: modern's eviction exposed premodern/remodel reverting to long o, fixed by removing the initial-position restriction rather than adding prefix exceptions. Existing STRUT handling covers c/h+o before -ver and h/sh before -vel; k/l+ost takes a checked vowel while host/post/most stay long. The existing doubled-r skip handles initial stressed er at longer lengths and single-onset ir (terrace/terrible/mirror), preserving NURSE in stirrup/squirrel and real inter-/over-/under- boundaries. ### Productive noun roots and stressed hiatus (2026-10-03, pending cumulative batch)

The -ability/-ibility noun establishes its independently supplied or rule-derived -able/-ible adjective frame, retains the root, demotes its primary and places the noun primary on -bil-. A supplied noun compound precedes a fabricated adjective spelling (in + stability), preserving the earlier exact instability. Its weak-suffix matcher excludes a diphthong offglide, so able/stable/disability are not falsely read as weak -able. This composes liability/reliability, capability, flexibility/possibility and their longer families without reading the target's own entry only under disableDict. -uality's primary sits on the second nucleus of the merged ua slot: the earlier u and palatalized t/d/s/ks survive even when that slot is stressed, and the secondary-beat calculation counts that extra nucleus. The existing hiatus classifier excludes consonantal qu (equality/quality). No code lexical mapping, dataset or stress-neutral-table row is added.

### Greek noun frames and Latin weak syllables (2026-10-04, accumulating)

Continue the same class-based loop without agents, new data or whole-word mappings. An open combining syllable keeps final -gram/-log full; -logue folds silent ue before rule syllable counting, leaving bare Logue intact. A noun dia- head keeps PRICE plus weak thematic a, with primary on the first nucleus; diagonal/diameter retain their existing hiatus stress. Terminal -gm loses g only after a vowel-spelled segment, with PRICE in -igm; vowel-initial derivatives keep g. The existing Greek -y noun/agent join also handles -logize, protecting apology/apologize and eulogy/eulogize. The shared neutral-suffix callback reads the independent -men noun frame for palatal -ci/-gi + -ment. Weak ad-/com-/de- + -ance/-ence root stress excludes hiatus; com- + mand/mend keeps root stress including plural s.

Adding PHR/PHL onsets improved diaphragm but corrupted epinephrine/aphrodisiac stress or vowel quality; attempted -iac compensation lost paranoiac, so neither ships. Diaphragm's head stress remains open, with its previous runtime output unchanged. No claim that every partially repaired word is now fully correct.

### Weak fricative rimes and prefix boundaries (2026-10-04, accumulating)

Continue class-based improvement without agents or new data. The shared posttonic callback centralizes weak fricative/soft-g -il after the primary, except directly after a bound prefix (con+cil remains outside the noun rime). ### Neutral suffix and particle boundaries (2026-10-04, accumulating)

Add -wise/-hood to the existing shared stress-neutral table, with the caller restoring adjective y before -hood (likelihood/livelihood). Keep nasal-dental -ngth and an unattested 3+-slot prefix+root before -wise in whole-word context; the first rebuild exposed anticlockwise/counterclockwise losing their root stress. Compound dispatch can use an already vouched silent-e HEAD plus a verb particle from the existing HEAD table when the particle has no TAIL entry; require a true vowel+C+e frame, two slots, and an ordinary particle. Both runtime modes share the same policy. The plural handler reuses a verified compound only for a particle-ending stem, preserving takeoffs/takeouts/tradeoffs/timeouts after their singulars are evicted. Unrestricted alternative compound tails corrupt pageant/comedic/lacerate; unrestricted plural compound reuse changes outdoes/overdoes and loses frogmouths/sociopaths, so adopt neither broad proposal. All rejected sources/reviews remain separate.

The existing soft-g guard retains a velar stop before the -ng + -est superlative, excluding bound in-/con- and soft -nge stems. Greek -archy/-archic endings take /k/ when an earlier vowel licenses the combining root; native starchy/research/archer stay unchanged. Greek hier- before arch-/glyph- retains PRICE. Hieroglyph/hieroglyphic, anarchy, adulthood, longest/youngest and other partial repairs remain open outside the segment owned here; don't claim their whole IPA is now correct. Pronunciation QA is external only, e.g. Dictionary.com's hierarchical entry attests PRICE and /k/; no source data is imported into runtime.

### Reusable actual-dispatch comparison (2026-10-04, accumulating tooling)

`yarn diagnose:routes` (`scripts/diagnose-routes.ts`) compares the existing outer-word morphology decision with the pipeline's fallback, and separately with the unsplit route. It observes actual source return points in an isolated compiled constructor; nested stem-helper returns are excluded. Per-instance bypasses affect only the current outer word and are restored in finally. Every original prediction must equal the unmodified public predict() output before any alternative is scored. Source/table/frequency/tool hashes accompany the report, and every selected word, including already correct words, contributes wins and losses. Default scope is the complete English subset; `--all`, `--include-long`, `--limit N` and `--out DIR` expose bounded/full diagnosis. Use `node --require tsx/cjs scripts/diagnose-routes.ts ...` if tsx CLI IPC is restricted. This diagnoses route decisions; it does not train/adopt a policy, consult gold at runtime, expose a production hook or update baselines.

Strict tool typecheck, help/invalid-limit checks, bounded long-word smoke and both complete scopes pass. With current sources/tables frozen: **6990** original outputs verified, baseline **5031 strict / 6198 lenient**; blanket no-morphology **4464 / 5750**, unsplit **4360 / 5688**. Full including long words: **107645** original outputs verified, baseline **62190 / 84698**, no-morphology **56796 / 79905**, unsplit **53834 / 78306**. Do not ship either blanket alternative. Full reports, exact branch returns and all changed strings: `/tmp/phonemize-morphology-route-lab/{common,full}/report.json`; every recorded source/table hash was rechecked against the unchanged main checkout.

The highest common branch-level proposal, excluding all two-letter o roots from the productive -os fallback, scores frozen full strict **+5/-0**, lenient **+9/-0**, common **+2/+3** and passes rebuilt corpus gates. It is nevertheless **rejected**: independent OOV controls expose nos/gos/los/jos/io plurals losing their valid open-root vowel. Keep its source/build/dumps under `/tmp/phonemize-short-o-dispatch/`, outside main; those candidate scores are not the project's current score. ### Strong -ier and merged -io nuclei checkpoint (2026-10-04, pending cumulative batch)

Fresh route diagnosis (6990 English-subset words) confirms that bypassing all morphology or all compound analysis is a large net loss. Measure narrow spelling/nucleus changes over frozen tables instead. A bare stop-onset -ier retains NEAR (bier/pier/tier), while crier/drier/flier/trier/plier retain their PRICE agent stems; stressed -ier uses NEAR, weak carrier/happier retain i+NURSE. [Merriam-Webster tier](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/tier) attests both layer-tier and the PRICE tying-agent sense: this rule supplies the default layer reading only, and does not solve the two noun senses. A merged initial io slot before a light l-tail retains PRICE in its first nucleus and weakens the second (violent/violence/violet/violin), rather than emitting two strong vowels. Bound -iol + -ence stays in the whole suffix context instead of borrowing the independent musical viol root. [Violent](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/violent) and [violin](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/violin) independently support the weak second nucleus. Violin's rule primary remains an open miss; its supplied final primary is retained by the miner's stress-mismatch gate, so segment-only strict gains do not imply a complete rule pronunciation repair. Premier retains its pre-existing prefix-vowel gap against dict.json (CMU independently accepts the /pri/ variant).

Frozen whole-dict including long words (107645): **13 changed strings, strict +6/-1, lenient +11/-0, common +3/+5**. The strict loss is Revier's supplied name reading; runtime preserves it. The initial rebuild exposes rule-exact violent/violence leaving the table, with nonviolent/nonviolence losing their root PRICE and primary in disableDict. Extend the existing transparent-prefix fallback to the bounded iol + ent/ence shape, shared by both modes, and rebuild again. Rebuilt alphabetic dump (100806): **12 changes, strict +6/-1, lenient +10/-0**; read every row. Runtime has **zero changed strings**. Review all **104** native roots, inflections, transparent prefixes and controls in both modes after rebuilding: the PRICE agents, weak -ier and the independent viol/violate/lion frames retain their prior behavior, including pre-existing rule gaps in the latter two. New tests pin only the owned nucleus/suffix, rather than falsely asserting those unrelated gaps are solved. Reject broad weak -age (full strict/lenient losses), generic final-io (full strict/common lenient losses), soft-rge agent restoration (enlarger regression), and broad open-a + -el/-our (camel/chapel/panel/travel and full-dict losses); rejected sources/diffs remain isolated.

### Bound -ite and free finite checkpoint (2026-10-04, pending cumulative batch)

Bound -posite/-quisite/de-/in-finite retain a weak final nucleus despite silent e; de-/in-finite and -quisite retract primary before two weak nuclei. A strong open o before bound -site keeps LOT. Perquisite's weak final ə/ɪ and inquisitive's thematic ə/ɪ likewise remain variant-sensitive. Acquisitive's weak initial vowel, coinfinite's merged co+i nucleus, and transfinite/cofinite's first root vowel remain open; tests pin only the owned rime/stress. ### Silent h, schedul/echo roots and short -ancy checkpoint (2026-10-04, pending cumulative batch)

A replay-verified post-lexical ablation over 107645 dictionary words measured 165 active rules through the real pipeline, including recursively rendered stems. The reusable `yarn diagnose:postlex` command defaults to the 6990-word English subset; `--all --include-long` reproduces the full-dict experiment. Its isolated constructor leaves runtime source/data untouched, checks every instrumented baseline against ordinary predict(), and replays removals only for words whose original execution changed at that rule. Already-correct words contribute losses too. Per-rule changed predictions, score deltas, metadata and source hashes go to the requested output folder. The CLI reproduced every original full-experiment common delta; default replay saw 80 active rules. Broad removal of weak -et, /ətʃ/→/ək/ or hard -ger was rejected: common gains conceal full-dict losses. Short C+ancy roots are a separable strong-vowel class, so only that frame now bypasses weak -ancy reduction (fancy/chancy and structurally identical names), retaining longer buoyancy/truancy/vacancy.

Honor-/honour-/honest- retain silent h and initial LOT; schedul- takes the US /sk/ onset in the existing sch branch; echo- retains its own Greek /k/. Echo's ch rule is scoped to the root's own onset, preserving subsequent native ch in echochamber/echochime/echochild. Independent support: [honor](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/honor), [honest](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/honest), [schedule](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/schedule), [echo](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/echo). Schedule's Canadian/British sh variant is attested; this is the existing en-US path. Honour's terminal OUR gap and longer echo words' vowel/stress gaps remain open; tests pin the owned onset/nucleus. No word-to-IPA map, target lookup, dataset/model, new PHONEME_RULES/SUFFIX_RULES row or parallel suffix handler.

Initial re-mining broke dishonor/dishonest, un-/non-honest/-honorable, honorifics, honorarily and non-/un-fancy by evicting their correct roots. Shared SILENT_HON_ROOT/SHORT_ANCY_ROOT recover the existing prefix, plural and -ily y-restoration branches. Full word-diff additionally caught francies choosing an unrelated -ie head after francy eviction; the existing plural guard now prefers the supported -y frame. Its supplied weak-vowel name reading stays valid, so the new test owns /s/ at the join rather than imposing TRAP on that lexical reading. Complete rebuild, then review all 193 native/OOV controls in both modes: former valid joins restored; extra echo derivatives gain the owned /k/. Honorary/honorarily lose only secondary marks. A synthetic echoch still has the pre-existing post-lexical final-ch corruption; do not claim that unrelated rule fixed. ### Greek y, closed yr and French -ique checkpoint (2026-10-04, pending cumulative batch)

The existing syn- lax-y branch now covers consonant-bound dys- and phys-, with centralization only on weak phys-. The existing onset-s branch voices the phys- root; dys- remains unvoiced. A closed yr before a dental/nasal coda takes NURSE; the phoneme loop preserves open yr, geminate-r boundaries and y+r+silent-e (lyric/tyranny/pyrrhic vs lyre/tyre/pyre). French final -ique has FLEECE before the existing silent-que /k/. Independent pronunciation support: [physics](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/physics), [physician](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/physician), [dyslexia](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/dyslexia), [myrtle](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/myrtle), [antique](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/antique). Clique's KIT/FLEECE and oblique's FLEECE/PRICE variants are both attested ([clique](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/clique), [oblique](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/oblique)); retain lexical readings rather than correcting a valid alternative to a reference string. Physiology/physiological weak vowels, dyslexia's open-e nucleus, communique's accent-final -que, and longer compounds' unrelated nuclei remain separate gaps. No word-to-IPA map, target lookup or dataset/model; one PHONEME_RULES row, no new suffix row or parallel suffix handler.

Reject the broad yr version (full frozen strict -3, lenient -7): it eats PRICE in lyre/tyre and checked vowels in names. Restrict its coda and preserve open/geminate/silent-e positions. Reject dys+vowel laxing: Dyson/Dysan retain PRICE; the adopted consonant-bound dys- frame is separate. Re-mining exposed valid physicians/antiques/techniques/myrtles and myrrh/Byrd inflections relying on evicted roots. Shared BORROWED_ROOT_FRAME restores the existing privative, pre-, plural, inflection, agent and neutral-wise routes. Vowel-final yr fragments cannot masquerade as plural bases (myrtis is not myrti+s). The existing DP may recover a final supported root only after an already-known genuine prefix head; arbitrary pos+tpique is rejected. Greek -ic/-ical adjectives keep whole-word suffix stress, preventing semiphysical/multiphysical from acquiring noun-compound head stress. Review all 268 real/synthetic roots, neighbours, prefixes, inflections and OOV controls in both modes after complete builds. Formerly valid tested joins and pre- nuclei are restored; synthetic merged semi-/multi-/meta- + dys- still takes the earlier weak-y /i/ path (versus the root's /ɪ/), an open OOV quality issue, not claimed fixed. Native -ic prefix probes take the suffix-stressed whole-word frame rather than the old lexical compound-head frame. Tests pin the owned segments/stress and guard postpique against a false fragment.

### 2026-10-04 — batch weak Latin -uum/-tia and Greek -oic

Extend existing weak u+a/o hiatus to u+u (continuum/vacuum), and existing weak final -cia/-sia palatalization to -tia (militia/scotia/consortia/inertia). Use whole-class frozen probes, a complete build, and the actual derived/native/runtime word diff before adoption. ## Rule Compression

Rules grow by accumulation. Compression folds patches back into simpler, more general forms. Run a compression pass whenever a trigger fires.

The English rule engine spans three modules (refactored 2026-06, snapshot-verified byte-identical), plus two more added 2026-09-30 for the stress-neutral suffix family (see the bound-morph table note below):

- `src/en/g2p.ts` — dictionary/morphology/compound dispatch, `tryMorphologicalAnalysis`, `tryCompoundSplit`
- `src/en/syllabify.ts` — syllabification, stress, `PHONEME_RULES`/`SUFFIX_RULES` (first match wins; order is load-bearing)
- `src/en/postlex.ts` — rule-path-only post-lexical correction tables. NOT mergeable into `src/en/phonotactics.ts`, which also applies to dict output.
- `src/en/morph-table.ts` — `STRESS_NEUTRAL_ROWS`, a declarative `[suffix, ipa]` table: `PHONEME_RULES`-shaped rule data, just not living inside `syllabify.ts`.
- `src/en/morph-parser.ts` — `tryStressNeutralSuffix`, the one composition function that applies every row in the table above, plus the `softenBaseFinal`/`preSuffixReduce` primitives it (and `g2p.ts`'s surviving `-al`/`-ular` loop) both call.

### Mined gram tables (2026-06)

`build-dict` also runs three miners that learn statistics from `data/en/dict.json` and emit runtime data (gitignored, regenerated each build):

- `scripts/mine-compound-parts.ts` → `compound-parts.json` — verified compound head/tail tables (both halves must verify; that requirement is the load-bearing filter)
- `scripts/mine-stress-grams.ts` → `stress-grams.json` — ending-gram × syllable-count → primary/secondary stress position from end
- `scripts/mine-vowel-grams.ts` → `vowel-grams.json` — ending/initial-gram → stressed/final/initial vowel + final coda

Adoption test everywhere: support ≥5, modal value ≥70%, net-fixes ≥3 vs the gram-free pipeline. Miners RESET their own table before importing the pipeline (re-mining against a live table un-adopts its own grams). Keys that depend on syllable count use the RUNTIME-visible count, not the dict's. New positions should follow the same recipe; 5-letter grams measured net-negative (don't re-add).

For provably score-neutral refactors, gate with `tsx scripts/snapshot-dump.ts` before/after: an empty diff over its 1.3M predictions freezes both eval scores by construction.

### Triggers

| Trigger | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Line count** | `src/en/g2p.ts` exceeds 1150 lines, or the rule modules — `src/en/g2p.ts` + `src/en/syllabify.ts` + `src/en/postlex.ts`, plus `src/en/morph-table.ts` + `src/en/morph-parser.ts` (added 2026-09-30; the table is `PHONEME_RULES`-shaped rule data moved out of `g2p.ts`'s inline suffix loop, and the parser is the composition logic that loop used to carry inline — excluding either from the count would let a future refactor dodge this trigger by relocating rule code into a new file instead of actually shrinking it) — together exceed the code-line count recorded after the most recent compression pass by more than 150 lines **of code** — comment and blank lines don't count. Last compression: **3245** code lines, five modules (2026-10-04, tenth pass; g2p 1010, syllabify 1702, postlex 484, morph-table 23, morph-parser 26) → next trigger above **3395**. The snapshot-identical consolidation and separately reviewable patch are documented above; score-neutral compression remains in the accumulating feature batch under the user's minimum +1-point commit rule. |
| **Session growth** | A single session adds ≥ 3 entries to `PHONEME_RULES` or `SUFFIX_RULES` |
| **Cluster overlap** | `yarn test:eval --cluster` shows the same grapheme appearing as top-hit across ≥ 2 different clusters |
| **Parallel handlers** | `tryMorphologicalAnalysis` gains a new suffix handler that shares base-lookup logic with an existing one |

The line-count trigger moved from a fixed ceiling to this relative threshold (set 2026-09-28) because five compression passes had driven the fixed ceiling down to 2599/2600 code lines, and what remains at that size is mostly the per-rule measured-evidence comments this loop requires and the core phonology the "What not to compress" list protects — so any single new rule re-tripped the fixed ceiling regardless of how compact the surrounding code already was; a threshold relative to the count recorded after the last pass tracks accumulated growth instead. Each future compression note must update the recorded baseline in this row.

"Lines of code" in the line-count trigger is counted **block-comment-aware**: a line inside a `/* … */` or `/** … */` block (including the block's own start/end lines) doesn't count, the same as a `//` line. Two integrators reported different counts from the same checkout (2,713 naive vs 2,557 block-aware) because a naive count treats every physical line as code unless it starts with `//`, which overcounts every multi-line `/** … */` doc comment above a rule. Use the block-aware count when checking the trigger.

### Procedure

1. **Identify candidates** — scan `PHONEME_RULES` and `SUFFIX_RULES` for:
   - Adjacent entries producing the same IPA whose regexes differ only in one character or anchor → merge with character-class alternation
   - Entries made redundant by a more-specific rule above them (dead rules) → delete
   - Two morphological handlers that strip different suffixes then perform identical base-lookup + allomorph logic → unify into one handler with a suffix table

2. **Propose the consolidated rule** — express it in the simplest regex that covers all the cases, respecting the existing ordering invariant (more-specific before more-general).

3. **Validate** — both gates must pass before committing:
   - `yarn test` — zero regressions
   - `yarn test:eval` — lenient accuracy must not decrease

4. **Commit the compression separately** from any feature work so the diff is reviewable in isolation.

### What not to compress

- Rules that are adjacent in the array but serve different phonological environments where order is load-bearing.
- The `syllableToIPA` silent-e and vowel-reduction logic — already compact; changes there touch core phonology.
- Any rule whose regex has a comment explaining a non-obvious constraint — collapse only after understanding the constraint.

## Notes

- `phonemize/<lang>-g2p` subpath imports give consumers each language processor class as a default export; pair with `useProcessor(new ...)` or `createPhonemizer({ processors: [...] })`.
- The rule-based G2P was partially LLM-generated and the README explicitly warns it may misfire. Prefer `addPronunciation()` (or extending `src-data/`) over rule edits when fixing a single word.
